which we view as a complex orbifold of dimension
n-1
.
If we write
H(\mathbb{A}_{f})=\bigsqcup_{j}H(\mathbb{Q})h_{j}K
as a finite disjoint union, then

(3.1.4)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})=\bigsqcup_{j}(H(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{j})\backslash\mathcal{D},

where
K_{j}=h_{j}Kh_{j}^{-1}
stabilizes the lattice
L_{j}=h_{j}L
.

The Shimura variety carries a family of special divisors

Z(m)\in\mathrm{Div}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D}))

indexed by positive integers
m
, which
we describe in terms of the decomposition (
3.1.4
).
For each
x\in V
with
\langle x,x\rangle>0
, define an analytic divisor

\mathcal{D}(x)=\{z\in\mathcal{D}:x\perp z\}

where
\perp
means orthogonal with respect to the hermitian or bilinear form, depending on whether the reader prefers the model (
3.1.1
) or (
3.1.2
).
The pullback of
Z(m)
via the uniformization

\mathcal{D}\to(H(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{j})\backslash\mathcal{D}\subset\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})

is the locally finite analytic divisor

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in L_{j}\\
\langle x,x\rangle=m\end{subarray}}\mathcal{D}(x)\in\mathrm{Div}(\mathcal{D}).

More generally, given a positive
m\in\mathbb{Q}
and a
K
-fixed function
\varphi\in S_{L}
, there is a special divisor

Z(m,\varphi)\in\mathrm{Div}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D}))

whose pullback via the above uniformization is

\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}\varphi(\mu)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mu_{j}+L_{j}\\
\langle x,x\rangle=m\end{subarray}}\mathcal{D}(x)\in\mathrm{Div}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{D}),

where
\mu_{j}
is the image of
\mu
under
h_{j}:L^{\prime}/L\to L_{j}^{\prime}/L_{j}
.
Taking
\varphi=\varphi_{0}
to be the characteristic function of
0\in L^{\prime}/L
recovers
Z(m)
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be the tautological line bundle over
\mathcal{D}
whose fiber over a point
z\in\mathcal{D}
is the complex line
\mathcal{L}_{z}=z\subset V(\mathbb{R})
.
Denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
the tautological line bundle endowed with the hermitian metric
\|\cdot\|
defined as follows: if we use the interpretation (
3.1.1
) to view
z\in\mathcal{D}
as a line in
V(\mathbb{R})
, then

\|s\|^{2}=-\frac{\langle s,s\rangle}{4\pi e^{\gamma}}

for every
s\in\mathcal{L}_{z}
. If we instead use (
3.1.2
) to view
z
as a line in
\epsilon V(\mathbb{C})
, the metric is the same, but with
\langle-,-\rangle
replaced by
[-,-]
.
The Chern form
\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})
is an
H(\mathbb{R})
-invariant Kähler form on
\mathcal{D}
.
The metrized tautological bundle descends to
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
, and its holomorphic sections are hermitian modular forms of weight
1
. As in the introduction, we abbreviate

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})=\int_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}.

In the sections below we will recall the construction of Green functions for certain linear combinations of special divisors, and compute their integrals with respect to the volume form
\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}
.

Remark 3.1.1
.

Exactly as in the case of orthogonal Shimura varieties
[
Kud03
]
, one can show that

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}\int_{Z(m,\varphi)}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-2}=-\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}\varphi(\mu)B(m,\mu,s_{0}).

On the right hand side the notation is that of §
2.5
.

3.2.
A geometric variant of the Siegel-Weil formula

For
z\in\mathcal{D}
and
x\in V(\mathbb{R})
, let

\varphi_{\infty}(z,x)=\exp\big(-2\pi\langle x_{z^{\perp}},x_{z^{\perp}}\rangle+2\pi\langle x_{z},x_{z}\rangle\big)

be the Gaussian. Here we are using the model (
3.1.1
), so that
z
determines an orthogonal decomposition

V(\mathbb{R})=z^{\perp}\oplus z,

and the vectors
x_{z^{\perp}}
and
x_{z}
are the projections of
x
to the two summands.

As in §
2.1
, let
G\cong\operatorname{U}(1,1)
be the quasi-split unitary group over
\mathbb{Q}
, so that
\operatorname{SL}_{2}\cong G^{\prime}\subset G
.
Fix a base point
z_{0}\in\mathcal{D}
and consider the Schwartz function

\varphi_{\infty}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\varphi_{\infty}(z_{0},x)\in S(V(\mathbb{R})).

It has weight
n-2
under the action of the Weil representation (§
2.3
) of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. In other words, using the notation of (
2.2.1
),

\displaystyle\omega(k)(\varphi_{\infty})=\underline{k}^{n-2}\varphi_{\infty}

for all
k\in\operatorname{SO}(2,\mathbb{R})
.

A choice of
\varphi_{f}\in S(V(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
determines a Schwartz function
\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f}\in S(V(\mathbb{A}))
.
Applying the construction (
2.3.1
) yields a corresponding theta function
\theta(g,h,\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f})
of the variables
(g,h)\in G(\mathbb{A})\times H(\mathbb{A})
. Restricting the first variable to

g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\subset G(\mathbb{A})
, we obtain the Siegel theta function

\theta(g,h,\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f})=\sum_{x\in V(\mathbb{Q})}\omega(g,1)\varphi_{\infty}(z_{0},h_{\infty}^{-1}x)\cdot\varphi_{f}(h_{f}^{-1}x),

where
h=h_{\infty}h_{f}\in H(\mathbb{A})
.

We may view this theta function as a function of the variables

(\tau,z,h_{f})\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{D}\times H(\mathbb{A}_{f})

as follows. Let
g_{\tau}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
be as in (
2.1.3
), and choose an
h_{\infty}\in H(\mathbb{R})
with
h_{\infty}z_{0}=z
,
so that
\varphi_{\infty}(z_{0},h_{\infty}^{-1}x)=\varphi_{\infty}(z,x)
. Now set

(3.2.1)

\displaystyle\theta(\tau,z,h_{f},\varphi_{f})

\displaystyle=v^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\theta(g_{\tau},h_{\infty}h_{f},\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f})

\displaystyle=v\sum_{x\in V(\mathbb{Q})}e\big(\langle x_{z^{\perp}},x_{z^{\perp}}\rangle\tau-\langle x_{z},x_{z}\rangle\bar{\tau}\big)\cdot\varphi_{f}(h_{f}^{-1}x).

In the variable
\tau
, we have the transformation law

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle\theta(\gamma\tau,z,h_{f},\varphi_{f})=(c\tau+d)^{n-2}\theta(\tau,z,h_{f},\omega(\gamma)^{-1}\varphi_{f})

for
\gamma\in\Gamma
.
In the variable
z
, we have the transformation law

(3.2.3)

\displaystyle\theta(\tau,\delta z,\delta h_{f},\varphi_{f})=\theta(\tau,z,h_{f},\varphi_{f}).

for
\delta\in H(\mathbb{Q})
,

Now suppose that
\varphi_{f}\in S_{L}=\mathbb{C}[L^{\prime}/L]
is fixed by the action of
K
.
Using (
3.2.1
) and (
3.2.3
), we see that the Siegel theta function (
3.2.1
)
descends to a function on
\mathcal{H}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
. We are interested in the geometric theta integral

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\tau,\varphi_{f})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}\int_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\theta(\tau,z,h_{f},\varphi_{f})\,\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}

as a function of
\tau\in\mathcal{H}
.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

Assume that
n>2
or that
V
is anisotropic.
Recalling the notation of (
2.3.2
) and (
2.4.4
), we have

\kappa\cdot\mathcal{I}(\tau,\varphi_{f})=\left\langle E_{L}(\tau,s_{0},n-2),\varphi_{f}\right\rangle,

where the pairing on the right is (
2.4.3
).

Proof.

We rewrite the integral in (
3.2.4
) as an integral over the quotient
H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})
.
To this end, we normalize the Haar measure on the compact subgroup
H(\mathbb{R})_{z_{0}}\subset H(\mathbb{R})
so that the volume is
1
.
Then we normalize the Haar measure
dh_{\infty}
on
H(\mathbb{R})
so that the induced measure on the quotient

H(\mathbb{R})/H(\mathbb{R})_{z_{0}}\cong\mathcal{D}
agrees with the measure defined by
\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}
.
Finally, we normalize the Haar measure
dh_{f}
on
H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
so that
\operatorname{vol}(H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A}))=1
.
Setting

\operatorname{vol}(K)=\int_{K}dh_{f},

we then have the identities

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})=\int_{H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})/H(\mathbb{R})_{z_{0}}K}dh_{\infty}dh_{f}=\operatorname{vol}(K)^{-1}.

Using (
3.2.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\tau,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{vol}(K)\int_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\theta(\tau,z,h_{f},\varphi_{f})\,\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}

\displaystyle=v^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})}\theta(g_{\tau},h_{\infty}h_{f},\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f})\,dh_{\infty}dh_{f},

and applying the Siegel-Weil formula of Theorem
2.3.1
shows

\displaystyle\kappa\cdot\mathcal{I}(\tau,\varphi)

\displaystyle=v^{1-\frac{n}{2}}E(g_{\tau},\lambda(\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f}),s_{0}).

As
\varphi_{\infty}
has weight
n-2
, Lemma
2.2.1
implies

\displaystyle E(g_{\tau},\lambda(\varphi_{\infty}\otimes\varphi_{f}),s_{0})

\displaystyle=v^{\frac{n}{2}-1}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\infty}\backslash\Gamma}\operatorname{Im}(\gamma\tau)^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n-2}{2}}(c\tau+d)^{2-n}\cdot(\omega_{L}(\gamma)\varphi_{f})(0)

\displaystyle=v^{\frac{n}{2}-1}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\infty}\backslash\Gamma}\operatorname{Im}(\gamma\tau)^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n-2}{2}}(c\tau+d)^{2-n}\cdot(\omega_{L}^{\vee}(\gamma)^{-1}\delta_{0})(\varphi_{f}).

Here
\delta_{0}\in S_{L}^{\vee}
denotes the functional
\varphi_{f}\mapsto\varphi_{f}(0)
.
As the Weil representation is unitary, we have

(\omega_{L}^{\vee}(\gamma)^{-1}\delta_{0})(\varphi_{f})=\langle\bar{\omega}_{L}(\gamma)^{-1}\varphi_{0},\varphi_{f}\rangle.

To complete the proof, substitute this equality into the final expression above, and recall the definition of
E_{L}(\tau,s,n-2)
from (
2.4.4
).
∎

3.3.
Automorphic Green functions

Let
\sigma
be a finite dimensional representation of
\Gamma=\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

on a complex vector space
V_{\sigma}
, and assume that
\sigma
factors through a finite quotient.
In our applications,
\sigma
will be the Weil representation

\omega_{L}:\Gamma\to\operatorname{Aut}(S_{L})

of (
2.4.2
), or its complex conjugate, or its dual.

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, denote by
H_{k}(\sigma)
the vector space of (weak) harmonic Maass forms of weight
k
for the group
\Gamma
with representation
\sigma
as in
[
BY09
]
.
We write

S_{k}(\sigma)\subset M_{k}(\sigma)\subset M_{k}^{!}(\sigma)

for the spaces of cusp forms, holomorphic modular forms, and weakly holomorphic modular forms, respectively.

A harmonic Maass form
f\in H_{k}(\sigma)
has a Fourier expansion

(3.3.1)

\displaystyle f(\tau)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Q}\\
m\gg-\infty\end{subarray}}c^{+}(m)q^{m}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Q}\\
m<0\end{subarray}}c^{-}(m)\Gamma(1-k,4\pi|m|v)q^{m}

with coefficients
c^{\pm}(m)\in V_{\sigma}
.
Here
\tau=i+iv\in\mathcal{H}
,
q=e^{2\pi i\tau}
, and

\Gamma(s,x)=\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-t}t^{s-1}dt

is the incomplete gamma function.
The coefficients are supported on rational numbers with uniformly bounded denominators.
The first summand on the right hand side of (
3.3.1
) is denoted by
f^{+}
and is called the holomorphic part of
f
, the second summand is denoted by
f^{-}
and is called the non-holomorphic part.

As in
[
BF04
]
, there is a surjective conjugate-linear differential operator

(3.3.2)

\xi_{k}:H_{k}(\omega_{L})\to S_{2-k}(\bar{\omega}_{L})

defined by

\xi_{k}(f)(\tau)=2iv^{k}\overline{\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bar{\tau}}}.

Its kernel is
M^{!}_{k}(\omega_{L})
.

Suppose
f\in H_{2-n}(\omega_{L})
is
K
-fixed with Fourier coefficients
c^{\pm}(m)\in S_{L}
.
Define the special divisor associated to
f
by

(3.3.3)

\displaystyle Z(f)=\sum_{m>0}\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}c^{+}(-m,\mu)Z(m,\varphi_{\mu})\in\operatorname{Div}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})).

Here
c^{\pm}(m,\mu)\in\mathbb{C}
is the value of
c^{\pm}(m)
at
\mu\in L^{\prime}/L
, and

\varphi_{\mu}
is the characteristic function of the coset
\mu+\hat{L}
.

We recall from §3 of
[
BHY15
]
the construction of an automorphic Green function for
Z(f)
.
Using (
3.2.1
), define an
S_{L}
-valued Siegel theta function

(3.3.4)

\theta_{L}(\tau,z,h)=\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}\theta(\tau,z,h,\varphi_{\mu})\varphi_{\mu}

for
\tau\in\mathcal{H}
,
z\in\mathcal{D}
, and
h\in H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
The transformation law (
3.2.2
) implies that it defines a non-holomorphic modular form for
\Gamma
of weight
n-2
with representation
\bar{\omega}_{L}
,
and so, using the notation (
2.4.3
), the pairing
\langle f,\theta_{L}(\tau,z,h)\rangle
is
\Gamma
-invariant as a function of
\tau\in\mathcal{H}
.

Following
[
Bor98
]
and
[
BF04
]
, we consider the regularized theta lift

(3.3.5)

\displaystyle\Phi(z,h,f)=\int_{\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{reg}}\langle f,\theta_{L}(\tau,z,h)\rangle\,d\mu(\tau)

of
f
, where
d\mu(\tau)=\frac{du\,dv}{v^{2}}
is the invariant measure.
Here the integral is regularized as in
[
Bor98
]
and
[
BHY15
]
.
The main properties of

\Phi(f)=\Phi(z,h,f),

viewed as a function on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
, are summarized in the following theorem, see Theorem 3.3.1 and Proposition 3.3.4 of
[
BHY15
]
.

Theorem 3.3.1
.

The Green function
\Phi(z,h,f)
is smooth on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})\smallsetminus Z(f)
, and has logarithmic singularities along
Z(f)
.
It is integrable over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
, with respect to the invariant measure descended from
\mathcal{D}
, if
n>2
or if
V
is anisotropic. The corresponding current on smooth compactly supported
(n-1,n-1)
-forms on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
satisfies the Green equation

dd^{c}[\Phi(f)]+\delta_{Z(f)}=[dd^{c}\Phi(f)].

3.4.
Integrals of Green functions

In this section we assume that either
n>2
, or that
n\geq 1
and
V
is anisotropic.
Choose an
S_{L}
-valued harmonic Maass form

f\in H_{2-n}(\omega_{L})

fixed by the action of the compact open subgroup
K\subset H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
S_{L}
.

The main result of this section expresses the integral

(3.4.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(f)=\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}\int_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\Phi(f)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}

in terms of the derivatives
B^{\prime}(m,\mu,s_{0})
at
s_{0}=(n-1)/2
of the coefficients (
2.5.2
) of
E_{L}(\tau,s,n)
.
The proof follows the argument of
[
Kud03
]
in the orthogonal case, which is based on the Siegel-Weil formula. In fact, we slightly generalize this argument by allowing harmonic Maass forms for the inputs of the regularized theta lift.
Let
\kappa\in\{1,2\}
be as in (
2.3.2
).

Lemma 3.4.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(f)

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle f,\,E_{L}(\tau,s_{0},n-2)\right\rangle\,d\mu(\tau)-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T)\right).

Here
\mathcal{F}_{T}=\{\tau\in\mathcal{F}:\operatorname{Im}(\tau)\leq T\}
is the truncation at height
T
of the standard fundamental domain
\mathcal{F}
for the action of
\Gamma
on the complex upper half plane.

Proof.

Exactly as in Proposition 2.5 of
[
Kud03
]
, on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})\smallsetminus Z(f)
we have the equality

\Phi(z,h,f)=\lim_{T\to\infty}\left(\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\langle f,\theta_{L}(\tau,z,h)\rangle\,d\mu(\tau)-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T)\right).

The integral
\mathcal{I}(f)
is therefore given by the limit as
T\to\infty
of

\displaystyle\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}\int_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\langle f,\theta_{L}(\tau,z,h)\rangle\,d\mu(\tau)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T).

As
\mathcal{F}_{T}
is compact, we may interchange the order of integration.
Inserting (
3.3.4
) and (
3.2.4
), we find that
\mathcal{I}(f)
is the limit as
T\to\infty
of

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle f,\int_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\theta_{L}(\tau,z,h)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}\right\rangle\,d\mu(\tau)-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T),

which we rewrite as

\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}f(\tau)(\mu)\cdot\mathcal{I}(\tau,\varphi_{\mu})\,d\mu(\tau)-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T).

Applying Proposition
3.2.1
completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 3.4.2
.

If
n>2
, or if
n=2
and
V
is anisotropic,
the integral (
3.4.1
) satisfies

\mathcal{I}(f)=\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Q}\\
m>0\end{subarray}}c^{+}(-m,\mu)B^{\prime}(m,\mu,s_{0}).

If
n=1
, the integral (
3.4.1
) satisfies

\mathcal{I}(f)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mu\in L^{\prime}/L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Q}\\
m\geq 0\end{subarray}}c^{+}(-m,\mu)B^{\prime}(m,\mu,s_{0}).

Proof.

Abbreviate

A_{T}(f)=\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle f,\,E_{L}(\tau,s_{0},n-2)\right\rangle\,d\mu(\tau),

so that Lemma
3.4.1
becomes

(3.4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(f)

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\left(\frac{A_{T}(f)}{\kappa}-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T)\right).

Using Lemma
2.4.1
we see that

E_{L}(\tau,s_{0},n-2)\,d\mu(\tau)=\big(L_{n}E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\big)\,d\mu(\tau)=-\bar{\partial}E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\,d\tau,

and so

\displaystyle A_{T}(f)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle f,\,\bar{\partial}E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\right\rangle d\tau

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}d\left(\left\langle f,\,E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\,\right\rangle d\tau\right)+\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle(\bar{\partial}f),\,E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\right\rangle d\tau.

Applying Stokes’ theorem to the first term, and

(\bar{\partial}f)d\tau=-(L_{2-n}f)\,d\mu(\tau)

to the second, we obtain

\displaystyle A_{T}(f)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\partial\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle f,\,E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\,\right\rangle d\tau-\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle L_{2-n}f,\,E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\right\rangle d\mu(\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{u=0}^{1}\left\langle f(u+iT),\,E_{L}^{\prime}(u+iT,s_{0},n)\,\right\rangle du

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle\overline{\xi_{2-n}(f)},\,E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\right\rangle v^{n}d\mu(\tau),

where
\xi_{2-n}
is the differential operator (
3.3.2
).
Inserting the Fourier expansions (
3.3.1
) and (
2.5.1
) of
f
and the Eisenstein series yields

\displaystyle A_{T}(f)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in L^{\prime}/L\\
m\in\mathbb{Q}\end{subarray}}c^{+}(-m,\mu)B^{\prime}(m,\mu;s_{0},T)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in L^{\prime}/L\\
m\in\mathbb{Q}^{+}\end{subarray}}c^{-}(-m,\mu)\Gamma(n-1,4\pi|m|T)B^{\prime}(m,\mu;s_{0},T)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}\left\langle\overline{\xi_{2-n}(f)},\,E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)\right\rangle v^{n}d\mu(\tau).

The exponential decay of the incomplete gamma function and the polynomial growth of the coefficients
c^{-}(m,\mu)
imply that the second term goes to zero in the limit
T\to\infty
. The third term converges to the Petersson scalar product of
\xi_{2-n}(f)
and
E_{L}^{\prime}(\tau,s_{0},n)
. But since
\xi_{2-n}(f)
is cuspidal and hence orthogonal to Eisenstein series, this Petersson scalar product vanishes.
Inserting this into (
3.4.2
), we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(f)

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\to\infty}\bigg(\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in L^{\prime}/L\\
m\in\mathbb{Q}\end{subarray}}c^{+}(-m,\mu)B^{\prime}(m,\mu;s_{0},T)-c^{+}(0,0)\log(T)\bigg).

The exponential decay of the coefficients
B^{\prime}(m,\mu;s_{0},T)
in (
2.5.2
) for
m<0
and the subexponential growth of the coefficients
c^{+}(-m,\mu)
imply that the contribution of all
m<0
in the above term vanishes.
If
n>1
we obtain by virtue of Lemma
2.5.4
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(f)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in L^{\prime}/L\\
m>0\end{subarray}}c^{+}(-m,\mu)B^{\prime}(m,\mu,s_{0}),

as desired.
When
n=1
, again by Lemma
2.5.4
, there is an additional contribution to the sum from
m=0
.
∎

Chapter 4
Integral models and arithmetic intersection theory

We recall the integral models of
\mathrm{GU}(n-1,1)
Shimura varieties constructed by Pappas and Krämer, and the arithmetic intersection theory of Gillet-Soulé and Burgos-Kramer-Kühn on their toroidal compactifications.

From here until the end of the paper, we assume
D=-\mathrm{disc}({\bm{k}})
is odd.

4.1.
Moduli problems

For any
n\geq 1
, work of Pappas
[
Pap00
]
and Krämer
[
Krä03
]
, as summarized in §2.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, provides us with a regular and flat
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stack

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}})

with reduced fibers. For an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-scheme
S
, the objects of the groupoid
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}(S)
are quadruples
(A,\iota,\psi,\mathcal{F})
in which

•

A\to S
is an abelian scheme of dimension
n
,

•

\iota:\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\operatorname{End}(A)
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-action,

•

\psi:A\to A^{\vee}
is a principal polarization whose induceded Rosati involution restricts to complex conjugation on the image of
\iota
,

•

\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable hyperplane
1
1

1

That is to say, an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module local direct summand of rank
n-1

satisfying the signature
(n-1,1)
condition of Krämer
[
Krä03
]
: the actions of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on
\mathcal{F}
and on
\operatorname{Lie}(A)/\mathcal{F}
are through the structure morphism
i_{S}:\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
and its complex conjugate, respectively.

Remark 4.1.1
.

The stack
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
is denoted
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kra}}_{(n-1,1)}
in
[
BHK+20a
]
.

Remark 4.1.2
.

One does not know a good theory of integral models
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
if
2
is ramified in
{\bm{k}}
, and
this lack of knowledge is the main reason for restricting to quadratic imaginary fields of odd discriminant.

Definition 4.1.3
.

A finite dimensional
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space
W
is
relevant
if it admits a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice
\mathfrak{a}\subset W
.
Two relevant hermitian spaces
W
and
W^{\prime}
are
strictly similar
if there is a
{\bm{k}}
-linear isomorphism
W\cong W^{\prime}
identifying the hermitian forms up to scaling by a positive rational number.

Proposition 4.1.4
.

Let
o(D)
denote the number of prime divisors of
D
.

(1)

Any two self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices in a relevant
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space are isometric everywhere locally.

(2)

There are
2^{o(D)-1}
isomorphism classes of relevant hermitian spaces of any fixed signature
(r,s)
.
If
r+s
is odd all lie in the same strict similarity class, but if
r+s
is even all lie in different strict similarity classes.

Proof.

The first claim is a theorem of Jacobowitz
[
Jac62
]
, which uses our assumption that
D
is odd. The second claim is Lemma 2.11 of
[
KR14
]
.
∎

As in Proposition 2.12(i) of
[
KR14
]
, there is a decomposition

(4.1.1)

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}=\bigsqcup_{W}\mathcal{M}_{W},

where the disjoint union is over the strict similarity classes of relevant hermitian spaces
W
of signature
(n-1,1)
,
and the generic fiber of each
\mathcal{M}_{W}
is a Shimura variety of type
\mathrm{GU}(W)
.
When
n
is odd, Proposition
4.1.4
implies that the disjoint union has a single term.
When
n
is even, the disjoint union is over the
2^{o(D)-1}
isomorphism classes of relevant
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces.

Denote by

\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}})

the moduli stack of elliptic curves with CM by
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
. More precisely, the functor of points assigns to an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-scheme
S
the groupoid
\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}(S)
whose objects are pairs
(A_{0},\iota_{0})
in which

•

A_{0}\to S
is an elliptic curve,

•

\iota_{0}:\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\operatorname{End}(A_{0})
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-action satisfying the signature
(1,0)
condition: the induced action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on
\operatorname{Lie}(A_{0})
is through the structure morphism
i_{S}:\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

As
A_{0}
is an elliptic curve, there is a unique principal polarization

•

\psi_{0}:A_{0}\to A_{0}^{\vee}
,

and the induced Rosati involution on
\operatorname{End}(A_{0})
restricts to complex conjugation on the image of
\iota_{0}
. By Proposition 5.1 of
[
KRY99
]
or Proposition 2.1.2 of
[
How15
]
,
\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}
is finite étale over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
.

Remark 4.1.5
.

For ease of notation, we usually write objects of
\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}
simply as
A_{0}
, suppressing the remaining data from the notation. Similarly, objects of
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
will usually be written simply as
A
.

When
n=1
, the data of
\psi
and
\mathcal{F}
in the moduli problem
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
are uniquely determined, and
\mathcal{M}_{(0,1)}
classifies pairs
(A,\iota)
over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-schemes
S
in which
A\to S
is an elliptic curve and
\iota:\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\operatorname{End}(A)
is an action such that the induced action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\operatorname{Lie}(A)
is through the conjugate of the structure morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
. Replacing
\iota
by its conjugate therefore defines a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{(0,1)}\cong\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}.

In particular, there is a decomposition of
\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}
analogous to (
4.1.1
), but this statement is vacuous, as the decomposition has a single term by Proposition
4.1.4
.
If
W_{0}
denotes the unique, up to strict similarly, relevant
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space of signature
(1,0)
, it will be convenient to set

\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}=\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}.

Fix relevant
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces
(W_{0},h_{0})
and
(W,h)
of signatures
(1,0)
and
(n-1,1)
, and
self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices
\mathfrak{a}_{0}\subset W_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}\subset W
.
The hermitian forms induce alternating
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear forms

(4.1.2)

e_{0}(a,b)=\mathrm{Tr}_{{\bm{k}}/\mathbb{Q}}\left(\frac{h_{0}(a,b)}{\sqrt{-D}}\right),\qquad e(a,b)=\mathrm{Tr}_{{\bm{k}}/\mathbb{Q}}\left(\frac{h(a,b)}{\sqrt{-D}}\right)

on
W_{0}
and
W
, and
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}
are self-dual with respect to these forms.

Definition 4.1.6
.

Suppose we are given an integer
N\geq 1
and an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-scheme
S
with
N\in\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times}
.
A
level
N
-structure
on
A_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(S)
is a pair
(\eta_{0},\xi_{0})
consisting of isomorphisms

\eta_{0}:A_{0}[N]\cong\underline{\mathfrak{a}_{0}/N\mathfrak{a}_{0}},\qquad\xi_{0}:\mu_{N}\cong\underline{\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}

of étale sheaves on
S
that identify the Weil pairing on
A_{0}[N]
induced by the principal polarization with the pairing on
\mathfrak{a}_{0}/N\mathfrak{a}_{0}
induced by
e_{0}
.
Similarly, a
level
N
-structure
on
A\in\mathcal{M}_{W}(S)
is a pair
(\eta,\xi)
consisting of isomorphisms

\eta:A[N]\cong\underline{\mathfrak{a}/N\mathfrak{a}},\qquad\xi:\mu_{N}\cong\underline{\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}

that identify the Weil pairing on
A[N]
with the alternating pairing on
\mathfrak{a}/N\mathfrak{a}
.

Over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
, we obtain finite étale covers

(4.1.3)

\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)\to\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]},\qquad\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}

by adding level structures to the moduli problems. By the first claim of Proposition
4.1.4
, these are independent of the choices of
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}
.

4.2.
The exceptional divisor

Now assume
n\geq 2
.
We recall some more constructions from §2.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, following work of Pappas
[
Pap00
]
and Krämer
[
Krä03
]
.

There is a normal and flat
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stack

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}})

defined as the moduli space of triples
(A,\iota,\psi)
whose components are as in the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
. However, instead of including a hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
as part of the moduli problem, we demand that the action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on
\operatorname{Lie}(A)
satisfy a Kottwitz-style signature
(n-1,1)
condition, and the wedge conditions of Pappas. We refer the reader to §2.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
for the precise definitions.

Forgetting the hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
defines a morphism

(4.2.1)

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)},

which can be realized as a blow-up. To interpret it as such, define the
singular locus

(4.2.2)

\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)},

as the reduced locus of nonsmooth points.
It is a proper
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stack of dimension
0
, supported in characteristics dividing
D
.

The morphism (
4.2.1
) can be identified with the blow-up of the singular locus, and so there is a canonical cartesian diagram

(4.2.3)

\textstyle{{\mathrm{Exc}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}}}

in which the upper left corner is the
exceptional divisor
.
In particular, (
4.2.1
) is relatively representable and projective, and restricts to an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Exc}_{(n-1,1)}\cong\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}.

Remark 4.2.1
.

If
\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
is a prime above
p\mid D
with residue field
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
-points of the singular locus are those

A\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}})

for which the action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on the
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
-vector space
\operatorname{Lie}(A)
is through the reduction map
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
.
At such a point,
any
hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
is
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable and satisfies Krämer’s signature condition, and the fiber over
A
of the left vertical morphism in (
4.2.3
) is the projective space parametrizing all such
\mathcal{F}
.

4.3.
Toroidal compactification

We turn to the study of compactifications of
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
.
When
n=1
this stack is finite (hence proper) over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
, and so we set

\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(0,1)}=\mathcal{M}_{(0,1)}.

For the rest of this section, assume
n\geq 2
.

One finds in §2 of
[
How15
]
the construction of a toroidal compactification

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}\subset\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)},

obtained by imitating the constructions of Faltings and Chai
[
FC90
]
. If one works over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/D]
, this is a very special case of the constructions of Lan
[
Lan13
]
.
The compactification is regular, and is smooth outside the exceptional divisor of (
4.2.3
).

The universal abelian scheme
A\to\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
extends uniquely to a semi-abelian scheme

\bar{A}\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)}

with
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-action.
At a geometric point of the boundary, this semi-abelian scheme is an extension of an abelian variety by a torus of rank two.
The universal hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
over
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
extends uniquely to an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable hyperplane

(4.3.1)

\bar{\mathcal{F}}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(\bar{A}),

which again satisfies Krämer’s signature
(n-1,1)
condition.

There is a similar toroidal compactification of
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}^{\mathrm{Pap}}
(now only normal instead of regular), and a Cartesian diagram

(4.3.2)

\textstyle{{\mathrm{Exc}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}}}

extending (
4.2.3
).
The vertical arrow on the right is the blow-up along the closed immersion in the bottom row.

Remark 4.3.1
.

For general Shimura varieties, a toroidal compactification depends on a choice of rational polyhedral cone decomposition of a convex cone sitting inside the real points of a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space.
For our
\mathrm{GU}(n-1,1)
Shimura varieties, the vector space in question is one-dimensional.
Hence the rational polyhedral cone decomposition is uniquely determined, and the toroidal compactifications are canonical.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{n}
be the moduli stack of principally polarized abelian schemes of dimension
n
.
There are canonical morphisms

(4.3.3)

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}\xrightarrow{\eqref{kramer-pappas}}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}^{\mathrm{Pap}}\to\mathcal{A}_{n/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}},

in which the first arrow is projective, and the second is finite.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

If
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
is any choice of smooth Faltings-Chai toroidal compactification,

\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
is canonically identified with the normalization of

(4.3.4)

\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}^{\mathrm{Pap}}\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}.

See
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0BAK
]
for normalization.

Proof.

By
[
How15
]
, the universal abelian scheme
A\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
extends (necessarily uniquely) to a semi-abelian scheme
\bar{A}
over the compactification.
This extension satisfies a universal property: Suppose
S
is an irreducible normal scheme with generic point
\eta\to S
, and we are given a morphism

\eta\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}.

If the pullback
A_{\eta}
extends to a semi-abelian scheme over
S
, then this extension is the pullback of

\bar{A}
via a (necessarily unique) morphism

S\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}

restricting to the given morphism at the generic point.

This extension property is analogous to Theorem IV.5.7(5) of
[
FC90
]
, but the statement is simplified by the uniqueness of the rational polyhedral cone decomposition of Remark
4.3.1
.
If one works over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/D]
, the extension property is a special case of Theorem 6.4.1.1(6) of
[
Lan13
]
. The proof over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
is exactly the same.

On the other hand, Theorem 11.4 of
[
Lan16
]
shows that the compactification of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
defined by normalization also carries a semi-abelian scheme satisfying the same universal property, and the lemma follows.
∎

Fix an
\mathcal{M}_{W}
as in (
4.1.1
).
We need compactifications of the étale covers

\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}

of (
4.1.3
).
Rather than repeat the constructions of
[
How15
]
with level structure,
we will appeal to the results of
[
Lan16
]
and
[
MP19
]
, in which integral models and compactifications of general Hodge-type Shimura varieties are constructed as normalizations of Faltings-Chai compactifications of Siegel moduli spaces.
These results cannot be applied directly to the covers above, as the failure of the first arrow in (
4.3.3
) to be finite (it contracts the entire exceptional divisor to a
0
-dimensional substack) implies that Lemma
4.3.2
is false if
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
is replaced by
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
. Thus the proof of Proposition
4.3.3
below requires the roundabout step of first compactifying covers of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
.

For any
N\geq 1
, define
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
as the normalization of

\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

The stack
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
is regular, flat, and proper over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
, and is a projective scheme if
N\geq 3
.
It is smooth away from its exceptional divisor

\mathrm{Exc}_{(n-1,1)}\times_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N).

In particular, it is smooth in a neighborhood of its boundary, which is a Cartier divisor smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
.

Proof.

There is a decomposition

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}=\bigsqcup_{W}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W},

exactly as in (
4.1.1
). We may add
N\geq 1
level structure, exactly as in Definition
4.1.6
, to obtain a finite étale cover

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

This cover has a compactification
\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
defined as the normalization of

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]},

which, by Lemma
4.3.2
, is the same as the normalization of

(4.3.5)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

The properness, flatness, and normality of
\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
follow from its construction as the normalization of a proper, flat, and smooth
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
-stack.
It is not regular (as this is not true even of its interior). However, its singular locus

\mathrm{Sing}_{W}(N)=\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}\times_{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)

is
0
-dimensional and proper over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
, and the smoothness of

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\smallsetminus\mathrm{Sing}_{W}(N)

implies the smoothness of

\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\smallsetminus\mathrm{Sing}_{W}(N).

This is a consequence of the following principle, found in §14 of
[
Lan16
]
and Theorem 1 of
[
MP19
]
: if
U\subset\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
is an open neighborhood of the boundary
\partial\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
, then the singularities of
U
are no worse than the singularities of
U\smallsetminus\partial\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
.

By the same principle (and its proof), the smoothness of
\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
near the boundary implies the smoothness of the boundary divisor itself. This is actually a particular feature of our
\mathrm{GU}(W)
Shimura varieties. For more general Shimura varieties the boundary has a canonical stratification by locally closed substacks (see Theorem 4.1.5 of
[
MP19
]
or §9 of
[
Lan16
]
), and the above cited principle tells us that each
stratum
is smooth. In our special case each boundary stratum is a divisor, and is simply a union of connected components of
\partial\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
. Hence the entire boundary is a Cartier divisor smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
.

Using the characterization of (
4.3.2
) as a blow-up, one may identify
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
with the blow-up of
\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
along its singular locus. As the singular locus does not meet the boundary, it follows that
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
is itself proper and flat with boundary a smooth Cartier divisor. Moreover, this shows that
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
is smooth away from the exceptional divisor of the blow-up, and in particular in an open neighborhood of its boundary. As the interior is regular, being étale over the regular stack
\mathcal{M}_{W}
, we find that
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
is regular.

Finally, when
N\geq 3
we claim that both the source and target of

\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)

are projective schemes. As this morphism is a blow-up, it suffices to prove this for the target.
For this, we use the results of Chapter V.5 of
[
FC90
]
. Adding principal level structure yields a finite étale cover

\mathcal{A}_{n}(N)\to\mathcal{A}_{n/\mathbb{Z}[1/N]},

and the compactification
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
may be chosen so that (
4.3.5
) factors as

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]},

where
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n}(N)
is the smooth projective scheme obtained by normalizing

\mathcal{A}_{n}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

This realizes
\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
as the normalization of the first arrow in the composition, and hence provides us with a finite and relatively representable morphism

\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{n}(N).

As the target is a projective scheme, so is the source.
∎

Remark 4.3.4
.

One could avoid the constructions of
[
How15
]
and
[
Lan13
]
altogether, and define

\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)}
from the start as a blow-up of the normalization of (
4.3.4
).
All properties that we need to know about this compactification could then be deduced from
[
Lan16
]
and
[
MP19
]
, except for the extension (
4.3.1
) of the universal hyperplane across the boundary (which is needed in the proof of Proposition
5.2.1
).
For this one needs detailed information about the local charts near the boundary, as found in
[
How15
]
.

4.4.
Arithmetic Chow groups

We summarize the arithmetic intersection theory of
Gillet-Soulé
[
GS90
,
Sou92
]
and Burgos-Kramer-Kühn
[
BBGK07
,
BGKK07
]

for the Shimura varieties
\mathcal{M}_{W}
of (
4.1.1
),
mostly for the purposes of fixing normalizations of arithmetic degrees, heights, and volumes.

As usual, denote by
\widehat{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W})
the group of isomorphism classes of hermitian line bundles on
\mathcal{M}_{W}
, and similarly for the toroidal compactification
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
.
In order to account for metrics that do not extend smoothly across the boundary, when we work on the compactification
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
we must relax the notion of a hermitian metric.

Accordingly, denote by
\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})
the group of isomorphism classes of pre-log singular hermitian line bundles on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
, in the sense of Definition 1.20 of
[
BBGK07
]
.
Exactly as in Proposition 5.2.1 of
[
How20
]
, the natural maps

(4.4.1)

\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W})\to\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W})

are injective.

Fix an integer
N\geq 3
, so that the toroidal compactification
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
of Proposition
4.3.3
is a regular scheme of dimension
n
, projective and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
.
For
0\leq d\leq n
, we have a homomorphism of arithmetic Chow groups

(4.4.2)

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}_{\mathrm{GS}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N))\to\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

from Theorem 6.23 and (7.49) of
[
BGKK07
]
.

The domain of (
4.4.2
) is the arithmetic Chow group of Gillet-Soulé
[
GS90
,
Sou92
]
, defined as the rational equivalence classes of arithmetic cycles
(\mathcal{Z},g)
in which
\mathcal{Z}
is a codimension
d
cycle on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
, and
g
is a
(d-1,d-1)
current on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})
satisfying the Green equation

dd^{c}g+\delta_{\mathcal{Z}}=[\omega]

of currents for some smooth
(d,d)
form
\omega
.

The codomain of (
4.4.2
) is the pre-log-log arithmetic Chow group of Burgos-Kramer-Kühn
[
BBGK07
,
BGKK07
]
. It is defined as rational equivalence classes of arithmetic cycles
(\mathcal{Z},\mathfrak{g})

where
\mathcal{Z}
is as above, and
\mathfrak{g}
is as in Definition 1.15 of
[
BBGK07
]
.
For our purposes it is enough to know that
\mathfrak{g}=(\omega,g)
is a pair consisting of a

(d-1,d-1)
current on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})
and a smooth
(d,d)
form
\omega
on the interior

\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})
, again related by the above Green equation (by Proposition 7.44 of
[
BGKK07
]
).
There are further constraints: The form
\omega
is required to have pre-log-log growth along the boundary of the toroidal compactification (which implies local integrability on the compactification, so that the Green equation makes sense).
The current
g
is required to be represented by a smooth form defined on the complement of some codimension
d
cycle on
\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})
, again satisfying certain growth conditions.
Moreover, the relation imposed between
g
and
\omega
is stronger than the Green equation. Nevertheless, the object
\mathfrak{g}
is completely determined by the current
g
, so we usually just write
(\mathcal{Z},g)
for the arithmetic cycle.

Whenever
N\mid N^{\prime}
the map

\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N^{\prime})\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)

induces a pullback

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N^{\prime}),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

allowing us to define, exactly as in §6.3 of
[
BGKK07
]
,

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})=\varprojlim_{N\geq 3}\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

There is an
arithmetic intersection pairing

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d_{1}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d_{2}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d_{1}+d_{2}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}},

an
arithmetic Chern class map

\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

and an
arithmetic degree

\widehat{\deg}:\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\mathbb{R}

induced by the analogous structures on the arithmetic Chow group of each scheme
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
with
N\geq 3
.

Remark 4.4.1
.

The conventions for delta currents, currents associated to smooth forms, and Green forms used in
[
BGKK07
]
and
[
BBGK07
]
differ from those of Gillet-Soulé
[
GS90
,
Sou92
]
by powers of
2
and
2\pi i
.
We will always use the conventions of Gillet-Soulé.
For example, the arithmetic Chern class map sends a pre-log singular hermitian line bundle
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
to the arithmetic divisor

\widehat{\mathrm{div}}(s)=(\mathrm{div}(s),-\log\|s\|^{2})

for any nonzero rational section
s
.

To make explicit the normalization of the arithmetic degree, first note that for each
N\geq 3
,
the finite étale cover
\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}
has constant fiber degree, in the sense that there is a
d_{N}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying

(4.4.3)

\frac{d_{N}}{\#\operatorname{Aut}(x)}=\#\{\mbox{geometric points}\ y\to\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)\ \mbox{above}\ x\}

for every geometric point
x\to\mathcal{M}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}
.
Now suppose we have a class

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}=(\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})_{N\geq 3}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

If we write each

\mathcal{Z}_{N}=\sum_{i}m_{N,i}\mathcal{Z}_{N,i}

as a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear combination of
0
-dimensional irreducible closed subschemes on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
,
then the real number

\widehat{\deg}_{N}(\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})=\sum_{i}m_{N,i}\cdot\#\mathcal{Z}_{N,i}(\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{alg}}_{i})\cdot\log(\#\mathbb{F}_{i})+\int_{\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})}g_{N},

where
\mathbb{F}_{i}
is the residue field of the unique prime of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
below
\mathcal{Z}_{N,i}
,
is well-defined up to adding a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
, and

(4.4.4)

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}})=\frac{1}{d_{N}}\cdot\widehat{\deg}_{N}(\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})

up to the same ambiguity.

Remark 4.4.2
.

There is no
1/2
in front of the integral, in apparent disagreement with §3.4.3 of
[
GS90
]
.
In fact there is no disagreement. For us
\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})
means the set of all
morphisms
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)
as
{\bm{k}}
-schemes, whereas Gillet-Soulé would take morphisms as
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes.
Thus for Gillet-Soulé the domain of integration would be our
\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})

together with its complex conjugate
, yielding an integral twice as big as ours.

We will also need the
complex degree

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}:\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\mathbb{Q}

sending a class

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}=(\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})_{N\geq 3}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

to

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}})=\frac{1}{d_{N}}\cdot\deg(\mathcal{Z}_{N/\mathbb{C}}),

where the degree on the right hand side is the usual degree of a
0
-cycle on the complex fiber
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)_{/\mathbb{C}}
.
Equivalently,

(4.4.5)

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}})=\frac{1}{d_{N}}\cdot\int_{\mathcal{M}_{W}(N)(\mathbb{C})}\omega_{N}

is the integral of the
(n-1,n-1)
form determined by the Green equation.

dd^{c}g_{N}+\delta_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}}=[\omega_{N}].

4.5.
Volumes and heights

For a pre-log singular hermitian line bundle

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

define the
complex volume

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})=\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1})

by applying the complex degree of §
4.4
to the
(n-1)
-fold iterated arithmetic intersection of the arithmetic Chern class

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

Of course this only depends on the restriction of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
to a line bundle on the complex fiber
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W/\mathbb{C}}
.
Similarly, define the
arithmetic volume

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})=\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{n})

using the iterated arithmetic intersection and arithmetic degree of §
4.4
.

Remark 4.5.1
.

The pre-log singularity of the metric on
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
implies the integrability of its Chern form: the smooth
(1,1)
form on
\mathcal{M}_{W}(\mathbb{C})
defined locally by

\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}})=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\partial\overline{\partial}\log\|s\|^{2}=-dd^{c}\log\|s\|^{2}

for any nonzero holomorphic section
s
.
We will frequently use the equivalent characterization of the complex volume

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{W}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}

as the (orbifold) integral of its top exterior power, which follows from (
4.4.5
).

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be a divisor on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
, and assume that
\mathcal{Z}
intersects the boundary

\partial\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
properly in the generic fiber.
As in the discussion following Theorem 7.58 of
[
BGKK07
]
, one can then define the
arithmetic height
(or
Faltings height
)
of
\mathcal{Z}
with respect to
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
, denoted

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}}(\mathcal{Z})\in\mathbb{R}.

To make it explicit, fix
N\geq 3
. Choose a pair
(\mathcal{Y},g_{\mathcal{Y}})
representing the iterated intersection

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

and do this in such a way that the supports of
\mathcal{Z}_{N}
and
\mathcal{Y}
do not intersect in the generic fiber, where
\mathcal{Z}_{N}
is the pullback of
\mathcal{Z}
via

\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

Now form the intersection

\mathcal{Z}_{N}\cdot\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{CH}^{n}_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}\cap\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N))_{\mathbb{Q}}

in the Chow group with support along the closed subset
\mathrm{Sppt}(\mathcal{Z}_{N})\cap\mathrm{Sppt}(\mathcal{Y})
.
As this closed subset is supported in finitely many nonzero characteristics, there is a natural change-of-support map

\operatorname{CH}^{n}_{\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N))\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\\
\mathfrak{p}\nmid N\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CH}^{n}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N))

where
\operatorname{CH}^{n}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N))
is the Chow group with support in the mod
\mathfrak{p}
fiber of
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
.
For each
\mathfrak{p}
there is a degree map

(4.5.1)

\deg_{\mathfrak{p}}:\operatorname{CH}^{n}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N))\to\mathbb{Z}

sending a reduced and irreducible codimension
n
subscheme (a.k.a., a closed point)
\mathcal{C}\subset\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
supported at
\mathfrak{p}
to

\deg_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathcal{C})=\#\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}})

where
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/\mathfrak{p}
.
The arithmetic height satisfies

(4.5.2)

d_{N}\cdot\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}}(\mathcal{Z})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\\
\mathfrak{p}\nmid N\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\end{subarray}}\deg_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathcal{Z}_{N}\cdot\mathcal{Y})\cdot\log(\#\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})+\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(\mathbb{C})}g_{\mathcal{Y}}

up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
, where
d_{N}
is as in (
4.4.3
).

A choice of pre-log singular hermitian metric on the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Z})
determines a class

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\in\widehat{\mathrm{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

If we view the constant function
1
as a global section of
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Z})
and set
g_{\mathcal{Z}}=-\log\|1\|^{2}
,
the arithmetic Chern class of this hermitian line bundle is

(\mathcal{Z},g_{\mathcal{Z}})\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

and Proposition 7.56 of
[
BGKK07
]
gives the relation

(4.5.3)

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1})=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}}(\mathcal{Z})+\int_{\mathcal{M}_{W}(\mathbb{C})}g_{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1}.

From the point of view of arithmetic volumes and heights, some hermitian line bundles are essentially indistinguishable.

Definition 4.5.2
.

We say that

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1},\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W})

are
numerically equivalent
if the difference

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1}-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
lies in the subgroup

(4.5.4)

\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\subset\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W})

of pre-log singular hermitian line bundles from (
4.4.1
), and satisfies

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{E}})=0

for all
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

Lemma 4.5.3
.

If
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
are numerically equivalent, then their Chern forms are equal. If we add the assumption that both lie in the subgroup (
4.5.4
) then

(4.5.5)

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{1}})=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}(\mathcal{Z})

for any divisor
\mathcal{Z}
as above.

Proof.

Set
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{1}-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
.
If
g
is any smooth
(n-2,n-2)
form on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(\mathbb{C})
, the arithmetic cycle class

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}=(0,g)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

satisfies

0=\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{E}})=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{W}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{E}})\wedge g.

As this holds for all choices of
g
, we must have
\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{E}})=0
.
This proves the desired equality of Chern forms.
The first equality of (
4.5.5
) is clear from

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{1}})=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{2}^{n-i}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{i}),

as every term with
i>0
vanishes. The second equality follows from (
4.5.3
) and the equality of Chern forms already proved.
∎

If
\mathcal{Z}
is a Cartier divisor on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
supported in nonzero characteristics, the line bundle

\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Z})
is canonically trivialized in the generic fiber by the constant function
1
.
For any real number
c
, denote by

(4.5.6)

(\mathcal{Z},c)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W})

the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Z})
endowed with the constant metric characterized by
-\log\|1\|^{2}=c
.
In particular, adding
(0,c)
to a hermitian line bundle just rescales
\|\cdot\|^{2}
by
e^{-c}
. The following lemma describes the effect of this on arithmetic volumes.

Lemma 4.5.4
.

For any
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})
and any constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}+(0,c)\big)=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})+nc\int_{\mathcal{M}_{W}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{n-1},

where
\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})
is the Chern form of Remark
4.5.1
.

Proof.

A trivial induction argument shows that

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{k-1}\cdot(0,c)=(0,c\cdot\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{k-1})\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{k}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

for all
k>0
. As
(0,c)\cdot(0,c)=0
we deduce

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}+(0,c)\big)^{k}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{k}+k\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{k-1}\cdot(0,c)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{k}+k(0,c\cdot\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})^{k-1}).

Setting
k=n
and taking the arithmetic degree completes the proof.
∎

Chapter 5
A Shimura variety and its line bundles

For the rest of this paper we fix a
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space
V
of signature
(n-1,1)
, with
n\geq 1
, and assume that
V
admits a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice (
1.5.1
). The hermitian form on
V
is denoted
\langle-,-\rangle
.
We will associate to
V
an
n
-dimensional open and closed substack

\mathcal{S}_{V}\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)},

construct some interesting hermitian line bundles on it, and explain the relations between them.

5.1.
A special Shimura variety

To attach a Shimura variety to our fixed
V
, choose relevant (Definition
4.1.3
) hermitian spaces
(W_{0},h_{0})
and
(W,h)
of signatures
(1,0)
and
(n-1,1)
, respectively, in such a way that

(5.1.1)

V\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(W_{0},W)

as
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces, where the hermitian form
\langle-,-\rangle
on the right is defined by the relation

(5.1.2)

\langle x,y\rangle\cdot h_{0}(w_{0},w^{\prime}_{0})=h(x(w_{0}),y(w^{\prime}_{0}))

for all
w_{0},w_{0}^{\prime}\in W_{0}
and
x,y\in V
.

Remark 5.1.1
.

Such
W_{0}
and
W
always exist, and in fact there is a distinguished choice: take
W_{0}={\bm{k}}
with its norm form, and
W=V
. Any other choice of
W_{0}
will be strictly similar to this distinguished choice (this is a trivial case of Proposition
4.1.4
), and the relation (
5.1.1
) then forces
W
to be strictly similar to
V
. In particular, the Shimura varieties

\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}\cong\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{M}_{W}\cong\mathcal{M}_{V}

depend only on
V
, and not on the particular
W_{0}
and
W
chosen in (
5.1.1
).

As in §2.2 of
[
KR14
]
, if
S
is a connected
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-scheme and

(A_{0},A)\in\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(S)\times\mathcal{M}_{W}(S),

then
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},A)
carries a positive definite hermitian form

(5.1.3)

\langle x,y\rangle=\psi_{0}^{-1}\circ y^{\vee}\circ\psi\circ x\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}},

where
\psi_{0}:A_{0}\to A_{0}^{\vee}
and
\psi:A\to A^{\vee}
are the principal polarizations.

As in §2.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, there is an open and closed substack

(5.1.4)

\mathcal{S}_{V}\subset\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}_{W}

characterized by its points valued in algebraically closed fields, which are those pairs

(A_{0},A)\in\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(F)\times\mathcal{M}_{W}(F)

for which there exists an isometry

V\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\big(T_{\ell}(A_{0}),T_{\ell}(A)\big)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}

of
{\bm{k}}_{\ell}
-hermitian spaces for every
\ell\neq\mathrm{char}(F)
.
Here the hermitian form on the right is defined by imitating the construction (
5.1.3
) on the level of
\ell
-adic Tate modules.
When
F=\mathbb{C}
this is equivalent (by the Hasse principle for hermitian spaces) to the existence of an isometry of
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces

V\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bm{k}}}\big(H_{1}(A_{0},\mathbb{Q}),H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Q})\big),

where the hermitian form on the right hand side is again defined by imitating the construction (
5.1.3
) in Betti homology.

Remark 5.1.2
.

The projection

\mathcal{S}_{V}\to\mathcal{M}_{W}

is a finite étale surjection, and the fiber over a geometric point
x\in\mathcal{M}_{W}(F)
satisfies

\sum_{y\in\mathcal{S}_{V,x}(F)}\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Aut}(y)|}=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\\
2^{1-o(D)}&\mbox{if $n$ is odd}.\end{cases}

Remark 5.1.3
.

The stack
\mathcal{S}_{V}
is denoted
\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{Kra}}
in
[
BHK+20a
]
.
Later we will want to vary
V
, and so we have included it in the notation to avoid confusion.
As explained in [loc. cit.], the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{V}
is a Shimura variety
for the subgroup
G\subset\mathrm{GU}(W_{0})\times\mathrm{GU}(W)
of pairs
(g_{0},g)
for which the similitude factors of the two components are equal.

Definition 5.1.4
.

Let
N
be a positive integer, and let
S
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-scheme with
N\in\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times}
.
A
level
N
-structure
on a pair
(A_{0},A)\in\mathcal{S}_{V}(S)
consists of level
N
-structures
(\eta_{0},\xi_{0})
and
(\eta,\xi)
on
A_{0}
and
A
, in the sense of Definition
4.1.6
, such that
\xi_{0}=\xi
.

Adding level structure to pairs
(A_{0},A)
defines a finite étale cover

\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)\to\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]},

and (
5.1.4
) lifts to a canonical open and closed immersion

\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)\subset\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}\mathcal{M}_{W}(N).

Define a toroidal compactification

(5.1.5)

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)\subset\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)

as the Zariski closure of
\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)
.
When
N=1
we abbreviate

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}=\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(1).

Note that
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
is also characterized as the normalization of

\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

Remark 5.1.5
.

As (
5.1.5
) is an open and closed immersion, and
\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)
is finite étale over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
, the compactification
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
inherits all the nice properties of
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)
. In particular, Proposition
4.3.3
holds word-for-word with
\mathcal{M}_{W}
replaced by
\mathcal{S}_{V}
, and the same is true of the entire discussion of arithmetic intersection theory in §
4.4
and §
4.5
.

Definition 5.1.6
.

If
n\geq 2
, define the
exceptional divisor

\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\mathcal{S}_{V}

as the pullback of the exceptional divisor (
4.2.3
) via
\mathcal{S}_{V}\to\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
. Equivalently, it is defined by the cartesian diagram

(5.1.6)

\textstyle{{\mathrm{Exc}_{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}}.}

Definition 5.1.7

(
[
KR14
]
)
.

For any positive
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
Kudla-Rapoport divisor

\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
is the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stack classifying triples
(A_{0},A,x)
consisting of a pair

(A_{0},A)\in\mathcal{S}_{V}(S)

and an

x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},A)

satisfying
\langle x,x\rangle=m
.

The natural forgetful morphism

\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)\to\mathcal{S}_{V}

is finite and unramified, with image of codimension
1
.
Denote by

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}

the normalization of
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
.

Remark 5.1.8
.

The morphism
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
determines a Weil divisor on
\mathcal{S}_{V}
in the usual way:
each irreducible component of
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
contributes an irreducible component to the divisor, counted with multiplicity equal to the length of the local ring of its generic point.
By abuse of notation, we denote this Weil divisor again by
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
.
Similarly there is a divisor determined by
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
, denoted the same way; it agrees with the Zariski closure in
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
of the divisor
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
.

Loosely speaking, each Kudla-Rapoport divisor is a union of unitary Shimura varieties associated to
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces of signature
(n-2,1)
. In Chapter
6
we will make this more precise, at least when
m
is a prime split in
{\bm{k}}
.

5.2.
Construction of hermitian line bundles

The construction (
1.1.1
) associates to the universal CM elliptic curve
A_{0}\to\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}=\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}
its metrized Hodge bundle

(5.2.1)

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}).

Similarly, the universal
A\to\mathcal{M}_{W}
determines a metrized Hodge bundle

(5.2.2)

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W}).

Pulling back the universal objects via projection to the two factors in (
5.1.4
) yields a universal pair
(A_{0},A)
of polarized abelian schemes (of relative dimensions
1
and
n
) over
\mathcal{S}_{V}
.
As in §2.4 of
[
BHK+20a
]
there is a
metrized line bundle of modular forms

(5.2.3)

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V}).

The line bundle underlying (
5.2.3
) has inverse

(5.2.4)

\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}=\operatorname{Lie}(A_{0})\otimes\operatorname{Lie}(A)/\mathcal{F},

where
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
is the universal hyperplane satisfying Krämer’s signature condition (§
4.1
).
The hermitian metric is defined as in §7.2 of
[
BHK+20a
]
:
if we use Proposition 2.4.2 of
[
BHK+20a
]
to identify

(5.2.5)

\mathcal{L}_{V,z}\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bm{k}}}(H_{1}(A_{0,z},\mathbb{Q}),H_{1}(A_{z},\mathbb{Q}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}\cong V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}

at a complex point
z\in\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
, the line
\mathcal{L}_{V,z}
is isotropic with respect to the
\mathbb{C}
-bilinear extension of the
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear form
[x,y]=\mathrm{Tr}_{{\bm{k}}/\mathbb{Q}}\langle x,y\rangle

on
V
, and

(5.2.6)

\|s\|^{2}=-\frac{[s,\overline{s}]}{4\pi e^{\gamma}}

for any
s\in\mathcal{L}_{V,z}
.
Note that our
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
is denoted
\widehat{\bm{\omega}}
in
[
BHK+20a
,
BHK+20b
]
.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

The hermitian line bundles (
5.2.2
) and (
5.2.3
) lie in the subgroups

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\subset\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{M}_{W})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\subset\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V}),

respectively, of (
4.4.1
).

Proof.

The extension to
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}
of the line bundle

\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}}\cong\det(\operatorname{Lie}(A))^{-1}

underlying (
5.2.2
) is part of (
4.3.1
).
The fact that the hermitian metric has a pre-log singularity along the boundary is a special case of
Theorem 6.16 of
[
BGKK05
]
.
Note that this also uses Proposition 3.2 of
[
FiM09
]
, which shows that any rank one log-singular hermitian vector bundle in the sense of
[
BGKK05
]
is also a pre-log-singular hermitian line bundle in the sense of Definition 1.20 of
[
BBGK07
]
.
This proves the claim for (
5.2.2
), and the proof for (
5.2.3
) is the same.
∎

Pulling back the hermitian line bundles (
5.2.1
) and (
5.2.2
) via projection to the two factors in (
5.1.5
), we obtain three hermitian line bundles

(5.2.7)

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}},\,\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}},\,\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

In the remaining sections of this chapter, we will make explicit the relations between them.
The reader may wish to skip directly to Theorem
5.5.1
for the main results.

5.3.
An application of the Chowla-Selberg formula

We will prove that, up to numerical equivalence (Definition
4.5.2
), the first line bundle in (
5.2.7
) is just the trivial line bundle with a constant metric.
The constant defining the metric is an interesting quantity in its own right.
Recall the Chowla-Selberg formula: the Faltings height, normalized as in §5.2 of
[
BHK+20b
]
,
of any elliptic curve with CM by
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
is

(5.3.1)

h^{\mathrm{Falt}}_{\bm{k}}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{L^{\prime}(0,\varepsilon)}{L(0,\varepsilon)}-\frac{1}{4}\log(4\pi^{2}D).

Proposition 5.3.1
.

The metrized line bundles

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})\quad\mbox{and}\quad(0,C_{1})\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})

are numerically equivalent, where
C_{1}=\log(2\pi)+2h^{\mathrm{Falt}}_{\bm{k}}
, and we are using the notation of (
4.5.6
).

Proof.

Fix an
N\geq 3
. As
\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)
is finite étale over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
, there is an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)\cong\bigsqcup_{i}\mathcal{X}_{i}

with each
\mathcal{X}_{i}\cong\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{{\bm{k}}_{i}}[1/N])
for a finite field extension
{\bm{k}}_{i}/{\bm{k}}
unramified outside
N
.
For each
\mathcal{X}_{i}
, consider the metrized Hodge bundle

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{X}_{i}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{X}_{i})

of the universal
A_{0}\to\mathcal{X}_{i}
. The underlying line bundle can be identified with an element in the ideal class group of
\mathcal{O}_{{\bm{k}}_{i}}[1/N]
, and hence some power of it admits a trivializing section

s_{i}\in H^{0}\big(\mathcal{X}_{i},(\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{X}_{i}})^{\otimes d_{i}}\big)\cong H^{0}(A_{0},\Omega^{\otimes d_{i}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{X}_{i}}).

Comparing (
1.1.1
) with the definition of the Faltings height (as in §5.2 of
[
BHK+20b
]
) shows that

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{\#\mathcal{X}_{i}(\mathbb{C})}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{i}(\mathbb{C})}\log\|s_{i,x}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=d_{i}\cdot C_{1},

up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
.

Letting
d
be the least common multiple of all
d_{i}
’s, we see that

\big(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}\big)^{\otimes d}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N))

admits a trivializing section
s
with
-\log\|s\|^{2}
constant on every connected component of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})
, and such that the average value of
-\log\|s\|^{2}
over any
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb{C}/{\bm{k}})
-orbit of components is
d\cdot C_{1}
,
up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
.

If we represent

d\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N))

by the arithmetic divisor

\widehat{\mathrm{div}}(s)=(0,-\log\|s\|^{2}),

then for any

(\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N))

the vanishing of the Chern form of
-\log\|s\|^{2}
implies the
*
-product formula

[-\log\|s\|^{2}]*g_{N}=-\log\|s\|^{2}\wedge\delta_{\mathcal{Z}_{N}},

which implies the intersection formula

d\cdot\widehat{\deg}_{N}\big((\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}\big)=-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{Z}_{N}(\mathbb{C})}\log\|s_{x}\|^{2}

up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
.

Let
L\subset\mathbb{C}
be a finite Galois extension of
{\bm{k}}
large enough that all complex points of
\mathcal{Z}_{N}
are defined over
L
, and rewrite the equality above as

d\cdot\widehat{\deg}_{N}\big((\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}\big)=\frac{-1}{[L:{\bm{k}}]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathcal{Z}_{N}(L)\\
\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/{\bm{k}})\end{subarray}}\log\|s_{x^{\sigma}}\|^{2}.

The right hand side is
dC_{1}\cdot\#\mathcal{Z}_{N}(\mathbb{C})
, and hence

\widehat{\deg}_{N}\big((\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}\big)=C_{1}\cdot\#\mathcal{Z}_{N}(\mathbb{C})=\widehat{\deg}_{N}\big((\mathcal{Z}_{N},g_{N})\cdot(0,C_{1})\big)

up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
.
Varying
N
shows that

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\big)=\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot(0,C_{1})\big)

for every
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})
,
and the claim follows.
∎

5.4.
Another hermitian line bundle

In order to relate the hermitian line bundles of (
5.2.7
), we recall from §5.1 of
[
BHK+20b
]
a fourth hermitian line bundle.
Denote by

H^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(A)=\mathbb{R}^{1}\pi_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}

the first relative algebraic de Rham cohomology of
\pi:A\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
, a rank
2n
vector bundle on
\mathcal{S}_{V}
.
The action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on
A
induces an action on
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A)=H^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(A)^{\vee}
, which is locally free of rank
n
over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
If

H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A)\to\mathcal{V}

denotes the largest quotient on which the action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
is through the structure morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{V}}
, then
\mathcal{V}
is a rank
n
vector bundle on
\mathcal{S}_{V}
, equipped with
a morphism

\det(\mathcal{V})^{-1}\to\bigwedge\nolimits^{n}H^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(A)\to H^{n}_{\mathrm{dR}}(A).

Given a complex point
z\in\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
and a vector

s_{z}\in\det(\mathcal{V}_{z})^{-1},

we view
s_{z}
as an element of
H^{n}(A_{z},\mathbb{C})
, and define

\|s_{z}\|^{2}=\left|\int_{A_{z}(\mathbb{C})}s_{z}\wedge\overline{s}_{z}\right|.

This defines a hermitian metric on
\det(\mathcal{V})^{-1}
, and hence we obtain a hermitian line bundle

(5.4.1)

\det(\mathcal{V})\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V}).

Proposition 5.4.1
.

Assume
n\geq 2
.
Recalling the exceptional divisor of Definition
5.1.6
and the notation (
4.5.6
), we have the equality

2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+2\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+\det(\mathcal{V})+(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},C_{2})

in
\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})
, where

C_{2}=2\log\left(\frac{2e^{\gamma}}{D}\right)+(2-n)\log(2\pi).

In particular,
\det(\mathcal{V})\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})
by (
5.2.7
).

Proof.

This is a restatement of Proposition 5.1.2 of
[
BHK+20b
]
, keeping in mind that the metric on

\det(\operatorname{Lie}(A))^{-1}=\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}

used in [loc. cit.] differs from (
1.1.1
) by a power of
2\pi
.
∎

It is an observation of Gross
[
Gro78
]
that the hermitian line bundle
\det(\mathcal{V})

behaves in the generic fiber, for all arithmetic purposes, like the trivial bundle with a constant metric.
This observation was extended to integral models in
§5.3 of
[
BHK+20b
]
, whose results are the basis of the following proposition.

Proposition 5.4.2
.

The Chern form of
\det(\mathcal{V})
is identically
0
.
If
n>2
then, up to numerical equivalence,

\det(\mathcal{V})=(0,C_{3}),

where

C_{3}=(4-2n)h_{\bm{k}}^{\mathrm{Falt}}+\log(4\pi^{2}D).

Proof.

Fix an integer
N\geq 3
.
As in Theorem 1 of
[
Gro78
]
, the
\mathbb{C}
-vector space
H^{0}(\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)_{/\mathbb{C}},\det(\mathcal{V}))
has dimension
1
, and the norm of any global section is a locally constant function on
\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})
. The vanishing of the Chern form follows.
Moreover, one can choose a nonzero global section
t
defined over a finite Galois extension
{\bm{k}}^{\prime}/{\bm{k}}
,
and then for every
{\bm{k}}
-algebra embedding

\sigma:{\bm{k}}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}
the norm
\|t^{\sigma}\|
must again be locally constant.

If
n>2
then
1
1

1

The assumption
n>2
is mistakenly omitted in Theorem 5.3.1 of
[
BHK+20b
]
, whose proof requires that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
has geometrically normal fibers.
The normality is a theorem of Pappas when
n>2
, but is false when
n=2
.

Theorem 5.3.1 of
[
BHK+20b
]
allows us to choose
t
so that it extends to
a nowhere vanishing section

t\in H^{0}\big(\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{{\bm{k}}^{\prime}}[1/N]},\det(\mathcal{V})\big).

Setting
d=[{\bm{k}}^{\prime}:{\bm{k}}]
and taking the tensor product of all
\operatorname{Gal}({\bm{k}}^{\prime}/{\bm{k}})
-conjugates of
t
, we obtain a section

s\in H^{0}(\mathcal{S}_{V}(N),\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes d})

such that
\mathrm{div}(s)=0
, and such that
-\log\|s\|
is locally constant.
Let

c_{X}=-\log\|s\|^{2}

denote its value on the connected component
X\subset\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)_{/\mathbb{C}}
.

Fix a finite Galois extension
L/{\bm{k}}
contained
\mathbb{C}
large enough that every component

X\subset\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)_{/\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
L
.
We may further enlarge
L
to assume that each
X
admits an
L
-point

x\in X(L)\subset\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)(L)

that extends to

\underline{x}:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[1/N])\to\mathcal{S}_{V}(N).

For example, start by fixing a complex point
x\in X(\mathbb{C})
corresponding to a pair
(A_{0},A)
for which
A
has complex multiplication. Then enlarge
L
so that both
A_{0}
and
A
(along with their level
N
-structures) are defined over
L
and have everywhere good reduction.

Applying Corollary 5.3.2 and Proposition 5.3.3 of
[
BHK+20b
]
to
\underline{x}
, we find

\frac{-1}{[L:{\bm{k}}]}\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/{\bm{k}})}\log\|s_{x^{\sigma}}\|^{2}=dC_{3}

up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
, and hence

\frac{1}{[L:{\bm{k}}]}\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(L/{\bm{k}})}c_{X^{\sigma}}=dC_{3}

up to the same ambiguity.

We have now shown that the average value of
-\log\|s\|^{2}
over any
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb{C}/{\bm{k}})
orbit of connected components in
\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})
is
dC_{3}
,
up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
. The proposition follows from this, by the same argument used in the proof of Proposition
5.3.1
.
∎

5.5.
Comparison of hermitian line bundles

We now come to the main results of Chapter
5
.

The exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
of Definition
5.1.6
is a recurring nuisance,
in part because it has nontrivial arithmetic intersection with
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
.
This will be explored more fully in §
5.6
below.
The following proposition allows us to avoid this nuisance by slightly modifiying
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
, and also clarifies the relation between the second and third line bundles in (
5.2.7
).

Theorem 5.5.1
.

Assume
n\geq 2
.
Recalling the notation (
4.5.6
), the hermitian line bundle

(5.5.1)

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}-(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

enjoys the following properties.

(1)

Every irreducible component
E\subset\mathrm{Exc}_{V}
satisfies

(E,0)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}=0

in
\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{2}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
, as well as the height relation

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(E)=0.

The same equalities hold if we replace
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
with
\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
or
