\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
.

(2)

We have the equality of Chern forms

\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})=2\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})=\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}).

(3)

If
n>2
then, up to numerical equivalence,

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+(0,C_{0}(n))\in\widehat{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V}),

where
C_{0}(n)
is the constant of Theorem
A
. In particular, up to numerical equivalence,

2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},C_{0}(n)).

Proof.

For (1), the key observation is that the abelian scheme
A\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
is a pullback via the vertical arrow on the right in (
5.1.6
).
In particular,
\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
is isomorphic to the pullback of

\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}}\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}).

If
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}
were a scheme we could trivialize this latter line bundle over a Zariski open neighborhood of the (
0
-dimensional) singular locus.
This would pull back to a trivialization of
\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
over an open neighborhood of
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}
, and in particular over an open neighborhood of
any irreducible component
E\subset\mathrm{Exc}_{V}
.

To account for the stackiness, simply fix an integer
N\geq 3

and apply the same reasoning with level
N
-structure to see that

\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}
is trivial in some Zariski open neighborhood of

E(N)=E\times_{\mathcal{S}_{V}}\mathcal{S}_{V}(N).

This implies the arithmetic intersection formula

(E(N),0)\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}=0

and varying
N
proves

(E,0)\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}=0.

The line bundle (
5.4.1
) is also a pullback via the vertical arrow on the right in (
5.1.6
), hence the same argument shows

(E,0)\cdot\det(\mathcal{V})=0.

The proof of Proposition
5.3.1
shows that, for any
N\geq 3
,
there is a positive multiple of

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

that can be represented by a purely archimedean arithmetic divisor
(0,g)
.
Any such arithmetic divisor satisfies

(E(N),0)\cdot(0,g)=0,

and varying
N
shows that

(E,0)\cdot\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}=0.

Rewriting the relation of Proposition
5.4.1
as

(5.5.2)

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+2\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+\det(\mathcal{V})+(0,C_{2}),

we have shown that the right hand side has trivial arithmetic intersection with
(E,0)
, and hence so does the left hand side. This proves the first equality of (1). The second is a formal consequence of this and (
4.5.3
).

The first equality of (2) follows from (
5.5.2
),
as the Chern forms of the final three terms on the right vanish by Lemma
4.5.3
, Proposition
5.3.1
, and Proposition
5.4.2
.
The second equality of (2) is clear from the definitions.

Claim (3) also follows from (
5.5.2
), using Proposition
5.3.1
, Proposition
5.4.2
, and the equality

C_{0}(n)=2C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}
.
∎

Remark 5.5.2
.

If
n>2
then part (3) of Theorem
5.5.1
implies

\displaystyle 2^{n}\cdot\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}\big(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+(0,C_{0}(n))\big)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}\big(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\big)+nC_{0}(n)\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})^{n-1}

where we have used Lemma
4.5.3
for the first equality, and Lemma
4.5.4
for the second.
In Proposition
6.7.1
we will show that this equality also holds when
n=2
.

The final claim of Theorem
5.5.1
has the following variant.

Proposition 5.5.3
.

Fix an
N\geq 3
.
There is a positive integer
r
such that the
r^{\mathrm{th}}
power of
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{\otimes 2}
becomes isomorphic to the
r^{\mathrm{th}}
power of

\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{Exc}_{V})

after pulling both line bundles back via the morphism

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

Proof.

We begin with the equality

2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+2\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}+\det(\mathcal{V})+(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},C_{2})

of metrized line bundles in
\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})
from Proposition
5.4.1
. In particular, after forgetting the metrics we obtain the same equality in
\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})
.

After pullback to
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
, the proof of Proposition
5.3.1
shows that some power of

\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
is trivial. Similarly, the proof of Proposition
5.4.2
shows that some power of
\det(\mathcal{V})
admits a trivializing section
s
over the interior
\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)
such that
\|s\|
is a locally constant function on the complex fiber. This constancy of the norm implies that
s
, when viewed as a rational section on the toroidal compactification
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
, still has no zeros or poles; the argument for this is exactly as in the proof of Proposition 5.2.1 of
[
How20
]
, and relies on the pre-log singularity of the metric on
\det(\mathscr{V})
proved in Proposition
5.4.1
.
Thus some power of
\det(\mathcal{V})
is trivial, and the claim follows.
∎

5.6.
Volume of the exceptional divisor

In this section we assume
n\geq 2
, and fix an irreducible (
=
connected) component
E
of the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
of Definition
5.1.6
.
Recalling the notation (
4.5.6
), our goal is to compute the arithmetic volume of

(E,0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}).

By definition of the exceptional divisor, there is a commutative diagram with cartesian squares

(5.6.1)

\textstyle{{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{e}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Exc}}_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}}}

in which
e
is a connected component of the
0
-dimensional reduced and irreducible
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stack

\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}
. In particular,
e
is supported in a single characteristic
p\mid D
, and admits a presentation as a stack quotient

e\cong\Delta\backslash\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}),

in which
\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
is the prime above
p
,
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
is a finite extension of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/\mathfrak{p}
, and
\Delta
is a finite group acting on
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
.
Define a rational number

m_{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{z\in e(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Aut}(z)|}=\frac{[\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}:\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}]}{|\Delta|}.

Proposition 5.6.1
.

The iterated intersection

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}\cdots\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}\cdot(E,0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

has arithmetic degree
\widehat{m}_{E}=m_{E}\log(p)

Proof.

After Remark
4.2.1
, one might expect that
E
is isomorphic to the projective space of hyperplanes in an
n
-dimensional vector space.
In particular, there should be a universal hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{O}_{E}^{n}
, with the property that the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}
to
E
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{n}/\mathcal{F}
.

The obstruction to this being true is due to stacky issues, which can be removed by fixing an integer
N\geq 3
prime to
p
and adding level
N
-structure to the universal pair
(A_{0},A)
over
e
.
Let
e(N)\to e
be the scheme classifying such level structures, and consider the cartesian diagram
(this is the definition of the upper left corner)

\textstyle{{E(N)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{e(N)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{e.}}

The scheme
e(N)
, being a reduced scheme finite over
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, is a disjoint union of finitely many spectra of finite extensions of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Fix a connected component
E^{\prime}\subset E(N)
, and let

\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})=e^{\prime}\subset e(N)

be the connected component below it.
As in Remark
4.2.1
,
E^{\prime}
is precisely the projective space over
e^{\prime}
classifying hyperplanes
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A|_{e^{\prime}})
.
Moreover, after fixing a trivialization
\operatorname{Lie}(A_{0}|_{e^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
, the isomorphism (
5.2.4
) identifies

\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}|_{E^{\prime}}=\operatorname{Lie}(A|_{E^{\prime}})/\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Pic}(E^{\prime})\cong\operatorname{CH}^{1}(E^{\prime})

where
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A|_{E^{\prime}})
is the universal hyperplane.
A routine exercise then shows that the iterated intersection

(\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}|_{E^{\prime}})\cdots(\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}|_{E^{\prime}})\in\operatorname{CH}^{n-1}(E^{\prime})\cong\mathbb{Z}

is represented by the cycle class of any
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
-valued point of
E^{\prime}
.
In other words, the cycle class of any section to
E^{\prime}\to e^{\prime}
.

Now allow the connected component
E^{\prime}\subset E(N)
to vary. If we fix
any section to
E(N)\to e(N)
, and use it to view
e(N)
as a
0
-cycle on
E(N)
, then

e(N)=(\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}|_{E(N)})\cdots(\mathcal{L}_{V}^{-1}|_{E(N)})\in\operatorname{CH}^{n-1}(E(N)).

This implies the arithmetic intersection formula

(e(N),0)=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}\cdots\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}\cdot(E(N),0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Finally, recalling (
4.4.4
), we deduce

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}\cdots\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}\cdot(E,0)\big)=\frac{\#e(N)(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}{d_{N}}\log(p)=\sum_{z\in e(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}\frac{\log(p)}{|\operatorname{Aut}(z)|}

up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
. Varying
N
completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.6.2
.

The hermitian line bundle
(E,0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})
has arithmetic volume

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(E,0)=(-2)^{n-1}\cdot\widehat{m}_{E}.

Proof.

Part (1) of Theorem
5.5.1
implies the second equality in

(E,0)\cdot(E,0)=(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\cdot(E,0)=2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\cdot(E,0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{2}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

This implies the iterated intersection formula

(E,0)\cdots(E,0)=2^{n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\cdots\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\cdot(E,0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}},

and the claim follows using Proposition
5.6.1
.
∎

Chapter 6
Kudla-Rapoport divisors at split primes

Let
\mathcal{S}_{V}
be the Shimura variety (
5.1.4
) associated to a
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space
V
of signature
(n-1,1)
containing a self-dual lattice.
Throughout this chapter we assume
n>2
.

Fix a prime
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
, and factor

p\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}=\mathfrak{p}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}.

Our goal is to explain how the Kudla-Rapoport divisor
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
of Definition
5.1.7
is related to a Shimura variety
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
with
V^{\flat}
a hermitian space of signature
(n-2,1)
, whose isometry class depends on
p
.
The assumption that
p
is split in
{\bm{k}}
is needed to guarantee that
V^{\flat}
, like the initial choice of
V
, contains a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice. Thus all the properties we have stated for
\mathcal{S}_{V}
also hold for this
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
of one dimension lower. As explained at the end of §
1.2
, this will be crucial for our later inductive arguments.

6.1.
Statement of the results

Let
V^{\flat}
denote the
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space of signature
(n-2,1)
whose local invariants satisfy

(6.1.1)

\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V^{\flat})=(p,-D)_{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V)

for all places
\ell\leq\infty
.
Using standard formulas for the Hilbert symbol, this is equivalent to

\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V^{\flat})=\left(\frac{p}{\ell}\right)\cdot\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V).

Equivalently,
V^{\flat}
is the orthogonal complement to the
{\bm{k}}
-span of any

x\in V
of hermitian norm
\langle x,x\rangle=p
.
Obviously
V^{\flat}
depends on the prime
p
fixed above, but this prime will not change and we suppress the dependence from the notation. Our assumption that
p
is split is essential for the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1.1
.

The hermitian space
V^{\flat}
admits a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice (so is relevant in the sense of Definition
4.1.3
).

Proof.

Suppose
\ell
is a rational prime unramified in
{\bm{k}}
. If
\ell\neq p
then
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}^{\times}
, and hence is a norm from the unramified extension
{\bm{k}}_{\ell}
. If
\ell=p
then
p
is again a norm from
{\bm{k}}_{\ell}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}\times\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. In either case,
(p,-D)_{\ell}=1
, and so the local invariants of
V
and
V^{\flat}
agree at all unramified primes.

In general, a
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space admits a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice if and only if it has local invariant
1
at every prime unramified in
{\bm{k}}
.
As
V
satisfies this condition by hypothesis, so does
V^{\flat}
.
∎

Lemma
6.1.1
allows us to form the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stacks

\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-2,1)}

analogous to

\mathcal{S}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)},

but in one dimension lower. The stack
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
is endowed with its own hermitian line bundle
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}}
as in (
5.2.3
), its own exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V^{\flat}}
as in Definition
5.1.6
, and its own universal pair
(A_{0}^{\flat},A^{\flat})
of polarized abelian schemes with
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-actions.

The following theorems, whose proofs will occupy the remainder of Chapter
6
, lie at the core of the inductive arguments of Chapter
8
. In both theorems,
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p)
is the divisor on
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
of Remark
5.1.8
, and the arithmetic heights and volumes are those of §
4.5
.

Theorem 6.1.2
.

If we set

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}=2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}-(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

as in Theorem
5.5.1
, and similarly with
V
replaced by
V^{\flat}
, then

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}=(p^{n-1}+1)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}).

Moreover, there is a rational number
a(p)
such that

\frac{\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))}{p^{n-1}+1}=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})+a(p)\log(p).

Remark 6.1.3
.

The proof of Theorem
6.1.2
gives no hint as to the value of the rational number
a(p)
.
It will be determined later in Theorem
8.4.2
.

Theorem 6.1.4
.

There is a rational number
b(p)
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))}{p^{n-1}+1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}})+b(p)\log(p)

\displaystyle\quad+(1-n)\left(\frac{L^{\prime}(0,\varepsilon)}{L(0,\varepsilon)}+\frac{\log(D)}{2}\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}).

The proofs are rather long, so we summarize now the key steps.
The central idea is to make precise the impressionistic relation

\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)``="(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}},

by decomposing

(6.1.2)

\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}=\mathcal{U}_{0}\sqcup\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}\sqcup\mathcal{U}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}

as a disjoint union of open and closed substacks (Proposition
6.2.2
below).
The stacks on the right hand side are related to
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
by closed immersions

i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\to\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}},\qquad i_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}:\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\to\mathcal{U}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},

and by a diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}_{0}}}

in which the leftward arrow is a finite étale surjection of degree
p^{n-1}-1
, and the rightward arrow is a closed immersion.
See (
6.5.12
) for the definition of the stack
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
.

Recalling the exceptional locus of Definition
5.1.6
,
denote by

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V}=\mathcal{S}_{V}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Exc}_{V}

the (open) nonexceptional locus of
\mathcal{S}_{V}
, set

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V}(p)=\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)\times_{\mathcal{S}_{V}}\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V},

and make the same definitions with
V
replaced by
V^{\flat}
.
Set

\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{\square}=\mathcal{U}_{\square}\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V}(p)

for
\square\in\{0,\mathfrak{p},\overline{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, and

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V^{\flat}}=\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}.

We will show that the closed immersions
i_{0}
,
i_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
i_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
are close to being isomorphisms. More precisely,
i_{0}
restricts to an isomorphism

i_{0}:\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V^{\flat}}\cong\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{0},

while

i_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
i_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
restrict to isomorphisms

i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\cong\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad i_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}:\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\cong\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}.

After taking compactifications into account, both theorems above will follow easily from these isomorphisms.
Note that it suffices to work only over the nonexceptional locus, as part (1) of Theorem
5.5.1
guarantees that the hermitian line bundles in questions have trivial arithmetic intersection with all components of the exceptional divisor.
This is why we work with
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
in Theorem
6.1.2
instead of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
.

6.2.
Decomposing the Kudla-Rapoport divisor

The decomposition (
6.1.2
) is a geometric reflection of a result of linear algebra.

As in (
5.1.1
), choose an isomorphism

V\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(W_{0},W)

in which
(W_{0},h_{0})
and
(W,h)
are relevant hermitian spaces of signatures
(1,0)
and
(n-1,1)
.
Fix self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices
\mathfrak{a}_{0}\subset W_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}\subset W
.
Any vector

x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})\subset V

with
\langle x,x\rangle=p
determines an orthogonal decomposition

W=\tilde{W}_{0}\oplus W^{\flat}

with
\tilde{W}_{0}=x(W_{0})
, and a corresponding decomposition

V={\bm{k}}x\oplus V^{\flat}

with
V^{\flat}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(W_{0},W^{\flat})
.

Lemma 6.2.1
.

The
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\mathfrak{a}\cap\tilde{W}_{0}

satisfies exactly one of

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=x(\mathfrak{a}_{0}),\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\mathfrak{p}^{-1}x(\mathfrak{a}_{0}),\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}x(\mathfrak{a}_{0}).

Proof.

Use
x
to identify
W_{0}=\tilde{W}_{0}
, and hence
\mathfrak{a}_{0}\subset\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\subset\mathfrak{a}
.
Recalling the symplectic forms

e_{0}:W_{0}\times W_{0}\to\mathbb{Q},\qquad e:W\times W\to\mathbb{Q}

of (
4.1.2
), the relation (
5.1.2
) implies that
e|_{W_{0}}=p\cdot e_{0}
.
The inclusion

e_{0}(p\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0},\mathfrak{a}_{0})=e(\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0},\mathfrak{a}_{0})\subset e(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})=\mathbb{Z},

together with the self-duality of
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
under
e_{0}
,
shows that
p\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{0}
. If equality held we would have

e_{0}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a}_{0})=p^{-1}e(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a}_{0})\subset pe(\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0},\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0})\subset pe(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})\subset p\mathbb{Z},

contradicting
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
being self-dual under
e_{0}
.

As
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable with

\mathfrak{a}_{0}\subset\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\subsetneq p^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, it is

\mathfrak{a}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, or
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{0}
.
∎

Proposition 6.2.2
.

Let
(A_{0},A,x)
be the universal triple over
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
, so that

x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},A)

satisfies
\langle x,x\rangle=p
.
There is a decomposition (
6.1.2
) into open and closed substacks in which

•

\mathcal{U}_{0}
is the locus of points where
\ker(x)=0
,

•

\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the locus of points where
\ker(x)=A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]
,

•

\mathcal{U}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the locus of points where
\ker(x)=A_{0}[\overline{\mathfrak{p}}]
.

Proof.

Recalling (
5.1.3
), the relation
\langle x,x\rangle=p
implies that multiplication-by-
p
factors as

A_{0}\xrightarrow{x}A\cong A^{\vee}\xrightarrow{x^{\vee}}A_{0}^{\vee}\cong A_{0},

and so
\ker(x)\subset A_{0}[p]
. Both of these group schemes are finite étale over
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
, which implies that each of
\mathcal{U}_{0}
,
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
\mathcal{U}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
is open and closed.
It only remains to prove that every geometric point
s\to\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
is contained in one of them.

Abbreviate
T_{0}=T_{p}(A_{0s})
and
T=T_{p}(A_{s})
.
Applying the snake lemma to the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{T_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{s}}
\textstyle{{T_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{s}}
\textstyle{{A_{0s}[p^{\infty}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{s}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{T\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{A_{s}[p^{\infty}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

and using the vertical arrow on the left to identify
T_{0}\subset T
, we find that

\ker(x_{s})\cong\tilde{T}_{0}/T_{0},

where
\tilde{T}_{0}=T\cap T_{0\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

After fixing an isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
of étale sheaves on
s
, there are unique
\mathcal{O}_{{\bm{k}},p}
-valued hermitian forms
h_{0}
and
h
on
T_{0}
and
T
, respectively, related to the Weil pairings
e_{0}
and
e
by (
4.1.2
). Thus we may apply Lemma
6.2.1
with
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}
replaced by
T_{0}
and
T
, to see that
\tilde{T}_{0}
must be one of
T_{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{-1}T_{0}
, or
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}T_{0}
. These three cases correspond to
\ker(x_{s})
being trivial,
A_{0s}[\mathfrak{p}]
, or
A_{0s}[\overline{\mathfrak{p}}]
.
∎

6.3.
Analysis of
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}

In this section we study the structure of the substack
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
of (
6.1.2
),
and make explicit its relation to
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
.
The analogous analysis of
\mathcal{U}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
is obtained by replacing
\mathfrak{p}
by
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
everywhere.

Return to the situation of Lemma
6.2.1
, so that

x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})
with
\langle x,x\rangle=p
determines an orthogonal decomposition

W=\tilde{W}_{0}\oplus W^{\flat}.

Set
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\mathfrak{a}\cap\tilde{W}_{0}
.

Lemma 6.3.1
.

If
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\mathfrak{p}^{-1}x(\mathfrak{a}_{0})
, there is an orthogonal decomposition

\mathfrak{a}=\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}\subset W

in which
\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}=\mathfrak{a}\cap W^{\flat}
.

Proof.

If we use
x
to identify
W_{0}=\tilde{W}_{0}
, the assumption
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{0}
implies that
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
is self-dual with respect to the hermitian form
h|_{\tilde{W}_{0}}=ph_{0}
. The desired decomposition then follows by elementary linear algebra.
∎

The lemma suggests that if
(A_{0},A,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}(S)
for an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme
S
, then
x:A_{0}\to A
should determine an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear splitting

(6.3.1)

A=\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}

of principally polarized abelian schemes. Indeed, this is the case.
If we set

\tilde{A}_{0}=A_{0}/A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]

and recall that
\ker(x)=A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]
, the morphism
x:A_{0}\to A
factors as

A_{0}\to\tilde{A}_{0}\xrightarrow{y}A

for some
y\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(\tilde{A}_{0},A)
satisfying
\langle y,y\rangle=1
.
In other words, the composition

\tilde{A}_{0}\xrightarrow{y}A\cong A^{\vee}\xrightarrow{y^{\vee}}\tilde{A}_{0}^{\vee}\cong\tilde{A}_{0}

is the identity. This implies that the composition

A\cong A^{\vee}\xrightarrow{y^{\vee}}\tilde{A}_{0}^{\vee}\cong\tilde{A}_{0}\xrightarrow{y}A

is a Rosati-fixed idempotent in
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A)
, and
A
admits a unique splitting (
6.3.1
)
of principally polarized abelian schemes over
S
such that this idempotent is the projection to the first factor.

Apply the above construction to the universal triple
(A_{0},A,x)
over
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
recall that
A
comes equipped with an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
satisfying Krämer’s signature condition.
Using the decomposition

\operatorname{Lie}(A)=\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\oplus\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat})

of vector bundles on
S
, denote by

\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\dagger}\subset\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}

the largest closed substack over which
\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\subset\mathcal{F}
.

Proposition 6.3.2
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\cong\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\dagger}.

Proof.

As above, let
S
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme.
Given a point
(A_{0},A,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\dagger}(S)
, the splitting (
6.3.1
) determines

A^{\flat}\in\mathcal{M}_{(n-2,1)}(S),

where we have endowed
A^{\flat}
with the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable hyperplane

\mathcal{F}^{\flat}=\mathcal{F}/\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)/\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\cong\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat}).

satisfying Krämer’s condition.
The pair
(A_{0},A^{\flat})
defines an
S
-point of

\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}_{(n-2,1)},

and we have now constructed a morphism

(6.3.2)

\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{(A_{0},A,x)\to(A_{0},A^{\flat})}\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}.

Conversely, start with an
S
-point

(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat})\in\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(S).

First define elliptic curves
A_{0}=A_{0}^{\flat}
and
\tilde{A}_{0}=A_{0}/A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]
.
Then define an abelian scheme
A
by (
6.3.1
), and endow
A
with its product principal polarization and product
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-action.
Recalling that
A^{\flat}
comes equipped with a hyperplane
\mathcal{F}^{\flat}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat})
satisfying Krämer’s condition, we endow
A
with the hyperplane

\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\oplus\mathcal{F}^{\flat}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A).

It is easy to check that
A
, with its extra data, defines an
S
-point of
\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}
, and that

(A_{0},A)
defines an
S
-point of the open and closed substack

\mathcal{S}_{V}\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}_{(n-2,1)}.

If we define
x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},A)
as the composition

A_{0}\to\tilde{A}_{0}\hookrightarrow\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}=A,

where the first arrow is the quotient map, then
\langle x,x\rangle=p
and
\ker(x)=A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]
.
The triple
(A_{0},A,x)
defines an
S
-point of
\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and the morphism

\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\xrightarrow{(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat})\to(A_{0},A,x)}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{p}}

is inverse to (
6.3.2
).
∎

Proposition
6.3.2
gives us a commutative diagram

(6.3.3)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\big(\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-2,1)}\big)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\big(\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}\big)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}

in which the top horizontal arrow is the composition

\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\xrightarrow{i_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\to\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]},

and the bottom horizontal arrow sends
(A_{0}^{\flat},A^{\flat})\mapsto(A_{0},A)
, where

(6.3.4)

A_{0}=A_{0}^{\flat},\quad\tilde{A}_{0}=A_{0}/A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}],\quad A=\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}.

Both horizontal arrows are finite and unramified.

Proposition 6.3.3
.

The homomorphism

\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]})_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]})_{\mathbb{Q}}

induced by (
6.3.3
) sends

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\mapsto\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}},

where
(A_{0}^{\flat},A^{\flat})
is the universal pair over
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
. The same map also sends

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\mapsto(\mathrm{Exc}_{V^{\flat}},0).

Proof.

It follows from (
6.3.4
) that the top horizontal arrow in (
6.3.3
) sends

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\mapsto\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{\tilde{A}_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}.

As we have inverted
p
on the base, the degree
p
quotient map
A_{0}^{\flat}\to\tilde{A}_{0}
induces an isomorphism on Lie algebras, and hence an isomorphism

\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{\tilde{A}_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}\cong\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}.

This isomorphism does not respect the metrics (
1.1.1
), but one can easily check that

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{\tilde{A}_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}_{0}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+(0,-\log(p))

as elements of
\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]})
. The correction term
(0,-\log(p))
is torsion in the arithmetic Picard group, as

2(0,-\log(p))=(\mathrm{div}(p),-\log(p^{2}))=0,

and the first claim follows.

For the second claim, let
S
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme, fix an
S
-point

(A_{0}^{\flat},A^{\flat})\in\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(S)
, and let
(A_{0},A)\in\mathcal{S}_{V}(S)
be its image under the top horizontal arrow in (
6.3.3
).
Tracing through the construction of
i_{\mathfrak{p}}
in Proposition
6.3.2
, we see that

\operatorname{Lie}(A)/\mathcal{F}\cong\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat})/\mathcal{F}^{\flat}.

It follows immediately from this and (
5.2.4
) that
\mathcal{L}_{V}|_{S}\cong\mathcal{L}_{V^{\flat}}|_{S}
.
At a complex point
s\in S(\mathbb{C})
we have a decomposition

H_{1}(A_{s},\mathbb{Q})=H_{1}(\tilde{A}_{0s},\mathbb{Q})\oplus H_{1}(A_{s}^{\flat},\mathbb{Q}),

which induces an isometric embedding

\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(H_{1}(A_{0s},\mathbb{Q}),H_{1}(A_{s}^{\flat},\mathbb{Q}))\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(H_{1}(A_{0s},\mathbb{Q}),H_{1}(A_{s},\mathbb{Q}))

of
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces. Recalling (
5.2.5
), this isometric embedding restricts to the isomorphism

\mathcal{L}_{V^{\flat},s}\cong\mathcal{L}_{V,s}
just constructed, which therefore preserves the metrics defined by (
5.2.6
).

Finally, we show that under the top horizontal arrow in (
6.3.3
), the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\mathcal{S}_{V}
pulls back to the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V^{\flat}}\subset\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
.
These divisors are, by Definition
5.1.6
, the pullbacks of the singular loci

(6.3.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}

\displaystyle\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}

(6.3.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-2,1)}

\displaystyle\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-2,1)}

of (
4.2.2
) under the vertical arrows in (
6.3.3
), and so it suffices to prove that the first singular locus (
6.3.5
) pulls back to the second singular locus (
6.3.6
) under the bottom horizontal arrow in (
6.3.3
).
Moreover, as each of (
6.3.5
) and (
6.3.6
) is a reduced
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stack of dimension
0
, and as the bottom horizontal arrow in (
6.3.3
) is finite and unramified, it suffices to check this on the level of geometric points.
This is an easy exercise in linear algebra, using the characterization of the singular locus found in
Remark
4.2.1
.
∎

Proposition 6.3.4
.

The nonexceptional locus
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
satisfies

\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\dagger},

and the isomorphism of Proposition
6.3.2
restricts to an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\cong\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{\mathfrak{p}}.

Proof.

Suppose
S
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme,

(A_{0},A,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}(S),

and let
A^{\flat}
be the abelian scheme of (
6.3.1
).
In particular,

\operatorname{Lie}(A)=\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\oplus\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat}).

The natural map

\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Exc}_{(n-1,1)}

endows
A
with a hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
, and results of Krämer, as summarized in Theorem 2.3.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, show that this hyperplane is actually determined by the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-action on
A
using the following recipe:
If we fix a
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}=\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
, and let

\overline{\epsilon}_{S}=\overline{\pi}\otimes 1-1\otimes i_{S}(\overline{\pi})\in\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{S},

where
i_{S}:\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
is the structure map, then

\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{ker}(\overline{\epsilon}_{S}:\operatorname{Lie}(A)\to\operatorname{Lie}(A)).

On the other hand, the action of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
on the Lie algebra of
A_{0}
is through the structure morphism
i_{S}
, and so the same is true of
\tilde{A}_{0}=A_{0}/A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]
.
This implies that
\overline{\epsilon}_{S}
annihilates
\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})
, and hence

\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\subset\mathcal{F}.

Having shown

(A_{0},A,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\dagger}(S),

the first claim of the proposition is proved.
The second claim follows from the first and Proposition
6.3.3
.
∎

Proposition 6.3.5
.

The closed immersion
i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\to\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
of Proposition
6.3.2
extends uniquely to a proper morphism

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\to\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathfrak{p}},

where the codomain is defined as the Zariski closure of

\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}.

or, equivalently, as the normalization of

\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}.

Proof.

For ease of notation, we omit all subscripts
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
in the proof.

As the exceptional locus of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}
does not meet the boundary, it suffices to construct the extension over its complement

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}=\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Exc}_{V^{\flat}}.

Consider the universal pair
(A_{0},A^{\flat})
over
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
, and let

x:A_{0}\to A=\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}

be as in the proof of Proposition
6.3.2
. In other words, the triple
(A_{0},A,x)
over
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
determines the isomorphism

i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\cong\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}.

of Proposition
6.3.4
.

Consider the open immersions

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{compact inclusion}}}{{\subset}}\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-2,1)}.

As in the discussion of §
4.3
, the universal abelian scheme over
\mathcal{M}_{(n-2,1)}
extends uniquely to a semi-abelian scheme over
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-2,1)}
.
Pulling this back via projection to the second factor in the fiber product, and then restricting to
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
, yields a semi-abelian scheme over
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
extending
A^{\flat}
.
Similarly, the elliptic curve
A_{0}
extends to an elliptic curve over
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
, obtained as a pullback from the first factor in the fiber product above.
The quotient
\tilde{A}_{0}=A_{0}/A_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]
therefore also extends to an elliptic curve over
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
.

The product
A=\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}
therefore extends to a semi-abelian scheme over
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}
, and the extension property used in the proof of Lemma
4.3.2
provides us with a morphism

i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}

taking values in the open subscheme

\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\big(\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Sing}_{(n-1,1)}\big)\cong\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\big(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Exc}_{(n-1,1)}\big).

Thus we obtain a morphism

i_{\mathfrak{p}}:\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-1,1)},

taking values in the open and closed substack
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
.

We now have a commutative diagram of solid arrows

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathfrak{p}}}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}^{\mathrm{nexc}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}}}

in which the vertical arrow on the right is integral, and hence affine, by its construction as a normalization; see
[
Sta18
,
Tag 035I
]
.
To complete the proof of the proposition, it suffices to show that there is a unique dotted arrow making the diagram commute.
This immediately reduces to the corresponding claim for affine schemes, in which
we are given homomorphisms of rings

\textstyle{{R^{\flat}}}
\textstyle{{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bar{R}^{\flat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

with
B
integral over
A
, and
\bar{R}^{\flat}\subset R^{\flat}
is an inclusion of normal domains with the same field of fractions.
The image of any
b\in B
under
B\to R^{\flat}
is integral over
\bar{R}^{\flat}
, hence contained in
\bar{R}^{\flat}
.
Thus there is a unique dotted arrow making the diagram commute.
∎

6.4.
Two lemmas on abelian schemes

For use in the next section, we prove two lemmas on abelian schemes.
The first is a criterion for determining when a polarization descends to a quotient.
The second is a criterion for the existence of an extension as a semi-abelian scheme.

Lemma 6.4.1
.

Suppose
f:X\to Y
is an isogeny of abelian schemes over a Noetherian scheme
S
, and
\psi_{X}:X\to X^{\vee}
is a polarization whose degree
d
is invertible in
\mathcal{O}_{S}
. The following are equivalent.

(1)

The kernel of
f
is contained in
\mathrm{ker}(\psi_{X})
, and is totally isotropic with respect to the
Weil pairing

e:\mathrm{ker}(\psi_{X})\times\mathrm{ker}(\psi_{X})\to\mu_{d}.

(2)

There exists a (necessarily unique) polarization
\psi_{Y}:Y\to Y^{\vee}
making the diagram

\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{X^{\vee}}}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{Y}}
\textstyle{{Y^{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\vee}}

commute.

Proof.

This is routine.
See, for example, Proposition 10.4 of
[
Pol03
]
.
∎

Lemma 6.4.2
.

Suppose
S
is a normal Noetherian scheme,
U\subset S
is a dense open subscheme, and

f:X\to Y
is an isogeny of abelian schemes over
U
whose degree
d
is invertible in
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
If
X
extends to a semi-abelian scheme over
S
then so does
Y
, and both extensions are unique.

Proof.

The uniqueness claim follows from Proposition I.2.7 of
[
FC90
]
, so we only need to prove the existence of a semi-abelian extension of
Y
.

Denote by
X^{*}\to S
the semi-abelian extension of
X
. The morphism

f:X\to Y
, as well as the multiplication-by-
d
-maps on
X
and
X^{*}
, are quasi-finite and étale by Lemma 2 of Chapter 7.3 of
[
BLR90
]
. In particular, both the composition

\mathrm{ker}(f)\to X[d]\to U

and the second arrow and the composition are étale morphisms. By
[
Sta18
,
Tag 02GW
]
, the first arrow is also étale.
As the first arrow is a closed immersion, we deduce that
\mathrm{ker}(f)\subset X[d]
is a union of connected components.

We have assumed that
S
is normal, so the same is true of the étale
S
-scheme
X^{*}[d]
.
As the connected components of a normal scheme are the same as its irreducible components, it follows that the Zariski closure of
\mathrm{ker}(f)
in
X^{*}[d]
is an open and closed subscheme
\mathrm{ker}(f)^{*}\subset X^{*}[d]
. In particular
\mathrm{ker}(f)^{*}\to S
is quasi-finite and étale.

By Lemma IV.7.1.2 of
[
MB85
]
, the fppf quotient
Y^{*}=X^{*}/\mathrm{ker}(f)^{*}
provides a semi-abelian extension of
Y
to
S
.
∎

6.5.
Analysis of
\mathcal{U}_{0}

In this section we study the substack
\mathcal{U}_{0}
of (
6.1.2
), and make explicit its relation with
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
.

Return to the situation of Lemma
6.2.1
, so that

x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})
with
\langle x,x\rangle=p
determines an orthogonal decomposition

(6.5.1)

W=\tilde{W}_{0}\oplus W^{\flat}.

Set
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=\mathfrak{a}\cap\tilde{W}_{0}
.

Lemma 6.5.1
.

Assume that
\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}=x(\mathfrak{a}_{0})
.
If we set
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{a}\cap W^{\flat}
and let
\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}\subset W^{\flat}
be its dual lattice with respect to
h|_{W^{\flat}}
, the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice

\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}=\mathfrak{p}\cdot\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}+\mathfrak{b},

is self-dual with respect to
h|_{W^{\flat}}
. Moreover, there are inclusions of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices

(6.5.2)

\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\supset}}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{b}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{2}}}{{\subset}}\mathfrak{a}

in
W
of the indicated indices, and a canonical
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear injection

(6.5.3)

\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}/\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\cong\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathfrak{b}/\mathfrak{b}\xrightarrow{t}\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}/\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we use
x
to identify
W_{0}=\tilde{W}_{0}
, so that
h|_{W_{0}}=ph_{0}
.
The projections to the two factors in (
6.5.1
) induce isomorphisms

\mathfrak{a}/(\mathfrak{a}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{b})\to\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{\vee}/\mathfrak{a}_{0},\qquad\mathfrak{a}/(\mathfrak{a}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{b})\to\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}/\mathfrak{b},

where
\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{\vee}=p^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{0}
is the dual lattice of
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
with respect to
h|_{W_{0}}
.
Composing these isomorphisms yields an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear isomorphism

(6.5.4)

p^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{0}/\mathfrak{a}_{0}\cong\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}/\mathfrak{b}

respecting the
p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-valued hermitian forms induced by
h|_{W_{0}}=ph_{0}
and
h|_{W^{\flat}}
.
It follows that
\mathfrak{p}\cdot(\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}/\mathfrak{b})\subset\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}/\mathfrak{b}

is maximal isotropic with respect to
h|_{W^{\flat}}
, and so
\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}

is self-dual under
h|_{W^{\flat}}
.

Consider the inclusions
\mathfrak{b}\subset\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}\subset\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-modules.
The first is an isomorphism everywhere locally except at
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
, while the second is an isomorphism everywhere locally except at
\mathfrak{p}
.
In particular, the first induces an isomorphism

\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathfrak{b}/\mathfrak{b}\cong\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}/\mathfrak{a}^{\flat}.

Restricting (
6.5.4
) to an isomorphism of
\mathfrak{p}
-torsion submodules defines (
6.5.3
).
The inclusions of (
6.5.2
), with the indicated indices, are clear from what we have said.
∎

Just as Lemma
6.5.1
is more complicated than Lemma
6.3.1
, the analysis of
\mathcal{U}_{0}
is more complicated than that of
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Let
S
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme.
Given Lemma
6.5.1
, one expects that any
S
-point
(A_{0},A,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{0}(S)
should determine a diagram of abelian schemes with
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-actions

(6.5.5)

\textstyle{{\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}}}
\textstyle{{\tilde{A}_{0}\times B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\deg=p}
\scriptstyle{\deg=p^{2}}
\textstyle{{A}}

in which the arrows are
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear isogenies of the indicated degrees.
There should also be a canonical
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear closed immersion

(6.5.6)

\tilde{A}_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]\cong B[\mathfrak{p}]\xrightarrow{t}A^{\flat}[\mathfrak{p}],

and the morphism
x:A_{0}\to A
should factor as

A_{0}\cong\tilde{A}_{0}\hookrightarrow\tilde{A}_{0}\times B\to A.

Moreover, these abelian schemes should come with polarizations with the following properties.
The elliptic curve
\tilde{A}_{0}
is equipped with its unique polarization of degree
p^{2}
,

B
is equipped with a polarization of degree
p^{2}
, and
A^{\flat}
is equipped with a principal polarization.
The pullback of the product polarization on
\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}
via the leftwards arrow in (
6.5.5
) is the product polarization on
\tilde{A}_{0}\times B
, and similarly for the the pullback of the principal polarization on
A
via the rightward arrow.

Here is how to construct the data (
6.5.5
) and (
6.5.6
) from
(A_{0},A,x)
.
As in the proof of Proposition
6.2.2
, at any geometric point
s\to S
we may apply Lemma
6.5.1
with
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}
replaced by the
p
-adic Tate modules of
A_{0s}
and
A_{s}
to obtain
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices

(6.5.7)

T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0s})\oplus T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat})\supset T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0s})\oplus T_{p}(B_{s})\subset T_{p}(A_{s}).

analogous to (
6.5.2
), along with an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear injection

(6.5.8)

\mathfrak{p}^{-1}T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0s})/T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0s})\cong\mathfrak{p}^{-1}T_{p}(B_{s})/T_{p}(B_{s})\xrightarrow{t}\mathfrak{p}^{-1}T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat})/T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat}).

To be clear, we have not yet constructed the abelian schemes
\tilde{A}_{0}
,
A^{\flat}
, and
B
, only lattices in
T_{p}(A_{s})[1/p]
that we choose to call
T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{s})
,
T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat})
, and
T_{p}(B_{s})
.
However, as
p\in\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times}
and both inclusions in (
6.5.7
) have finite index, there are abelian schemes
C
and
D
over
S
with
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-actions and
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear isogenies

\textstyle{{D}}
\textstyle{{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{2}}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{{A}}

of the indicated degrees that identify

(6.5.9)

T_{p}(C_{s})=T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0s})\oplus T_{p}(B_{s}),\qquad T_{p}(D_{s})=T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0s})\oplus T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat}).

The condition that
\langle x,x\rangle=p
implies that

A_{0}\xrightarrow{x}A\cong A^{\vee}\xrightarrow{x^{\vee}}A_{0}^{\vee}\cong A_{0}

is multiplication by
p
, which implies that the composition

A\cong A^{\vee}\xrightarrow{x^{\vee}}A_{0}^{\vee}\cong A_{0}\xrightarrow{x}A

is a Rosati-fixed element
\alpha\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A)[1/p]
such that
\alpha\circ\alpha=p\alpha
.
In particular, we obtain an idempotent

p^{-1}\alpha\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A)[1/p]\cong\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(C)[1/p]\cong\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(D)[1/p],

whose induced actions on
T_{p}(C_{s})[1/p]
and
T_{p}(D_{s})[1/p]
are just the projections to the first summands in (
6.5.9
). It follows that we in fact have

p^{-1}\alpha\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(C)\quad\mbox{and}\quad p^{-1}\alpha\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(D).

These idempotents are the projections to the first factors for unique decompositions

C=\tilde{A}_{0}\times B,\qquad D=\tilde{A}_{0}\times A^{\flat}

of abelian schemes with
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-actions, and the image of
x
under

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},A)[1/p]\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},C)[1/p]\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},\tilde{A}_{0})[1/p]

is an isomorphism
A_{0}\cong\tilde{A}_{0}
.
In particular, we now have
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear isogenies (
6.5.5
)
such that taking
p
-adic Tate modules recovers (
6.5.7
), and (
6.5.8
) induces
the closed immersion (
6.5.6
).

It remains to construct the polarizations on
\tilde{A}_{0}
,
B
, and
A^{\flat}
.
If we pull-back the principal polarization on
A
via
\tilde{A}_{0}\times B\to A
, we obtain a polarization on
\tilde{A}_{0}\times B
of degree
p^{4}
.
As the idempotent

p^{-1}\alpha\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(C)=\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(\tilde{A}_{0}\times B)

constructed above is Rosati-fixed, this pullback polarization splits as the product of polarizations

(6.5.10)

\tilde{A}_{0}\to\tilde{A}_{0}^{\vee}\quad\mbox{and}\quad B\to B^{\vee}.

We claim that the kernels of both polarizations in (
6.5.10
) are isomorphic, étale locally on
S
, to the constant
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-module scheme
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/(p)
. It suffices to check this at a fixed geometric point
s\to S
, where the inclusions of
p
-adic Tate modules (
6.5.7
) behave in exactly the same way as the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices in (
6.5.2
).
For example, in the proof of Lemma
6.5.1
we saw that there
are canonical isomorphisms

\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}/\mathfrak{b}\cong\mathfrak{a}/(\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{b})\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}^{\vee}/\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}\cong p^{-1}\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}/\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}

(recall that in the proof of the lemma we identified
\mathfrak{a}_{0}=x(\mathfrak{a}_{0})=\tilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}
),
and hence there are analogous isomorphisms

\frac{T_{p}(B_{s}^{\vee})}{T_{p}(B_{s})}\cong\frac{T_{p}(A_{s})}{T_{p}(\tilde{A}_{0,s})\times T_{p}(B_{s})}\cong\frac{T_{p}(A_{0_{s}}^{\vee})}{T_{p}(A_{0,s})}\cong\frac{p^{-1}T_{p}(A_{0,s})}{T_{p}(A_{0,s})}.

The rightmost quotient is obviously isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/(p)
, and hence the fibers at
s
of the kernels of (
6.5.10
) are both isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/(p)
.

Next we claim that we have equalities

\ker(B\to A^{\flat})=\mathfrak{p}\cdot\ker(B\to B^{\vee})=\ker(B\to B^{\vee})[\overline{\mathfrak{p}}]

of subgroup schemes of
\ker(B\to B^{\vee})
, all maximal isotropic under the Weil pairing.
The second equality is clear, as we have just shown that
\ker(B\to B^{\vee})
is étale locally isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/(p)
.
As above, the first equality and the maximal isotropy can be checked on the level of
p
-adic Tate modules at a geometric point
s\to S
, where they follow from the fact that our abelian schemes were constructed so that their
p
-adic Tate modules have the same linear-algebraic properties as the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices in
Lemma
6.5.1
. More precisely, by construction the lattice

T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat})=\mathfrak{p}T_{p}(B_{s}^{\vee})+T_{p}(B_{s})

is self-dual under the Weil pairing on
T_{p}(B_{s})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and it follows that

\ker(B_{s}\to A_{s}^{\flat})=\frac{T_{p}(A_{s}^{\flat})}{T_{p}(B_{s})}=\mathfrak{p}\cdot\frac{T_{p}(B_{s}^{\vee})}{T_{p}(B_{s})}=\mathfrak{p}\cdot\ker(B_{s}\to B_{s}^{\vee})

is a maximal isotropic submodule of
\ker(B_{s}\to B_{s}^{\vee})=T_{p}(B_{s}^{\vee})/T_{p}(B_{s})
.

By Lemma
6.4.1
, the maximal isotropy of the subgroup scheme

\ker(B\to A^{\flat})\subset\ker(B\to B^{\vee})

under the Weil pairing implies that the polarization

B\to B^{\vee}
descends to a principal polarization on
A^{\flat}
.
This completes the construction of the abelian schemes (
6.5.5
) with all of their expected extra structure.

Now apply this construction to the universal triple
(A_{0},A,x)
over
\mathcal{U}_{0}
.
As
p\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{0}}^{\times}
, the
p
-power isogenies (
6.5.5
) induce an isomorphism

(6.5.11)

\operatorname{Lie}(A)\cong\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\times\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat})

of rank
n
vector bundles on
\mathcal{U}_{0}
.
Recalling that
A
comes equipped with an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-stable hyperplane
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
, denote by

\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}\subset\mathcal{U}_{0}

the largest closed substack over which
\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\subset\mathcal{F}

Define a finite étale cover

(6.5.12)

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\to\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}

of degree
p^{n-1}-1
as follows:
for any
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme
S
, let
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(S)
be the groupoid of triples
(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat},t)
in which

(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat})\in\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(S)\quad\mbox{and}\quad t:A^{\flat}_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]\to A^{\flat}[\mathfrak{p}]

is an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear closed immersion of group schemes over
S
.

Proposition 6.5.2
.

There is a canonical isomorphism
i_{0}:\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\cong\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

The morphism
\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
is essentially described above.
Suppose
S
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-scheme and

(A_{0},A,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}(S).

We can use the isomorphism (
6.5.11
) to endow the abelian scheme

A^{\flat}
of (
6.5.5
) with the rank one local direct summand

\mathcal{F}^{\flat}=\mathcal{F}/\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)/\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})=\operatorname{Lie}(A^{\flat})

to obtain a point
A^{\flat}\in\mathcal{M}_{(n-2,1)}(S)
. Setting
A_{0}^{\flat}=\tilde{A}_{0}
,
this defines a morphism

\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{(A_{0},A,x)\mapsto(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat})}\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]},

and the closed immersion (
6.5.6
) defines the desired lift to

\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
.

Now we construct the inverse.
Start with a point

(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat},t)\in\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(S).

Denote by
B
the abelian scheme dual to

B^{\vee}=A^{\flat}/\mathrm{Im}(t).

Using the principal polarization to identify
A^{\flat}
with its dual, the quotient map
A^{\flat}\to B^{\vee}
dualizes to a morphism
B\to A^{\flat}
, and the composition

B\to A^{\flat}\to B^{\vee}

is a degree
p^{2}
polarization (it is the pullback via
B\to A^{\flat}
of the principal polarization on
A^{\flat}
).
It has the property that each factor on the right hand side of

\ker(B\to B^{\vee})=\ker(B\to B^{\vee})[\mathfrak{p}]\times\ker(B\to B^{\vee})[\overline{\mathfrak{p}}]

has order
p
, and the second factor is the kernel of
B\to A^{\flat}
. In particular, the induced map

B[\mathfrak{p}]\to A^{\flat}[\mathfrak{p}]
is an isomorphism, and the closed immersion

A^{\flat}_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]\xrightarrow{t}A^{\flat}[\mathfrak{p}]\cong B[\mathfrak{p}]

has image
\ker(B\to B^{\vee})[\mathfrak{p}]
.
Setting
\tilde{A}_{0}=A^{\flat}_{0}
, the resulting isomorphism

\tilde{A}_{0}[\mathfrak{p}]\cong\ker(B\to B^{\vee})[\mathfrak{p}]

admits a unique
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-linear extension to an isomorphism

t:\tilde{A}_{0}[p]\cong\ker(B\to B^{\vee})

identifying the Weil pairings on source and target.

The antidiagonal subgroup

\Delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\tilde{A}_{0}[p]\xrightarrow{a_{0}\mapsto(-a_{0},t(a_{0}))}\tilde{A}_{0}\times B.

is totally isotropic under the Weil pairing induced by the product polarization (where
\tilde{A}_{0}
is given the polarization of degree
p^{2}
), which therefore descends, by Lemma
6.4.1
, to a principal polarization on the quotient

A=(\tilde{A}_{0}\times B)/\Delta.

As
p\in\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times}
, there is an induced isomorphism of Lie algebras (
6.5.11
),
which allows us to define a corank one local direct summand
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(A)
as the product

\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{A}_{0})\times\mathcal{F}^{\flat}.

This defines a point
A\in\mathcal{M}_{(n-1,1)}(S)
. Setting
A_{0}=\tilde{A}_{0}
, the composition

A_{0}=\tilde{A}_{0}\hookrightarrow\tilde{A}_{0}\times B\to A

defines
x\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0},A)
with
\langle x,x\rangle=p

The above construction determines a morphism

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\xrightarrow{(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat},t)\to(A_{0},A,x)}\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)

taking values in the open substack
\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}
, which is inverse to the map
\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
constructed above.
∎

Proposition
6.5.2
gives us morphisms

(6.5.13)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
6.5.12
)
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}

in which the arrow on the right is the (finite and unramified) composition

{\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\to\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]},

sending
(A_{0}^{\flat},A^{\flat},t)\mapsto(A_{0},A)
, where
A_{0}=A_{0}^{\flat}
, and
A
is related to
A^{\flat}
by a diagram (
6.5.5
).

Proposition 6.5.3
.

The hermitian line bundles

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}})

and

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})

have the same images under the homomorphisms

\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}})_{\mathbb{Q}}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

induced by (
6.5.13
).
The same is true of

(\mathrm{Exc}_{V^{\flat}},0)
and
(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)
, and of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
.

Proof.

Denote by
(A^{\flat}_{0},A^{\flat})
the pullback of the universal object via the left arrow in (
6.5.13
), and by
(A_{0},A)
the pullback of the universal object via the right arrow in (
6.5.13
).
The isomorphism of Proposition
6.5.2
, and hence the right arrow in (
6.5.13
), is constructed in such a way that these pullbacks are related by isogenies (
6.5.5
) of abelian schemes over
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
. In particular, there is a quasi-isogeny

f\in\operatorname{Hom}(A^{\flat}_{0}\times A^{\flat},A)[1/p]

of degree
\deg(f)=p
.
As in the proof of Proposition
6.3.3
, this implies that

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}_{0}/\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}}+(0,-\log(p))

as elements of
\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}})
, and the term
(0,-\log(p))
is torsion.
The first claim follows from this.
The remaining claims are essentially the same as in Proposition
6.3.3
, and we leave the details to the reader.
∎

Proposition 6.5.4
.

The nonexceptional locus
\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{0}
satisfies

\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\mathrm{nexc}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{0}^{\dagger}.

Moreover, the morphism of Proposition
6.5.2
restricts to an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\cong\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{nexc}}_{0}.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as Proposition
6.3.4
, and the details are left to the reader.
∎

Proposition 6.5.5
.

Denote by
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}
the normalization of
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
.
The closed immersion
i_{0}:\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\to\mathcal{U}_{0}
of Proposition
6.5.2
extends to a proper morphism

\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}\to\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{0},

where the codomain is defined as the Zariski closure of

\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]},

or, equivalently, as the normalization of

\mathcal{U}_{0}\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
.

Proof.

Consider the universal triple
(A_{0},A^{\flat},t)
over
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
, and let
(A_{0},A)
be the pullback of the universal pair over
\mathcal{S}_{V}
via the composition

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}\mathcal{U}_{0}\to\mathcal{S}_{V}.

As in the proof of Proposition
6.3.5
,
we know that
A^{\flat}
extends to a semi-abelian scheme over
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}
, obtained as a pullback via

\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{(n-2,1)},

and that
A_{0}
extends to an elliptic curve over
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}
, obtained as a pullback via

\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}\to\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)},

Using Lemma
6.4.2
and the isogenies (
6.5.5
), we deduce that
A
also extends to a semi-abelian scheme over
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}
. With this extension in hand, the proof is essentially identical to that of Proposition
6.3.5
.
∎

6.6.
Proofs of Theorems 6.1.2 and 6.1.4

In this section we prove Theorems
6.1.2
and
6.1.4
.
Define an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]
-stack

\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)=\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}.

Propositions
6.3.2
and
6.5.2
provide us with a canonical closed immersion

\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)\xrightarrow{i=i_{0}\sqcup i_{\mathfrak{p}}\sqcup i_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}}\mathcal{U}_{0}\sqcup\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}\sqcup\mathcal{U}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}=\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}

with image the closed substack
\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{0}\sqcup\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{p}}\sqcup\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Hence we have a diagram

(6.6.1)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}

in which
\alpha
is a finite étale surjection of degree
p^{n-1}+1
, and
\beta
is finite.
The following lemma shows that
i
is very close to being an isomorphism.

Lemma 6.6.1
.

As divisors on
\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
, we have

\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}=\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)+E,

where
\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)
has no irreducible components contained in the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\subset\mathcal{S}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
of Definition
5.1.6
, and
E
is supported entirely on the exceptional divisor.

Proof.

The
exceptional locus
of
\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)
is the preimage of the exceptional divisor under
\alpha
, and an irreducible component of it is
exceptional
if it is contained in the exceptional locus.
Similarly, the
exceptional locus
of
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
is the preimage of the exceptional divisor under
\beta
, and an irreducible component of it is
exceptional
if it is contained in the exceptional locus.

The final claims of Propositions
6.3.4
and
6.5.4
show that
i
restricts to an isomorphism between the non-exceptional loci of the source and target. In particular, it establishes a bijection between the generic points of non-exceptional irreducible components, and corresponding non-exceptional generic have local rings of the same (finite) length.

However, the morphism
\alpha
is finite étale, so no irreducible component of the source can map into the exceptional divisor of the target.
Thus the closed immersion
i
actually establishes a bijection between irreducible components of the source and non-exceptional irreducible components of the target, in a way that matches up their multiplicities. The claim follows immediately.
∎

We want to construct a compactification of
\mathcal{Z}_{V}^{\dagger}(p)
with sufficiently nice properties that we can form its pre-log-log arithmetic Chow groups as in §
4.4
. To this end, we define

(6.6.2)

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)=\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]},

where
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}
was defined in Proposition
6.5.5
.
Equivalently,
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)
is the normalization of

\alpha:\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}.

Propositions
6.3.5
and
6.5.5
imply that the diagram (
6.6.1
) extends to

(6.6.3)

\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}}}
\textstyle{{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}},}

For any
N\geq 1
prime to
p
, we can add level structure to
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}
by defining

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(N)=\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(N),

and letting
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
be the normalization of

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}.

Equivalently, this is the normalization of

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]}.

Lemma 6.6.2
.

The stack
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
is regular, flat, and proper over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]
, and is a projective scheme if
N\geq 3
. It is smooth in a neighborhood of its boundary

\partial\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)=\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\smallsetminus\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(N),

which is a Cartier divisor smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
4.3.3
, and is again based on the results of
[
Lan16
]
and
[
MP19
]
.

Recalling from Remark
5.1.3
that the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}
is the Shimura variety associated to a reductive group
G^{\flat}
and a certain compact open subgroup of
G^{\flat}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, one can easily check that the generic fiber of the finite étale cover

(6.6.4)

\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\to\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]}

is obtained by to passing to two smaller compact open subgroups.

Recall from §
5.1
that
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
is constructed as an open and closed substack

\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\subset\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}\mathcal{M}_{W^{\flat}}(N).

In this construction, we may replace
\mathcal{M}_{W^{\flat}}(N)
with the stack
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W^{\flat}}(N)
appearing in the proof of Proposition
4.3.3
to obtain a blow-down

\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\subset\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}(N)\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W^{\flat}}(N).

Repeating the construction of (
6.6.4
) with
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
replaced by
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
yields a finite étale cover

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]},

and
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
is the blow-up of the normal stack
\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
along its proper and
0
-dimensional locus of nonsmooth points.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.3.3
, we now have morphisms

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]}\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]},

and we may define
\bar{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
as the normalization of the composition.
We may now apply the results of
[
Lan16
]
and
[
MP19
]
to deduce properties of
\bar{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
from properties of its interior.
Arguing exactly as in the proof of Proposition
4.3.3
, this compactification is normal and flat, and is a projective scheme if
N\geq 3
.
Its boundary is a smooth Cartier divisor.
The non-smooth locus has dimension
0
, and does not meet the boundary. As
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
can be identified with the blow-up of
\bar{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)
along its nonsmooth locus, it has all the same properties, and is also regular (being smooth near the boundary and regular in the interior).
∎

Lemma
6.6.2
and Remark
5.1.5
provide us with an
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]
-stack

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p,N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]}\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}}(N)_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[\frac{1}{Np}]},

with all the nice properties enumerated in Proposition
4.3.3
. Thus (
6.6.2
) has its own theory of pre-log singular hermitian line bundles and Burgos-Kramer-Kühn arithmetic Chow groups

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})=\varprojlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\geq 3\\
p\nmid N\end{subarray}}\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p,N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

exactly as in §
4.4
. These Chow groups include notions of arithmetic heights and volumes as in §
4.5
, taking values in the abelian group
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Q}\log(p)
.

One can repeat the construction of the diagram (
6.6.3
) with level structures, and so obtain pullbacks

\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{*}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{d}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\beta\circ i)^{*}}

and

(6.6.5)

\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{*}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\beta\circ i)^{*}}

Lemma 6.6.3
.

If two pre-log singular hermitian line bundles

\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{\mathcal{P}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

have the same pullback to
\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
then

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}})^{n-2}=(p^{n-1}+1)\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat})^{n-2}.

Moreover, there is an
a(p)\in\mathbb{Q}
such that

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))=(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat})+\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(E)+a(p)\log(p),

where
E
is the divisor of Lemma
6.6.1
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
6.6.1
, the closed immersion
i
in (
6.6.1
)
restricts to an isomorphism of non-exceptional loci, and hence induces an isomorphism in the generic fiber.
Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\beta^{*}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}})^{n-2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}(\beta\circ i)^{*}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}})^{n-2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\alpha^{*}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat})^{n-2}

\displaystyle=(p^{n-1}+1)\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat})^{n-2}.

The second claim follows from Lemma
6.6.1
and the equalities

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p))=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}((\beta\circ i)^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{P}})=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\alpha^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat})=(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat})

in
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Q}\log(p)
, where the first and last equalities are obtained directly by unpacking the definitions of pullbacks, heights, and arithmetic intersections in
[
BGKK07
]
, and using the fact that
\alpha
is (away from the boundary) a finite étale surjection of degree
p^{n-1}+1
.
∎

Proof of Theorems
6.1.2
and
6.1.4
.

The hermitian line bundles

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\subset\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})

and

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\flat}},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\subset\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}})

of Theorem
5.5.1
have the same images, at least up to torsion, under the pullback maps

\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{*}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p))}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{S}_{V})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\beta\circ i)^{*}}

induced by (
6.6.1
).
Indeed, it suffices to verify this on each of the open and closed substacks in the decomposition

\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p)=\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}.

On the substack
\mathcal{T}_{V^{\flat}}
this is the content of Proposition
6.5.3
. On the substacks
\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/p]}
this is the content of
Proposition
6.3.3
.

It now follows from the injectivity of

\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{\dagger}_{V}(p),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\dagger}_{V}(p))

that these hermitian line bundles also have the same image, up to torsion, under the maps of (
6.6.5
).
In other words, the hypotheses of Lemma
6.6.3
are satisfied by
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
, proving the equality

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}=(p^{n-1}+1)\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})^{n-2},

and the equality

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))=(p^{n-1}+1)\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})+\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(E)

up to a rational multiple of
\log(p)
