. The second term on the right vanishes by claim (1) of Theorem
5.5.1
, completing the proof of Theorem
6.1.2
.

Similarly, Propositions
6.3.3
and
6.5.3
show that the hypotheses of Lemma
6.6.3
are satisfied by

\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\flat}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{\mathcal{P}}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}},

and so

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))=(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}})+\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}}(E)

up to a rational multiple of
\log(p)
. Once again, the second term on the right vanishes by claim (1) of Theorem
5.5.1
.
For the first term on the right, Proposition
5.3.1
and Lemmas
4.5.3
and
4.5.4
imply

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A_{0}^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}}+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}((0,C_{1})+\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}})+(n-1)C_{1}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\flat}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\flat}}})^{n-2},

where

C_{1}=\log(2\pi)+2h^{\mathrm{Falt}}_{\bm{k}}=-\frac{L^{\prime}(0,\varepsilon)}{L(0,\varepsilon)}-\frac{\log(D)}{2}.

This proves Theorem
6.1.4
.
∎

6.7.
Comparing volumes

Recall from Remark
5.5.2
that when
V
has signature
(n-1,1)
with
n>2
(an assumption in force throughout Chapter
6
) there is an explicit relation between the arithmetic volumes of
\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
and

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
.

The restriction to
n>2
, which originated in Proposition
5.4.2
, can now be removed using
Theorems
6.1.2
and
6.1.4
.

Proposition 6.7.1
.

The arithmetic volume relation

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})+nC_{0}(n)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}),

known by Remark
5.5.2
when
n>2
, also holds for
n=2
.
Here
C_{0}(n)
is the constant of Theorem
A
.

Proof.

Assume that
V
has signature
(1,1)
, and fix a prime
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
.

Let
V^{\sharp}
be the
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space of signature
(2,1)
whose local invariants satisfy

\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V)=(p,-D)_{\ell}\cdot\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V^{\sharp})

for all places
\ell\leq\infty
, so that the discussion of §
6.1
applies with the pair
(V^{\sharp},V)
replacing
(V,V^{\flat})
. Define
H_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
by the relation

(p+1)H_{p}=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\sharp}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p))-\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p)).

On the one hand, part (3) of Theorem
5.5.1
and Lemma
4.5.3
imply

(6.7.1)

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\sharp}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p))=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}+(0,C_{0}(3))}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p)).

In the codimension
2
arithmetic Chow group of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V^{\sharp}}
we have the relation

\Big(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}+(0,C_{0}(3))\Big)^{2}=\Big(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}\Big)^{2}+\Big(0,2C_{0}(3)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}})\Big),

as in the proof of Lemma
4.5.4
.
Directly from the definition of arithmetic height in §
4.5
, it follows that the right hand side of (
6.7.1
) is equal to

\displaystyle\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p))+2C_{0}(3)\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V^{\sharp}}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}).

Combining the first claim of Theorem
6.1.2
with the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
, we find

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V^{\sharp}}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}})=(p+1)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}).

Putting all of this together allows us to rewrite (
6.7.1
) as

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\sharp}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p))=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A^{\sharp}/\mathcal{S}_{V^{\sharp}}}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V^{\sharp}}(p))+2C_{0}(3)(p+1)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}),

which is equivalent to

(6.7.2)

H_{p}=2C_{0}(3)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}).

On the other hand, the height formulas of Theorems
6.1.2
and
6.1.4
imply

\displaystyle H_{p}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})-\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})+2\left(\frac{L^{\prime}(0,\varepsilon)}{L(0,\varepsilon)}+\frac{\log(D)}{2}\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})-\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})+2(C_{0}(3)-C_{0}(2))\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})

up to a rational multiple of
\log(p)
, and combining this with (
6.7.2
) proves

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})-\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})-2C_{0}(2)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})\in\mathbb{Q}\log(p).

The only way this can hold for all primes
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
is if this real number is
0
, completing the proof when
n=2
.
∎

Chapter 7
The generating series of arithmetic divisors

We continue to work with the Shimura variety
\mathcal{S}_{V}
of (
5.1.4
)
associated to a
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space
V
of signature
(n-1,1)
containing a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice.
Throughout this chapter we assume
n>2
.

After explaining the connection between the complex orbifold
\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
and the Shimura variety (
3.1.3
) associated to the unitary group
\operatorname{U}(V)
, we will use the results of §
3.3
to construct Green functions for the Kudla-Rapoport divisors
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
. We then recall from
[
BHK+20a
]
the modularity of a generating series of arithmetic divisors, in a slightly more refined form than it is stated there.

7.1.
Arithmetic Kudla-Rapoport divisors

We want to construct Green functions for the special divisors
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
using the machinery of regularized theta lifts from §
3.3
. In order to do this, we must make explicit the relation between the complex orbifold
\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
and the Shimura variety (
3.1.3
).

Recall from §
5.1
that in the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{V}
we fixed an isomorphism

V\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(W_{0},W),

where
W_{0}
and
W
are
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian spaces of signatures
(1,0)
and
(n-1,1)
, respectively.
The subgroup
G\subset\mathrm{GU}(W_{0})\times\mathrm{GU}(W)
of Remark
5.1.3
acts on both
W_{0}
and
W
via unitary similitudes. The group
G
also acts on
V
, defining a surjective homomorphism

G\to H=\operatorname{U}(V),

whose kernel is the central
\mathrm{Res}_{{\bm{k}}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
in
G
.

Fix self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattices

\mathfrak{a}_{0}\subset W_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}\subset W
as in §
4.1
, and define a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice

L=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})\subset V.

Denote by
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
the largest compact open subgroup fixing the lattices
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{a}
.
By abuse of notation, denote in the same way the image of
K
under
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
We now have a commutative diagram

(7.1.1)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
3.1.3
)
\textstyle{{G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{D}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{D}\times H(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K},}

in which the identification on the left is that of §2 of
[
BHK+20a
]
.

Let
H^{\infty}_{2-n}(D,\varepsilon^{n})
denote the space of
\mathbb{C}
-valued harmonic
Maass forms
f
of weight
2-n
, level
\Gamma_{0}(D)
, and character
\varepsilon^{n}
such that

•

f
is bounded at all cusps of
\Gamma_{0}(D)
different from the cusp
\infty
,

•

f
has polynomial growth at
\infty
, in sense that there is a

P_{f}=\sum_{m\leq 0}c^{+}(m)q^{m}\in\mathbb{C}[q^{-1}]

such that
f-P_{f}=o(1)
as
q
goes to
0
.

Such a harmonic Maass form has a Fourier expansion analogous to (
3.3.1
) with Fourier coefficients
c^{\pm}(m)\in\mathbb{C}
.

Fix an
f\in H_{2-n}^{\infty}(D,\varepsilon^{n})
with Fourier coefficients
c^{\pm}(m)
.
This form can be lifted to a vector valued harmonic Maass form, in the sense of §
3.3
, by setting

(7.1.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(D)\backslash\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}(f|_{2-n}\gamma)(\omega_{L}(\gamma)^{-1}\varphi_{0})\in H_{2-n}(\omega_{L}),

where
\varphi_{0}\in S_{L}=\mathbb{C}[L^{\prime}/L]
is the characteristic function of
0\in L^{\prime}/L
.
We denote the Fourier coefficients of
\tilde{f}
by
\tilde{c}^{\pm}(m,\mu)
for
\mu\in L^{\prime}/L
and
m\in\mathbb{Q}
.
The coefficients of
\tilde{f}
can be computed in terms of the coefficients of
f
, and for
m<0
we have

(7.1.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{+}(m,\mu)=\begin{cases}c^{+}(m)&\text{if $\mu=0$}\\
0&\text{if $\mu\neq 0$}\end{cases}

as in Proposition 6.1.2 of
[
BHK+20a
]
or §5 of
[
Sch09
]
.

Under the covering map (
7.1.1
), the divisors
Z(m)
and the hermitian line bundle
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
defined in §
3.1
pull pack to the divisors
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
defined in §
5.1
.
This allows us to apply the construction (
3.3.5
) to the vector valued form (
7.1.2
) to obtain a Green function

\Phi(z,h,\tilde{f})
for the analytic divisor

\sum_{m>0}c^{+}(-m)Z(m)\in\mathrm{Div}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D}))

of (
3.3.3
), and then pull it back to a Green function
\Phi_{V}(f)
for the divisor

\sum_{m>0}c^{+}(-m)\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)\in\mathrm{Div}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{S}_{V}).

We will apply this construction for particular choices of
f
.
As in §7.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, for every
m>0
there is a unique
f_{m}(\tau)\in H^{\infty}_{2-n}(D,\varepsilon^{n})
with
q
-expansion

f_{m}(\tau)=q^{-m}+O(1).

For any
f\in H_{2-n}^{\infty}(D,\varepsilon^{n})
with Fourier coefficients
c^{\pm}(m)
, we then have

f(\tau)=\sum_{m>0}c^{+}(-m)f_{m}(\tau).

Applying the above constructions to these particular harmonic forms yield Green functions

\Phi_{V}(m)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Phi_{V}(f_{m})

for the divisors
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
on
\mathcal{S}_{V}
.

Recall from Remark
5.1.8
that
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
is the Zariski closure of
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)
in the toroidal compactification
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
of §
5.1
.
The following proposition shows that one can add boundary components to
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
in order to obtain a class in the arithmetic Chow group (§
4.4
) of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
.
We will see in Theorem
7.3.1
below that these boundary components are (for our purposes) negligible.

Proposition 7.1.1
.

Fix an integer
m>0
, and recall the divisor
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
on
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
from Remark
5.1.8
.
There is a unique divisor
\mathcal{B}_{V}(m)
on
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
that is supported on the boundary and satisfies

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)+\mathcal{B}_{V}(m),\Phi_{V}(m))\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

Proof.

This follows from the analysis of
\Phi_{V}(m)
near the boundary of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
carried out in
[
BHY15
]
;
see Theorem 7.2.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
.
A precise description of the boundary divisor
\mathcal{B}_{V}(m)
is given by (5.3.3) of
[
BHK+20a
]
.
∎

Remark 7.1.2
.

As we assume
n>2
, Theorem
3.4.2
and (
7.1.3
) imply the integral formula

\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-1}=\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})B^{\prime}(m,0,s_{0}),

where
B^{\prime}(m,0,s_{0})
is the derivative at
s_{0}=(n-1)/2
of the Eisenstein series coefficient of (
2.5.2
), at
\mu=0
, determined by the self-dual lattice
L\subset V
.

7.2.
The modularity theorem

Proposition
7.1.1
defines arithmetic divisors

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

for all
m>0
. We extend the definition to
m=0
by setting

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(0)=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}+(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},-\log(D)),

where the second term is the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
of Definition
5.1.6
endowed with the constant Green function
-\log(D)
.
The following is the main result of
[
BHK+20a
]
.

Theorem 7.2.1

(Theorem 7.3.1 of
[
BHK+20a
]
)
.

The generating series

(7.2.1)

\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot q^{m}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})[[q]]

is the
q
-expansion of a modular form in
M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})
.

A close examination of the proof of Theorem
7.2.1
reveals more information
about the modular form (
7.2.1
). It admits a natural decomposition

(7.2.2)

\widehat{\phi}_{V}=\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}+\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}+\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}

as a sum of three forms. This decomposition will play an important role in §
8.4
, and so the remainder of this section is devoted to describing the three terms on the right.

Recall from Proposition
2.7.1
the Eisenstein series

E_{r}(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}e_{r}(m)q^{m}\in M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

indexed by a positive divisor
r\mid D
.
In Proposition
2.7.3
we defined, for every divisor
r\mid D
, a constant

\gamma_{r}\in\{\pm 1,\pm i\}
. Using these, define the
Eisenstein part
of (
7.2.1
) by

\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)=\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)\cdot\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(0)-\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)+\sum_{\ell\mid r}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{l}}},0)\right),

where
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{l}}}
is the special fiber of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
at the unique prime
\mathfrak{l}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
above
\ell\mid r
.
We may view
\ell\in{\bm{k}}^{\times}
as a rational section of the trivial hermitian line bundle on
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
, and doing so shows that the class

(\mathrm{div}(\ell),-\log|\ell|^{2})\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{V})_{\mathbb{Q}}

is trivial; here we are using the notation (
4.5.6
).
This implies
(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{l}}},0)=(0,\log(\ell))
in the arithmetic Chow group, and hence

(7.2.3)

\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)=\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{-1}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},-2\log(D/r)\big)\right).

The
exceptional part
of (
7.2.1
) is

(7.2.4)

\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}(\tau)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{E\subset\mathrm{Exc}_{V}}\vartheta_{E}(\tau)\cdot(E,0),

where the sum extends over the irreducible components
E\subset\mathrm{Exc}_{V}
of the exceptional divisor on
\mathcal{S}_{V}
, and the scalar valued theta series
\vartheta_{E}(\tau)
counts vectors in a positive definite rank
n
hermitian lattice determined by the component
E
. More precisely, recall that the top horizontal arrow in (
5.6.1
) collapses
E
to a connected
0
-dimensional closed substack

e\subset\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{(n-1,1)}.

The hermitian lattice associated to
E
is then defined by

L_{E}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}(A_{0,s},A_{s})

for any geometric point
s\to e
, as in §2.6 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, and the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient in the
q
-expansion of
\vartheta_{E}(\tau)
is the number of vector in
L_{E}
of hermitian norm
m
.

Having defined all but one of the four modular forms appearing in (
7.2.2
), we complete the definitions by setting

(7.2.5)

\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}=\widehat{\phi}_{V}-\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}-\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}.

The following result justifies calling this the
cuspidal part
of (
7.2.1
).

Proposition 7.2.2
.

The generating series (
7.2.5
) is cuspidal, in the sense that it lies in the subspace

S_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\subset M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}).

Proof.

The proposition is implicit in the proof of Theorem 7.3.1 of
[
BHK+20a
]
. The essential point is that Theorem 4.2.3 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, a variant of a result of Borcherds, gives a criterion for determining not just when a formal
q
-expansion is modular, but when it lies in the subspace

M^{\infty}_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})\subset M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

of forms that vanish at every cusp except possibly
\infty
.

In the proof of Theorem 7.3.1 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, this criterion is used to show that the formal generating series

\widehat{\phi}_{V}-\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\mid D\\
r>1\end{subarray}}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)\cdot\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(0)-\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)+\sum_{\ell\mid r}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{l}}},0)\right)

lies in

M^{\infty}_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

(which immediately implies that
\widehat{\phi}_{V}
is modular).
This is spelled out more directly in the proof of Theorem 7.1.5 of
[
BHK+20a
]
, but for the generating series, valued in the usual Chow group of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
, obtained from
\widehat{\phi}_{V}
by forgetting the Green functions.

In other words,

\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)+E_{1}(\tau)\cdot\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(0)-\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\right)

vanishes at every cusp except possibly
\infty
.
The Eisenstein series
E_{1}(\tau)
has this same property (Remark
2.7.2
), and therefore so also does
\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)
.
On the other hand, one can check directly (using Proposition
2.7.1
for the Eisenstein part) that the constant term in the
q
-expansion of (
7.2.5
) also vanishes, proving its cuspidality.
∎

7.3.
Heights of arithmetic divisors

Recall from (
4.5.3
) that if the divisor
\mathcal{Z}
underlying

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}=(\mathcal{Z},g_{\mathcal{Z}})\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

meets the boundary of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
properly in the generic fiber, then

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-1})=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}}(\mathcal{Z})+\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}g_{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-1}.

We cannot apply this directly to the arithmetic divisor of Proposition
7.1.1
, because the underlying divisor
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)+\mathcal{B}_{V}(m)
typically contains boundary components with nonzero multiplicities.
The following result, as in §3.2 of
[
BBGK07
]
or §3.4 of
[
Hö14
]
, shows that even when
\mathcal{B}_{V}(m)\neq 0
these boundary components are negligible for our purposes.

Theorem 7.3.1
.

The arithmetic divisor of Proposition
7.1.1
satisfies

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m))+\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-1}.

The same relation holds if
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
is replaced by
\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
or the hermitian line bundle
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
of Theorem
5.5.1
.

The proof of Theorem
7.3.1
requires quite a bit of preparation, and will occupy the remainder of this section.

Fix an
N\geq 3
, and recall from §
5.1
the finite morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
-schemes

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]}

obtained by adding level structure and compactifying.
To ease notation, the pullback of the special divisor
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
via this map is denoted the same way, and similarly for
\mathcal{L}_{V}
and
\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
.

Proposition 7.3.2
.

There is a positive integer
k
and sections

f_{1},\dots,f_{n-1}\in H^{0}\big(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg},\otimes k}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}\big)

with the following properties:

(1)

All possible intersections of

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m),\operatorname{div}(f_{1}),\dots,\operatorname{div}(f_{n-1})

are proper.

(2)

In the generic fiber, the divisor
\operatorname{div}(f_{1})
is disjoint from the boundary.

(3)

The codimension 2 cycle

\operatorname{div}(f_{1})\cap\operatorname{div}(f_{2})

is disjoint from the boundary.

Proof.

This uses the theory of Baily-Borel (or minimal) compactifications of integral models developed in §5 of
[
MP19
]
. As noted after Lemma
4.3.2
, the compactifications of integral models of Hodge type Shimura varieties found in
[
MP19
]
are not obtained by imitating the constructions of Faltings-Chai in the Siegel case.
Rather, they are defined by normalizing the natural morphisms from integral models of open Shimura varieties to Faltings-Chai compactifications of Siegel moduli spaces. Because of Lemma
4.3.2
, all of the results proved in
[
MP19
]
can be applied directly to the integral models

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\subset\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)

used in the proof of Proposition
4.3.3
.

As in §5.2.1 of
[
MP19
]
, the universal abelian scheme
A\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
extends uniquely to a semi-abelian scheme over its toroidal compactification, and the inverse of the determinant of its Lie algebra defines an extension of the Hodge bundle

\omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)}

to a line bundle on the toroidal compactification.
This allows us to define the Baily-Borel compactification

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}}=\mathrm{Proj}\Big(\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N),\omega^{\otimes r})\Big).

Theorem 5.2.11 of
[
MP19
]
provides us with a morphism

\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}}

of projective
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
-schemes such that the composition

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}}

is an open immersion whose complement (the Baily-Borel boundary) is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
.
In particular, as the classical Baily-Borel compactification of the complex fiber of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)
has dimension
0
, the boundary of the integral Baily-Borel compactification has dimension
1
.

Also by Theorem 5.2.11 of
[
MP19
]
, the Hodge bundle
\omega
on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}^{\mathrm{Pap}}(N)
is the pullback of a uniquely determined ample line bundle on the Baily-Borel compactification (denoted the same way), whose pullback via the composition

\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)\xrightarrow{\eqref{compact inclusion}}\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W}(N)\to\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}}

agrees with
\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}
.

Fix a positive integer
k
large enough that
\omega^{k}
defines a closed immersion of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}}
into projective space, and choose a hyperplane section

s_{1}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}},\omega^{\otimes k})

whose divisor does not contain any of the (finitely many) boundary points of the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}}
,
and does not contain the image of any generic point of the divisor

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N).

By construction, the divisor of the pullback

f_{1}\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg},\otimes k}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})

of
s_{1}
does not meet the toroidal boundary in the generic fiber, and intersects
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
properly.

Now choose a hyperplane section

s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}(N)^{\mathrm{min}},\omega^{\otimes k})

whose divisor does not contain any point of the intersection
\mathrm{div}(f_{1})
with the Baily-Borel boundary (this intersection is
0
-dimensional, because the boundary is
1
-dimensional), and does not contain the image of any generic point of
\mathrm{div}(f_{1})
,
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
, or their intersection. Now define

f_{2}\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\omega^{\mathrm{Hdg},\otimes k}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})

as the pullback of
s_{2}
, and continue in this fashion.
∎

Corollary 7.3.3
.

There is a positive integer
k
and sections

F_{1},\dots,F_{n-1}\in H^{0}\big(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\mathcal{L}_{V}^{\otimes k}\big)

with the following properties:

(1)

On the complement of the exceptional divisor

\mathrm{Exc}_{V}(N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)\times_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}}\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),

all possible intersections of

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m),\operatorname{div}(F_{1}),\dots,\operatorname{div}(F_{n-1})

are proper. In particular, this holds on the generic fiber of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
.

(2)

In the generic fiber, the divisor
\operatorname{div}(F_{1})
is disjoint from the boundary.

(3)

The codimension 2 cycle

\operatorname{div}(F_{1})\cap\operatorname{div}(F_{2})

is disjoint from the boundary.

Proof.

This is clear from Propositions
5.5.3
and
7.3.2
, and the fact that the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
has empty generic fiber and does not intersect the boundary.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.3.1
.

Fix sections
F_{1},\ldots,F_{n-1}
as in Corollary
7.3.3
. Each determines an arithmetic divisor

(\mathcal{Z}_{i},g_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(\operatorname{div}(F_{i}),-\log\|F_{i}\|^{2})

representing
k\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})
, and satisifying the Green equation

dd^{c}[g_{i}]+\delta_{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}=k[\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})].

As the divisors
\mathcal{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Z}_{n-1}
intersect properly in the generic fiber, a direct examination of the construction of the arithmetic intersection pairing in §4.3 of
[
BGKK07
]
shows that the iterated intersection

k^{n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-1}=(\mathcal{Z}_{1},g_{1})\cdots(\mathcal{Z}_{n-1},g_{n-1})\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

is represented by a codimension
n-1
arithmetic cycle

\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}=(\mathcal{Y},g_{\mathcal{Y}}),

in which the support of
\mathcal{Y}
is contained in the naive scheme-theoretic intersection
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{Z}_{n-1}
, and

g_{\mathcal{Y}}=g_{1}*\cdots*g_{n-1}

is the iterated star product.
(Really, this means the iterated star product of the pre-log-log Green objects

\mathfrak{g}_{i}=(k\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}),g_{i})\in\widehat{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pre}},\mathcal{Z}_{i}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C}),1),

in the sense of
[
BBGK07
,
BGKK07
]
, but we continue to suppress this extra notational complexity.)

Our choice of sections
F_{i}
guarantees that
\mathcal{Y}
does not intersect the boundary of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
, and does not intersect
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)
in the generic fiber.
Thus

\displaystyle k^{n-1}\widehat{\deg}_{N}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\deg}_{N}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}\big)

(7.3.1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\\
\mathfrak{p}\nmid N\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}\end{subarray}}\deg_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)\cdot\mathcal{Y})\cdot\log(\#\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}/\mathfrak{p})+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)*g_{\mathcal{Y}}.

Here we are working in the arithmetic Chow group of the
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}[1/N]
-scheme
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)
, as in §
4.4
, so these equalities are understood up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
. The degree
\deg_{\mathfrak{p}}
is that of (
4.5.1
), applied to the
0
-cycle

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)\cdot\mathcal{Y}\in\mathrm{CH}^{n}_{\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)\cap\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N))

as in the discussion of §
4.5
.

The integral on the right hand side of (
7.3.1
) is examined in Proposition
7.3.4
below. Substituting the expression found there into (
7.3.1
), and comparing with (
4.5.2
), we find the equality stated in Theorem
7.3.1
holds up to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\{\log(p):p\mid N\}
. The theorem follows by allowing
N\geq 3
to vary.
∎

It remains to prove the formula for the integral in (
7.3.1
) used above.

Proposition 7.3.4
.

Continuing with the notation in the above proof, we have

\int_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)*g_{\mathcal{Y}}=k^{n-1}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-1}+\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}g_{\mathcal{Y}}.

Here, continuing with our abuse of notation, the final integral is really over the pullback of
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})
via

\mathcal{S}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})\to\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

We argue as in the proof of Theorem 3.3 of
[
BBGK07
]
. See also Theorem 3.4.3 of
[
Hö14
]
.
Abbreviate

\Omega=\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}),

and denote by
D=\partial\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})
the toroidal boundary divisor.
For
\varepsilon>0
denote by
B_{\varepsilon}(D)
an
\varepsilon
-neighborhood of
D
.

As in the proof above, the iterated arithmetic intersection

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(\mathcal{Z}_{2},g_{2})\cdots(\mathcal{Z}_{n-1},g_{n-1})\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-2}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N),\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

is represented by a codimension
n-2
arithmetic cycle

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}=(\mathcal{Z},g_{\mathcal{Z}}),

in which the support of
\mathcal{Z}
is contained in the naive scheme-theoretic intersection
\mathcal{Z}_{2}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{Z}_{n-1}
, and the iterated star product

g_{\mathcal{Z}}=g_{2}*\cdots*g_{n-1}

is a
(n-3,n-3)
-form with a logarithmic singularity along
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
and a pre-log-log singularity along
D
, satisfying the Green equation

dd^{c}[g_{\mathcal{Z}}]+\delta_{\mathcal{Z}}=k^{n-2}[\Omega^{n-2}].

Recall from
[
BBGK07
, Section 1.3]
that the star product

g_{\mathcal{Y}}=g_{1}*g_{\mathcal{Z}}

can be represented as follows: Arguing as in
[
Bur97
, p. 362]
there exists an embedded resolution of singularities of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\cup\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
, denoted

\pi:\tilde{X}\to\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C}),

which factors through embedded resolutions of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}(\mathbb{C})
,
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
, and
\mathcal{Z}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\cup\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
, and such that the strict transforms of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})
are disjoint. By the above assumptions on the
F_{i}
, the strict transform of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}(\mathbb{C})
is also disjoint from
\pi^{-1}(D)
.
Let
\sigma_{\mathcal{Z}},1-\sigma_{\mathcal{Z}}
be a partition of the unity on
\tilde{X}
for which
\sigma_{\mathcal{Z}}=1
in a neighborhood of the strict transform of
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbb{C})\cup D
and
\sigma_{\mathcal{Z}}=0
in a neighborhood of the strict transform of
\mathcal{Z}_{1}(\mathbb{C})
.
Then, according to identity (1.5) of
[
BBGK07
]
,

g_{\mathcal{Y}}=k^{n-2}\sigma_{\mathcal{Z}}g_{1}\wedge\Omega^{n-2}+(dd^{c}(1-\sigma_{\mathcal{Z}})g_{1})\wedge g_{\mathcal{Z}}.

In particular, if
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small then on
B_{\varepsilon}(D)
we have

(7.3.2)

\displaystyle g_{\mathcal{Y}}=k^{n-2}g_{1}\wedge\Omega^{n-2}.

We may now apply

[
BBGK07
, Theorem 1.14]
with
y=\mathcal{Z}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)(\mathbb{C})
,
g_{y}=\Phi_{V}(m)
,
z=\mathcal{Y}
, and
g_{z}=g_{\mathcal{Y}}

to see that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})}

\displaystyle\Phi_{V}(m)*g_{\mathcal{Y}}=\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}g_{\mathcal{Y}}+k^{n-1}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)\Omega^{n-1}

\displaystyle-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)}(g_{\mathcal{Y}}\wedge d^{c}\Phi_{V}(m)-\Phi_{V}(m)\wedge d^{c}g_{\mathcal{Y}}).

By (
7.3.2
) the limit on the right hand side is equal to

k^{n-2}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\bigg(\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)}g_{1}\wedge\Omega^{n-2}\wedge d^{c}\Phi_{V}(m)-\Phi_{V}(m)\wedge d^{c}g_{1}\wedge\Omega^{n-2}\bigg),

which vanishes by Lemma
7.3.5
below.
∎

Lemma 7.3.5
.

If
F_{1}
is any meromorphic section of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{\otimes k}
on
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(N)(\mathbb{C})
, whose divisor is disjoint from the boundary, then

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)}\log\|F_{1}\|\wedge\Omega^{n-2}\wedge d^{c}\Phi_{V}(m)=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)}\Phi_{V}(m)\wedge d^{c}\log\|F_{1}\|\wedge\Omega^{n-2}=0.

Before we prove this lemma, we need to recall from Section 4.3 of
[
BHY15
]
the complex analytic description of the boundary of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
.
Using (
7.1.1
), it suffices to do this for the boundary of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
. By (
3.1.4
) the variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(H,\mathcal{D})
is a finite disjoint union of connected Shimura varieties of the form
X_{\Gamma}=\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{D}
, where
\Gamma\subset H=\operatorname{U}(V)
is an arithmetic subgroup stabilizing a self-dual hermitian lattice in
V
.
Hence it suffices to consider the boundary of the canonical toroidal compactification
\bar{X}_{\Gamma}
of
X_{\Gamma}
.

The boundary of
\bar{X}_{\Gamma}
is a smooth divisor. Its connected components correspond to
\Gamma
-orbits of isotropic lines in
V
.
Fix an isotropic line
I\subset V
. Let
\ell\in I
be a generator, and let

\widetilde{\ell}\in V
be isotropic such that
\langle\ell,\widetilde{\ell}\rangle=1
.
The orthogonal complement

W=\ell^{\perp}\cap\widetilde{\ell}{}^{\perp}

is a positive definite hermitian space over
{\bm{k}}
of dimension
n-2
, and we have

V=W\oplus{\bm{k}}\ell\oplus{\bm{k}}\widetilde{\ell}.

Every
z\in\mathcal{D}
, viewed as a negative definite
\mathbb{C}
-line in
V(\mathbb{R})
as in (
3.1.1
),
has a unique basis vector of the form

\mathfrak{z}+\tau\sqrt{-D}\ell+\widetilde{\ell}

with
\mathfrak{z}\in W(\mathbb{R})
and
\tau\in\mathbb{C}
.
The condition that
z
is negative definite is equivalent to the real number

\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}-\langle(\mathfrak{z},\tau),(\mathfrak{z},\tau)\rangle=2\sqrt{D}\cdot\mathrm{Im}(\tau)-\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle

being positive. Consequently,
\mathcal{D}
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\widetilde{\ell}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{(\mathfrak{z},\tau)\in W(\mathbb{R})\times\mathcal{H}:\;2\sqrt{D}\cdot\mathrm{Im}(\tau)>\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle\}.

The Chern form
\Omega
is given in these coordinates by

(7.3.3)

\displaystyle\Omega=-dd^{c}\log\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau).

For
\varepsilon>0
we define

\displaystyle U_{\varepsilon}(\ell)

\displaystyle=\left\{z\in\mathcal{D}:\;-\frac{\langle z,z\rangle}{|\langle z,\ell\rangle|^{2}}>\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\cong\{(\mathfrak{z},\tau)\in\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\widetilde{\ell}}:\;\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)>1/\varepsilon\}.

The stabilizer
\operatorname{U}(V)_{\ell}
of
I={\bm{k}}\ell
acts on this subset.
Let
\Gamma_{\ell}=\Gamma\cap\operatorname{U}(V)_{\ell}
.
If
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small,
then

\Gamma_{\ell}\backslash U_{\varepsilon}(\ell)\longrightarrow X_{\Gamma}

is an open immersion.
The center of
\operatorname{U}(V)_{\ell}

is given by the subgroup of translations
T_{a}
for
a\in\mathbb{Q}
, where

T_{a}(x)=x+a\langle x,\ell\rangle\sqrt{-D}\ell

for
x\in V
. The action of these translations on
\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\widetilde{\ell}}
is given by

T_{a}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)=(\mathfrak{z},\tau+a).

The center of
\Gamma_{\ell}

is of the form

\Gamma_{\ell,T}=\{T_{a}:a\in r\mathbb{Z}\}

for a unique
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
.
If we put
q_{r}=e^{2\pi i\tau/r}
, then
(\mathfrak{z},\tau)\mapsto(\mathfrak{z},q_{r})
defines an isomorphism from
\Gamma_{\ell,T}\backslash U_{\varepsilon}(\ell)
to

V_{\varepsilon}(\ell)=\left\{(\mathfrak{z},q_{r})\in\mathbb{C}^{n-2}\times\mathbb{C}:\;0<|q_{r}|<\exp\left(-\tfrac{\pi}{r\sqrt{D}}(\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle+1/\varepsilon)\right)\right\}.

Adding the origin to this punctured disc bundle yields the disc bundle

\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}(\ell)=\left\{(\mathfrak{z},q_{r})\in\mathbb{C}^{n-2}\times\mathbb{C}:\;|q_{r}|<\exp\left(-\tfrac{\pi}{r\sqrt{D}}(\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle+1/\varepsilon)\right)\right\}.

The action of
\Gamma_{\ell}/\Gamma_{\ell,T}
on
V_{\varepsilon}(\ell)
extends to a properly discontinuous action on
\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}(\ell)
, which leaves the boundary divisor
q_{r}=0
invariant.
We obtain an open immersion of orbifolds

\Gamma_{\ell}\backslash U_{\varepsilon}(\ell)\longrightarrow\left(\Gamma_{\ell}/\Gamma_{\ell,T}\right)\backslash\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}(\ell).

It can be used to glue the right hand side to
X_{\Gamma}
to obtain a partial compactification near the cusp
I
. The family of these open immersions for small
\varepsilon>0
defines a base of open neighborhoods of the boundary divisor
D_{I}
associated with
I
.

Any boundary point
z\in D_{I}
has a representative of the form
(\mathfrak{z}_{0},0)\in\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}(\ell)
. The images of the sets

(7.3.4)

\displaystyle B_{\delta}(\mathfrak{z}_{0},0)=\left\{(\mathfrak{z},q_{r})\in\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}(\ell):\langle\mathfrak{z}-\mathfrak{z}_{0},\mathfrak{z}-\mathfrak{z}_{0}\rangle<\delta,\;|q_{r}|<\delta\right\}.

in
\bar{X}_{\Gamma}
for small
\delta>0
define a base of open neighborhoods of the boundary point.

Proof of Lemma
7.3.5
.

In view of the above local description of the boundary, it suffices to show that for every isotropic line
I\subset V
and for a small neighborhood

Y=B_{\delta}(\mathfrak{z}_{0},0)

of any boundary point
(\mathfrak{z}_{0},0)
of
D_{I}
we have

(7.3.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y}\log\|F_{1}\|\wedge\Omega^{n-2}\wedge d^{c}\Phi_{V}(m)=0,

(7.3.6)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y}\Phi_{V}(m)\wedge d^{c}\log\|F_{1}\|\wedge\Omega^{n-2}=0.

Using the coordinates of (
7.3.4
), we have

\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)=-\frac{\sqrt{D}r}{\pi}\log|q_{r}|-\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle.

By (
7.3.3
) we find

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)^{2}}(d\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau))\wedge(d^{c}\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau))+\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)}dd^{c}\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle.

If we write in addition

q_{r}=e^{2\pi i\tau/r}=t\cdot e^{i\rho}
in polar coordinates with
t\geq 0
and
\rho\in[0,2\pi)
, and employ the identities
d\log|q_{r}|=\frac{dt}{t}
and
d^{c}\log|q_{r}|=\frac{1}{4\pi}d\rho
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)^{2}}\left(-\frac{\sqrt{D}r}{\pi}\frac{dt}{t}-d\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle\right)\wedge\left(-\frac{\sqrt{D}r}{4\pi^{2}}d\rho-d^{c}\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}{}+\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)}dd^{c}\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle.

Hence the restriction of
\Omega
to
\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y
is given by

\displaystyle\Omega\mid_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)^{2}}d\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle\wedge\left(\frac{\sqrt{D}r}{4\pi^{2}}d\rho+d^{c}\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle\right)+\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)}dd^{c}\langle\mathfrak{z},\mathfrak{z}\rangle.

The differential form
d^{c}\log\|F_{1}\|
is the sum of a smooth
1
-form on
Y

and a constant multiple of
\frac{1}{\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)}d^{c}\operatorname{N}(\mathfrak{z},\tau)
. This implies that

\displaystyle\Omega^{n-2}\wedge d^{c}\log\|F_{1}\|\mid_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y}=\frac{h(t,\rho,\mathfrak{z})}{|\log t|^{n-1}}d\rho\wedge d\mathfrak{z}_{1}\dots d\mathfrak{z}_{n-2}\wedge d\bar{\mathfrak{z}}_{1}\dots d\bar{\mathfrak{z}}_{n-2},

where
h
is a continuous function in
t\in[0,1)
,
\rho\in[0,2\pi]
and
\mathfrak{z}
, which is smooth for
t>0
.

On the other hand, according to
[
BHY15
, Theorem 4.10]
, the restriction of the Green function
\Phi_{V}(m)
to
Y
is the sum of a continuous function and constant multiples of the functions

\log t,\qquad\log|\log t|,\qquad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in S_{m}\end{subarray}}\log|\langle x,\mathfrak{z}+\tilde{\ell}\rangle|,

where
S_{m}
denotes the finite set

S_{m}=\{x\in L\cap\ell^{\perp}:\;\langle x,\mathfrak{z}_{0}+\tilde{\ell}\rangle=0,\langle x,x\rangle=m\}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y}\Phi_{V}(m)\wedge d^{c}\log\|F_{1}\|\wedge\Omega^{n-2}\leq C\frac{1}{|\log\varepsilon|^{n-2}}

for some positive constant
C
.
Since
n\geq 3
, we find that the limit in (
7.3.6
) vanishes.

For the limit in (
7.3.5
) we may argue similarly. Here
[
BHY15
, Theorem 4.10]
implies that the restriction of
d^{c}\Phi_{V}(m)
to
Y
is the sum of a
1
-form with log-log growth along
D\cap Y
and constant multiples of the differentials

d\rho,\qquad\frac{d\rho}{|\log t|},\qquad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in S_{m}\end{subarray}}d^{c}\log|\langle x,\mathfrak{z}+\tilde{\ell}\rangle|.

On the other hand,
\log\|F_{1}\|
is the sum of a continuous function and
\log|\log t|
.
Putting the terms together, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{\varepsilon}(D)\cap Y}\log\|F_{1}\|\wedge\Omega^{n-2}\wedge d^{c}\Phi_{V}(m)\leq C^{\prime}\frac{\log|\log\varepsilon|}{|\log\varepsilon|^{n-2}}

for some positive constant
C^{\prime}
.
Again the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
vanishes.
∎

Lemma
7.3.5
completes the proof of Proposition
7.3.4
, which completes the proof of Theorem
7.3.1
.

Chapter 8
The volume calculations

This chapter contains our main results.
We continue to work with the Shimura variety
\mathcal{S}_{V}
of (
5.1.4
) defined by a
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space
V
of signature
(n-1,1)
with
n\geq 1
, containing a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice
L\subset V
.
We maintain the assumption that
D
is odd imposed since Chapter
4
.

We will use induction on
n
to compute the complex and arithmetic volumes (as defined in §
4.5
) of the hermitian line bundle

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}=2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}-(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

of Theorem
5.5.1
. All results stated in the introduction will follow as consequences of this calculation.

8.1.
Volumes of Shimura curves

First we consider the case
n=2
, so that
V
has signature
(1,1)
and
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
has dimension one.
The following proposition is a consequence of the main result of
[
How20
]
, which is itself a consequence of the
calculation of arithmetic volumes of modular curves and quaternion Shimura curves due to Kühn
[
Kü01
]
, Bost (unpublished), and Kudla-Rapoport-Yang
[
KRY06
]
.

Proposition 8.1.1
.

Suppose
n=2
.
The metrized Hodge bundle of
A\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
has complex volume

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|^{2}}{2^{o(D)-1}\cdot 12\cdot|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|^{2}}\prod_{\ell\mid D}(1+\ell^{*})

and arithmetic volume

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})=\left(-2-\frac{4\zeta^{\prime}(-1)}{\zeta(-1)}-\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{1-\ell^{*}}{1+\ell^{*}}\cdot\log(\ell)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}),

where
\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})
is the class group of
{\bm{k}}
,
o(D)
is the number of prime divisors of
D
, and we abbreviate

\ell^{*}=\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V)\ell.

Proof.

As in (
5.1.4
), we realize

\mathcal{S}_{V}\subset\mathcal{M}_{W_{0}}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\mathcal{M}_{W}

as a union of connected components, where
W_{0}
and
W
have signatures
(1,0)
and
(1,1)
, respectively.

The
\mathrm{GU}(W)
Shimura datum defining the generic fibers of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Pap}}_{W}
and
\mathcal{M}_{W}
actually has reflex field
\mathbb{Q}
rather than
{\bm{k}}
, and in §4 of
[
How20
]
one finds the definition of a regular and flat Deligne-Mumford stack

\mathcal{X}_{W}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})

such that
\mathcal{M}_{W}^{\mathrm{Pap}}\cong\mathcal{X}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}
, and such that the universal abelian surface over
\mathcal{M}_{W}^{\mathrm{Pap}}
descends to
A\to\mathcal{X}_{W}
. The stack
\mathcal{X}_{W}
has its own canonical toroidal compactification and its own theory of arithmetic Chow groups, and Theorem A of
[
How20
]
implies

(8.1.1)

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{X}_{W}})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}\cdot 12\cdot|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\prod_{\ell\mid D}(1+\ell^{*})

and

(8.1.2)

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{X}_{W}})=\left(-1-\frac{2\zeta^{\prime}(-1)}{\zeta(-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{1-\ell^{*}}{1+\ell^{*}}\cdot\log(\ell)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{X}_{W}}).

The composition

\mathcal{S}_{V}\to\mathcal{M}_{W}\to\mathcal{M}_{W}^{\mathrm{Pap}}\cong\mathcal{X}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}

extends to a proper morphism

\varphi:\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}\to\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}},

which, using Remark
5.1.2
, restricts to a finite étale surjection of degree

\deg(\varphi)=|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|/|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|

from
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Exc}_{V}
to the smooth locus of
\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}
.

If
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
is any pre-log singular hermitian line bundle on
\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{V}
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}
is its base change to
\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{V/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}
, then unpacking the definitions shows that
1
1

1

The factor of
2
appears in the second equality because it is the degree of the finite flat morphism
\mathcal{X}_{W/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}\to\mathcal{X}_{W}
. It does not appear in the first equality because, under the conventions of Remark
4.4.2
, this morphism induces an isomorphism on complex points.

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\varphi^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}})=\deg(\varphi)\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}})

and

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\varphi^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}})=2\cdot\deg(\varphi)\cdot\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}).

The proposition follows by applying these to the hermitian line bundles

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{X}_{V}},\qquad\varphi^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}}=\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}

and using (
8.1.1
) and (
8.1.2
).
∎

We now express the complex and arithmetic volumes of
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
in terms of the meromorphic function
\mathbf{A}_{V}(s)
defined by (
1.1.5
). This will form the base case of the inductive proofs of Theorems
8.2.3
and
8.4.2
below.

Corollary 8.1.2
.

Suppose
n=2
.
The hermitian line bundle (
5.5.1
) has complex volume

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-2}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0),

and arithmetic volume

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\log(D)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}).

Proof.

Replacing
W
by
V
in (
1.1.3
), and using
D\equiv 3\pmod{4}
, we
find

\prod_{\ell\mid D}\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)\operatorname{inv}_{\ell}(V)=1.

Recalling (
1.1.5
), this proves the second equality in

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}L(1,\varepsilon)\zeta(2)}{8\pi^{3}}\prod_{\ell\mid D}\left(1+\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)\mathrm{inv}_{\ell}(V)\ell^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{D^{\frac{1}{2}}L(1,\varepsilon)\zeta(2)}{8\pi^{3}}\prod_{\ell\mid D}\left(1+\ell^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{24\cdot|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\prod_{\ell\mid D}\left(1+\ell^{*}\right).

The final equality follows from
\zeta(2)=\pi^{2}/6
and Dirichlet’s class number formula

D^{\frac{1}{2}}L(1,\varepsilon)=2\pi|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|/|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|.

Comparing this with Proposition
8.1.1
, and using the equality of Chern forms of Theorem (
5.5.1
), shows

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-2}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0).

For the second claim, taking the logarithmic derivative of (
1.1.5
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{1}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{1}(0)}+\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{2}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{2}(0)}-\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{\log(\ell)}{1+\ell^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{1}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{1}(0)}+\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{2}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{2}(0)}-\frac{1}{2}\log(D)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell\mid D}\left(\frac{1-\ell^{*}}{1+\ell^{*}}\right)\log(\ell).

One can use the functional equation of
L(s,\varepsilon^{k})
to see that

(8.1.3)

\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{k}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{k}(0)}=-2\frac{L^{\prime}(1-k,\varepsilon^{k})}{L(1-k,\varepsilon^{k})}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(k)}{\Gamma(k)}+\log\left(\frac{4\pi}{D}\right)+(-1)^{k}\log(D)

for all
k\geq 1
. Recalling the constant
C_{0}(2)
of Theorem
A
, and using the well-known formulas
\Gamma^{\prime}(1)/\Gamma(1)=-\gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}(2)/\Gamma(2)=1-\gamma
, we find

\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{1}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{1}(0)}+\frac{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}_{2}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{2}(0)}=-1+C_{0}(2)-2\frac{\zeta^{\prime}(-1)}{\zeta(-1)}.

Putting this all together gives

\displaystyle 2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}-2C_{0}(2)+\log\left(D\right)=-2-4\frac{\zeta^{\prime}(-1)}{\zeta(-1)}-\sum_{\ell\mid D}\left(\frac{1-\ell^{*}}{1+\ell^{*}}\right)\log(\ell),

and comparing with Proposition
8.1.1
shows that

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})=\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}-2C_{0}(2)+\log(D)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}).

The second claim follows from this using Proposition
6.7.1
and the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
.
∎

8.2.
The complex volume formula

We now use induction on
n\geq 2
to compute the complex volumes of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
on the Shimura variety
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
associated to a relevant hermitian space
V
of signature
(n-1,1)
.

Taking arithmetic intersection against the fixed arithmetic cycle class

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2}\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-2}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and then applying the complex degree of §
4.5
defines a linear functional

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\mathbb{Q},

denoted

(8.2.1)

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\mapsto\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})

and called
complex intersection against
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2}
.
Of course this only depends on the underlying divisor
\mathcal{Z}
and line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{V}
on
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
, and in fact only on their restrictions to the complex fiber.

Assuming that
n>2
(an assumption imposed throughout Chapter
7
), we may apply (
8.2.1
) coefficient-by-coefficient to the generating series of special divisors (
7.2.1
). This yields a modular form

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=\sum_{m\geq 0}\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})\cdot q^{m}\in M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

with rational coefficients,
and similarly for the other forms appearing in the decomposition

\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)=\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)+\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}(\tau)+\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)

of (
7.2.2
). Note that we have the obvious equality

(8.2.2)

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=0,

as the coefficients of the generating series (
7.2.4
) lie in the kernel of

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\operatorname{CH}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathbb{C}}).

Proposition 8.2.1
.

For
n>2
, we have the equalities of modular forms

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})

\displaystyle=-\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})+\sum_{m>0}\left(\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}\right)\cdot q^{m}

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau).

Proof.

The second claim follows easily from (
7.2.3
) and the definitions.

For the first claim, we compare the two modular forms coefficient-by-coefficient.
As the equality of constant terms is obvious, it suffices to prove that

(8.2.3)

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}

for all
m>0
. The left hand side is equal to the image of

(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)+\mathcal{B}_{V}(m))\cdot\mathcal{L}_{V}^{n-2}\in\mathrm{CH}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})

under the composition

(8.2.4)

\mathrm{CH}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})\to\mathrm{CH}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V/\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{\deg}\mathbb{Q}

of the restriction to the complex fiber and the usual degree of a
0
-cycle.
As in the proof of Theorem
7.3.1
, one may represent the cycle class
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{n-2}\in\mathrm{CH}^{n-2}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})
by a
1
-cycle that does not meet the boundary in the generic fiber, and hence the left hand side of (
8.2.3
) is equal to the image of

\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{V}^{n-2}\in\mathrm{CH}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V})

under the composition (
8.2.4
).

Fix a smooth (on all of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
, not just the interior) metric on
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
, and let
\varphi
be the Chern form of this metric. The exterior power

\varphi^{n-2}\in H^{2n-4}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{C})

agrees with the cycle class of
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{n-2}
.
As the second arrow in (
8.2.4
) factors through the cycle class map to cohomology, it follows that

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\varphi^{n-2}.

We cannot apply this directly to
\varphi=\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})
because the metric on

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
does not extend smoothly across the boundary, but it is easy to see that one can write

\varphi^{n-2}=\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}+d\alpha

for a pre-log-log form
\alpha
on
\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
.
Propositions 7.6 and 7.12 of
[
BGKK07
]
imply that the pullback of
\alpha
to
\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})
is locally integrable on the closure
\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})
, and satisfies the equality of currents
[d\alpha]=d[\alpha]
. Evaluating both sides of this equation at the constant function
1
yields

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}d\alpha=0,

completing the proof.
∎

Recall from §
2.6
and §
2.7
the holomorphic Eisenstein series

\sum_{m\geq 0}B(m,0,s_{0})\cdot q^{m}\in M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

associated to the self-dual hermitian lattice
L\subset V
.

Proposition 8.2.2
.

Assume
n>2
.
We have the equality of modular forms

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2}).

Equivalently, given (
8.2.2
),

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=0.

Proof.

Given Proposition
8.2.1
and the equality

\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}B(m,0,s_{0})\cdot q^{m}

of Proposition
2.7.3
, the first claim is just a restatement of the equality

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}=-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})B(m,0,s_{0})

of Remark
3.1.1
.
Here we are using the discussion of §
7.1
, and especially the diagram (
7.1.1
), to relate the Shimura variety for the unitary group
H=\mathrm{U}(V)
used throughout §
3.1
to the complex orbifold
\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})
currently under consideration.
∎

Theorem 8.2.3
.

If
n\geq 2
, the complex volumes of (
5.2.3
) and (
5.5.1
) are

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)

and

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-n}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0).

Proof.

The two claims are equivalent, by the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
,
and both hold when
n=2
by Corollary
8.1.2
. It therefore it suffices to assume that
n>2
, and that the first equality holds for all relevant (Definition
4.1.3
) hermitian spaces
V^{\flat}
of signature
(n-2,1)
.

Let
p
be a prime split in
{\bm{k}}
, and let
V^{\flat}
be the hermitian space of signature
(n-2,1)
defined by (
6.1.1
).
Using the first claim of Theorem
6.1.2
and the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
, we find

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}=(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}}).

On the other hand, we have the equality

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}=-B(p,0,s_{0})\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})

from the proof of Proposition
8.2.2
.
Directly from the definition (
1.1.5
) and the explicit formulas of Corollaries
2.6.5
and
2.6.6
, we find that

\frac{B(p,0,s_{0})}{p^{n-1}+1}=-\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}.

Combining the three formulas in the previous paragraph, one obtains

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}})=\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}).

The theorem follows from this and our induction hypothesis

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}})=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0).\qed

Remark 8.2.4
.

Theorem
8.2.3
is also true when
n=1
.
In this case

\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathcal{M}_{1,0}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathcal{M}_{0,1}(\mathbb{C})

is a complex orbifold of dimension
0
parametrizing certain pairs of elliptic curves with complex multiplication by
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
, the Chern form of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
is (obviously) trivial, and the theory of complex multiplication implies

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}1=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Aut}(s)|}=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|^{2}}{2^{o(D)-1}\cdot|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|^{2}}.

This agrees with

\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{a}_{1}(0)=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|^{2}}{2^{o(D)-1}\cdot|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|^{2}},

where the final equality is by Dirichlet’s class number formula.

8.3.
An alternate proof of the complex volume formula

We have invoked Remark
3.1.1
in the proofs of Proposition

8.2.2
and Theorem
8.2.3
,
but we have enough information at this point to give an independent proof of
Theorem
8.2.3
, and deduce much of Remark
3.1.1
and
Proposition
8.2.2
as a consequence.
This is not logically necessary in what follows, but the same method will be used in the proof of Theorem
8.4.2
below, and so we explain the argument here.

As before, the proof of Theorem
8.2.3
is by induction on
n\geq 2
, with the case
n=2
already known by Corollary
8.1.2
. Thus we assume
n>2
.

Suppose
p
is a prime split in
{\bm{k}}
, and let
V^{\flat}
denote the
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space of signature
(n-2,1)
characterized by (
6.1.1
).
It follows from Theorem
6.1.2
and the induction hypothesis that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}

\displaystyle=(p^{n-1}+1)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V^{\flat}})

\displaystyle=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0).

Exactly as in the proof of Theorem
8.2.3
,
Corollaries
2.6.5
and
2.6.6
imply

-\frac{B(p,0,s_{0})}{p^{n-1}+1}=\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)},

and therefore

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(p)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}=-\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)\cdot B(p,0,s_{0}).

Combining this with Proposition
8.2.1
shows that the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of the
cuspidal
modular form

(8.3.1)

\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})=\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})-\deg_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}^{n-2})

is

(8.3.2)

\left(\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})-\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{2^{o(D)-1}|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)\right)\cdot B(p,0,s_{0}).

The essential thing is that the quantity in parentheses in (
8.3.2
) is independent of the choice of split prime
p
, which we now allow to vary.
The Hecke bound for coefficients of cusp forms implies that the absolute value of (
8.3.2
) is
O(p^{n/2})
as
p\to\infty
. On the other hand, Corollary
2.6.8
shows that the absolute value of
B(p,0,s_{0})
is greater than a constant times
p^{n-1}
. As
n>2
, we conclude from this that the quantity in parentheses in (
8.3.2
) vanishes, completing our alternate proof of
Theorem
8.2.3
.

Moreover, having given a new proof that the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
Fourier coefficient of (
8.3.1
)
vanishes for all primes
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
, we deduce from Corollary
A.2
that the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
Fourier coefficient vanishes for all
m
with
\varepsilon(m)=1
.
Again invoking Proposition
8.2.1
, we deduce that

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})^{n-2}=-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})B(m,0,s_{0})

for all such
m
. In this way, our alternate proof recovers much of
Remark
3.1.1
and Proposition
8.2.2
in a new way.

8.4.
The arithmetic volume formula

The arithmetic intersection and arithmetic degree of §
4.4
provide us with a bilinear pairing

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\mathbb{R},

sending

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}\mapsto\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}).

The hermitian line bundle

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}=2\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}-(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

of Theorem
5.5.1
therefore determines a linear functional

\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})\to\mathbb{R},

called
arithmetic intersection against
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}
, given by

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\mapsto\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}).

Assuming (as throughout Chapter
7
) that
n>2
, we have the modular form

(8.4.1)

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=\sum_{m\geq 0}\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)\cdot q^{m}

in
M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})
obtained
by applying this linear functional coefficient-by-coefficient to (
7.2.1
),
and similarly for the other forms appearing in the decomposition

\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)=\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)+\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}(\tau)+\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)

of (
7.2.2
).
As
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
has trivial arithmetic intersection with every irreducible component of the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Exc}_{V}\subset\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
by Theorem
5.5.1
, it has trivial arithmetic intersection with every coefficient of
7.2.4
, and

(8.4.2)

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{exc}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=0.

Motivated by Proposition
8.2.2
, we make the following conjecture.

Conjecture 8.4.1
.

Assuming
n>2
, we have the equality of modular forms

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}).

Equivalently, given (
8.4.2
),

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=0.

At long last we come to the central result of this work: the calculation of the arithmetic volume of
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
via induction.
The calculation will simultaneously yield evidence for Conjecture
8.4.1
, and also provide an explicit value for the mysterious rational numbers
a(p)
of Theorem
6.1.2
.

Theorem 8.4.2
.

The hermitian line bundle
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
satisfies the following properties.

(1)

If
n\geq 2
, its arithmetic volume (in the sense of §
4.5
) is

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\log(D)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}).

(2)

If
n>2
, then for any prime
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
the rational number
a(p)
of Theorem
6.1.2
is given by

a(p)=2\cdot\frac{p^{n-1}-1}{p^{n-1}+1}\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}),

where
V^{\flat}
the hermitian space of signature
(n-2,1)
with local invariants determined by (
6.1.1
).

(3)

If
n>2
then for any positive
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\varepsilon(m)=1
, the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
Fourier coefficient of

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})\in S_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

vanishes.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
. As we already know the first claim for
n=2
by Corollary
8.1.2
, it suffices to assume that
n>2
, and that the first claim holds for all relevant (Definition
4.1.3
) hermitian spaces
V^{\flat}
of signature
(n-2,1)
.

We define
C(V)
by the relation

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=C(V)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}),

and similarly for any
V^{\flat}
as above, so that our induction hypothesis is

(8.4.3)

C(V^{\flat})=2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}+\log(D).

Recall from Proposition
2.7.3
the Eisenstein series

\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}B(m,0,s_{0})q^{m}\in M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n}).

Recall also from Proposition
2.7.4
the rational number

\beta_{\ell}=(-1)^{n+1}\operatorname{inv}_{\ell}(V)\cdot\begin{cases}\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\ell^{\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\\[8.61108pt]
\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\ell^{\frac{n-1}{2}}&\mbox{if $n$ is odd}\end{cases}

for every prime
\ell\mid D
.

Lemma 8.4.3
.

Let
p
be a prime split in
{\bm{k}}
, let
V^{\flat}
the hermitian space of signature
(n-2,1)
with local invariants determined by (
6.1.1
). If we abbreviate

\displaystyle\kappa(p)

\displaystyle=2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\log(D)+\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{2\log(\ell)}{1+\beta_{\ell}\cdot\left(\frac{p}{\ell}\right)^{n}},

\displaystyle\alpha(p)

\displaystyle=(p^{n-1}+1)a(p)-2(p^{n-1}-1)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}),

then the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of the modular form

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})\in M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

is equal to

-\frac{\kappa(p)}{2}\cdot B(p,0,s_{0})\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})+\alpha(p)\cdot\log(p).

Proof.

By Theorem
7.3.1
, the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of (
8.4.1
) is

(8.4.4)

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(p)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))+\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(p)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}.

Using Theorem
6.1.2
, we rewrite the first term on the right hand side as

\displaystyle\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(p))

\displaystyle=(p^{n-1}+1)\left(\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})+a(p)\log(p)\right)

(8.4.5)

\displaystyle=(p^{n-1}+1)\left(C(V^{\flat})\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})+a(p)\log(p)\right).

Now consider the second term on the right hand side of (
8.4.4
).
By Remark
7.1.2
and the equality

2\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})=\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})

of Theorem
5.5.1
, it is equal to

\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(p)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}=\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})B^{\prime}(p,0,s_{0}).

Theorem
8.2.3
implies the equality

(8.4.6)

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})=2\cdot\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}),

which we use to rewrite the integral above as

(8.4.7)

\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(p)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}=C_{1}\cdot(p^{n-1}+1)\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}}),

in which we have set

C_{1}=2\cdot\frac{B^{\prime}(p,0,s_{0})}{p^{n-1}+1}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}.

Plugging (
8.4.5
) and (
8.4.7
) into (
8.4.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(p)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)

\displaystyle=(p^{n-1}+1)(C_{1}+C(V^{\flat}))\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})

\displaystyle\quad+(p^{n-1}+1)a(p)\log(p).

By direct calculation, Corollaries
2.6.5
and
2.6.6
(along with the definition (
1.1.5
) of
\mathbf{A}_{V}(s)
and
\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(s)
) give the explicit formulas

(8.4.8)

\frac{B(p,0,s_{0})}{p^{n-1}+1}=-\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}

and

\displaystyle\frac{B^{\prime}(p,0,s_{0})}{B(p,0,s_{0})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}-\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\frac{p^{n-1}-1}{p^{n-1}+1}\log(p)-\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{\log(\ell)}{1+\beta_{\ell}\cdot\left(\frac{p}{\ell}\right)^{n}}.

Using these equalities we see that the constant
C_{1}
can be rewritten as

C_{1}=-2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}+2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}-2\frac{p^{n-1}-1}{p^{n-1}+1}\log(p)+\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{2\log(\ell)}{1+\beta_{\ell}\cdot\left(\frac{p}{\ell}\right)^{n}}.

Combining this with the induction hypothesis (
8.4.3
) shows that

\displaystyle C_{1}+C(V^{\flat})

\displaystyle=\kappa(p)-2\frac{p^{n-1}-1}{p^{n-1}+1}\log(p),

and we obtain

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(p)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)=(p^{n-1}+1)\kappa(p)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})+\alpha(p)\log(p).

To complete the proof of the lemma, substitute the equality

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V^{\flat}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{complex vol swap}}}{{=}}\frac{\mathbf{A}_{V^{\flat}}(0)}{2\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{explicit p}}}{{=}}-\frac{B(p,0,s_{0})}{2(p^{n-1}+1)}\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})

into the first term on the right hand side.
∎

Lemma 8.4.4
.

We have the equality of modular forms

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)

(8.4.9)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\log(D/r)\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau).

Moreover, for positive
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\gcd(m,D)=1
the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of this modular form is

-\frac{1}{2}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})+\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{2\log(\ell)}{1+\beta_{\ell}\cdot\left(\frac{m}{\ell}\right)^{n}}\right)B(m,0,s_{0}).

Proof.

Directly from the definition (
7.2.3
) of
\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)

we find

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

\displaystyle=-\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{r\mid D}\widehat{\deg}\big((\mathrm{Exc}_{V},\log(D/r))\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau).

For the first term on the right hand side, we use Theorem
5.5.1
to see that

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=2^{-1}\cdot\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=2^{-1}\cdot\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}).

For the second term on the right hand side, it follows from Theorem
5.5.1
that
(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}=0
.
As in the proof of Lemma
4.5.4
, we have the easy arithmetic intersection formula

(0,\log(D/r))\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}=\big(0,\log(D/r)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}\big)\in\widehat{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{V},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}),

and hence, directly from the definition (
4.4.4
) of arithmetic degree,

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}((0,\log(D/r))\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

\displaystyle=\log(D/r)\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}

\displaystyle=\log(D/r)\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}).

Thus

\widehat{\deg}((\mathrm{Exc}_{V},\log(D/r))\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=\log(D/r)\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}).

The equality (
8.4.9
) follows immediately from these formulas.

For the second claim, using Proposition
2.7.3
and Proposition
2.7.4
, the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of the modular form on the right hand side of (
8.4.9
) is

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}e_{r}(m)-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}e_{r}(m)\log(D/r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})B(m,0,s_{0})-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\left(\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{\log(\ell)}{1+\beta_{\ell}\cdot\left(\frac{m}{\ell}\right)^{n}}\right)B(m,0,s_{0}),

as desired.
∎

The proof of Theorem
8.4.2
now proceeds by examining the Fourier coefficients of the modular form

(8.4.10)

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})=\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})-\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}).

If we abbreviate

c=\frac{\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})}{2}-\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\frac{\log(D)}{2}\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}),

then comparing Lemma
8.4.3
and Lemma
8.4.4
shows that the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of (
8.4.10
) is

cB(p,0,s_{0})+\alpha(p)\log(p)

for all primes
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
.
In particular, the modular form

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{cusp}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})-c\sum_{m\geq 0}B(m,0,s_{0})

has the property that its
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient is
\alpha(p)\log(p)\in\mathbb{Q}\log(p)
for all such primes.
It follows from Corollary
A.4
that
\alpha(p)=0
, proving the second claim of Theorem
8.4.2
.

We have now seen the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of the
cuspidal
modular form (
8.4.10
) is
cB(p,0,s_{0})
for all primes
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
, and may argue as in §
8.3
.
The Hecke bound for coefficients of cusp forms implies that the absolute value of (
8.3.2
) is
O(p^{n/2})
as
p\to\infty
