, while Corollary
2.6.8
shows that the absolute value of
B(p,0,s_{0})
is greater than a constant times
p^{n-1}
. We conclude that
c=0
, proving the first claim of Theorem
8.4.2
.

Finally, having now shown that the
p^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient of (
8.4.10
) vanishes for all primes
p
split in
{\bm{k}}
, Corollary
A.2
shows that the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
coefficient vanishes whenever
\varepsilon(m)=1
. This proves the third claim of Theorem
8.4.2
.
∎

8.5.
Consequences of Theorem 8.4.2

We now collect many corollaries of Theorem
8.4.2
.
The first of these are explicit formulas for the arithmetic volumes of the other (perhaps more natural) hermitian line bundles on
\mathcal{S}_{V}
defined in §
5.2
.

Corollary 8.5.1
.

Assuming
n\geq 2
, we have the arithmetic volume formulas

\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})=\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}-nC_{0}(n)+\log(D)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\frac{\log(D)}{2}\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})+\frac{(-1)^{n-1}}{2}\sum_{E\subset\mathrm{Exc}_{V}}\widehat{m}_{E}.

In the second equality the sum is over all irreducible components
E\subset\mathrm{Exc}_{V}
of the exceptional divisor of Definition
5.1.6
and, if
p\mid D
denotes the prime at which
E
is supported,
\widehat{m}_{E}=m_{E}\log(p)
is the constant of §
5.6
.

Proof.

The first equality follows from Theorem
8.4.2
, using Proposition
6.7.1
and the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
.

For the second equality, combining
5.5.1
with the intersection formula

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}\cdot(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)=0

of Theorem
5.5.1
shows that

\displaystyle 2^{n}\cdot\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}+(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0))

\displaystyle=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})+\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0).

Combining this with Theorem
8.4.2
and the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
yields

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})=\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\frac{\log(D)}{2}\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})+\frac{\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\mathrm{Exc}_{V},0)}{2^{n}}.

Now use Corollary
5.6.2
.
∎

The most interesting aspect of the expected equality

\sum_{m\geq 0}\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)\cdot q^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

of Conjecture
8.4.1
is that the coefficients of the modular form on the right hand side can be made completely explicit, by combining the formulas of §
2.7
and Theorem
8.2.3
with the following corollary of Theorem
8.4.2
.

Corollary 8.5.2
.

Assuming
n>2
, we have the equality of modular forms

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\frac{3}{2}\log(D)-\log(r)\right)\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau).

Proof.

Start with the equality

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\log(D/r)\gamma_{r}E_{r}(\tau)

of Lemma
8.4.4
, and substitute the arithmetic volume formula of Theorem
8.4.2
into the first term on the right hand side.
∎

Assume now that
n>2
.
In light of Corollary
8.5.2
, Conjecture
8.4.1
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)

(8.5.1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}-\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\sum_{r\mid D}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\frac{3}{2}\log(D)-\log(r)\right)\gamma_{r}e_{r}(m),

for all positive
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Of course the right hand side can be made explicit using the formulas of §
2.7
.

For example, let us now suppose
\gcd(m,D)=1
, and abbreviate

\kappa(m)=2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V}(0)}+\log(D)+\sum_{\ell\mid D}\frac{2\log(\ell)}{1+\beta_{\ell}\cdot\left(\frac{m}{\ell}\right)^{n}},

where

\beta_{\ell}=(-1)^{n+1}\operatorname{inv}_{\ell}(V)\cdot\begin{cases}\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\ell^{\frac{n}{2}}&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\\[8.61108pt]
\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\ell^{\frac{n-1}{2}}&\mbox{if $n$ is odd.}\end{cases}.

We can use
Propositions
2.7.3
and
2.7.4
to rewrite (
8.5.1
) as

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}-\frac{\kappa(m)}{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\cdot B(m,0,s_{0}).

As in the proof of Proposition
8.2.2
, Remark
3.1.1
implies

(8.5.2)

\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}=-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})\cdot B(m,0,s_{0})

(the factor of
1/2
appears because of the relation

2\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{V})=\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})

of Theorem
5.5.1
), and we find that (
8.5.1
) is equivalent to

(8.5.3)

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\kappa(m)\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}.

The following corollary of Theorem
8.4.2
shows that this equality holds for many values of
m
.

Corollary 8.5.3
.

Assume
n>2
.
The equality (
8.5.3
) holds for all positive
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\varepsilon(m)=1
.

Proof.

The final claim of Theorem
8.4.2
shows that the
m^{\mathrm{th}}
Fourier coefficients of

\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\phi}^{\mathrm{eis}}_{V}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1})

agree for all
m
with
\varepsilon(m)=1
, and hence (by Corollary
8.5.2
) the equality (
8.5.1
) holds for all such
m
.
The discussion above shows that this is equivalent to (
8.5.3
).
∎

Again suppose that
n>2
and
\gcd(m,D)=1
, and recall the equality

\widehat{\deg}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}^{\mathrm{tot}}(m)\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}^{n-1}\big)=\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m))+\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}

of Theorem
7.3.1
.
As in the proof of Lemma
8.4.3
, the integral on the right hand side is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}_{V}(\mathbb{C})}\Phi_{V}(m)\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-1}

\displaystyle=B^{\prime}(m,0,s_{0})\cdot\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Kbun complex integral}}}{{=}}-2\frac{B^{\prime}(m,0,s_{0})}{B(m,0,s_{0})}\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}.

Solving for the height term, we find that the conjectural equality of (
8.5.3
) is equivalent to

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\left(\kappa(m)+2\frac{B^{\prime}(m,0,s_{0})}{B(m,0,s_{0})}\right)\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}.

By the explicit calculation of
B(m,0,s)
found in Corollaries
2.6.5
and
2.6.6
, this last conjectural equality is equivalent to

(8.5.4)

\mathrm{ht}_{\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V}}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{V}(m))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{V,m}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{V,m}(0)}+\log(D)\right)\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{V}(m)(\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathrm{c}_{1}}(\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{V})^{n-2}.

Here if
n
is even we have set

\mathbf{A}_{V,m}(s)=\mathbf{a}_{1}(s)\cdots\mathbf{a}_{n-1}(s)\cdot m^{s+n-1}\sigma_{-2s-n+1}(m),

while if
n
is odd we have set

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{V,m}(s)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{a}_{1}(s)\cdots\mathbf{a}_{n-1}(s)\cdot m^{s+n-1}\sigma_{-2s-n+1,\varepsilon}(m)

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{\ell\mid D}\left(1+\left(\frac{-1}{\ell}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\left(\frac{m}{\ell}\right)\operatorname{inv}_{\ell}(V)\ell^{-s-\frac{n-1}{2}}\right).

Corollary 8.5.4
.

Assume
n>2
. The equality (
8.5.4
)
holds for all positive
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\varepsilon(m)=1
.

Proof.

The discussion above shows that (
8.5.4
) is equivalent to (
8.5.3
), which is known when
\varepsilon(m)=1
by Corollary
8.5.3
.
∎

8.6.
Volume of the Hodge bundle

We now derive Theorem
A
of the introduction as a consequence of Theorem
8.4.2
. Let
W
be a
{\bm{k}}
-hermitian space of signature
(n-1,1)
with
n\geq 1
, containing a self-dual
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}
-lattice. Let
\mathcal{M}_{W}
be the moduli space of (
4.1.1
), and recall that in §
5.2
we associated a metrized Hodge bundle

\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Pic}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{W},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{pre}})

to the universal abelian scheme
A\to\mathcal{M}_{W}
.

Theorem 8.6.1
.

The metrized Hodge bundle has complex volume

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}})=\mathbf{A}_{W}(0)\times\begin{cases}2^{n-1}&\mbox{if $n$ is odd }\\
2^{n-o(D)}&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\end{cases}

and arithmetic volume

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}})=\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}_{W}^{\prime}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{W}(0)}-nC_{0}(n)+\log(D)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}}),

where
C_{0}(n)
is the constant defined in Theorem
A
.

Proof.

First assume
n\geq 2
.
Let
W_{0}={\bm{k}}
endowed with its hermitian form
h(x,y)=x\overline{y}
,
and define a hermitian form on
V=\operatorname{Hom}_{\bm{k}}(W_{0},W)
as in (
5.1.2
).
In particular
W\cong V
as hermitian spaces.

As in Remark
5.1.2
, there is a finite étale surjection

\mathcal{S}_{V}\to\mathcal{M}_{W}

of degree

t_{n}=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}\times\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\\
2^{1-o(D)}&\mbox{if $n$ is odd.}\end{cases}

Denote again by
A\to\mathcal{S}_{V}
the pullback of
A\to\mathcal{M}_{W}
via this morphism,
so that

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}})=\frac{1}{t_{n}}\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}})

and

\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{W}})=\frac{1}{t_{n}}\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{S}_{V}}).

Using these formulas, the theorem for
n>2
follows from Theorem
8.2.3
and Corollary
8.5.1
, along with the equality of Chern forms of Theorem
5.5.1
.

It remains to treat the case
n=1
, so that

\mathcal{M}_{W}\cong\mathcal{M}_{(0,1)}\cong\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}.

In this case

\mathbf{A}_{W}(s)=\mathbf{a}_{1}(s)
. Dirichlet’s class number formula implies

\mathbf{a}_{1}(0)=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|},

while (
8.1.3
) and (
5.3.1
) imply

2\frac{\mathbf{a}_{1}^{\prime}(0)}{\mathbf{a}_{1}(0)}-C_{0}(1)+\log(D)=-\frac{L^{\prime}(0,\varepsilon)}{L(0,\varepsilon)}-\frac{\log(D)}{2}=\log(2\pi)+2h_{\bm{k}}^{\mathrm{Falt}}.

The theory of complex multiplication implies

\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}})=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}(\mathbb{C})}1=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}(\mathbb{C})}\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Aut}(x)|}=\frac{|\mathrm{CL}({\bm{k}})|}{|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{k}}^{\times}|}=\mathbf{A}_{W}(0),

while the argument of Proposition
5.3.1
, which is essentially the Chowla-Selberg formula, implies the first equality in

\displaystyle\widehat{\deg}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\deg}(0,\log(2\pi)+2h_{\bm{k}}^{\mathrm{Falt}})

\displaystyle=(\log(2\pi)+2h_{\bm{k}}^{\mathrm{Falt}})\int_{\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}(\mathbb{C})}1

\displaystyle=\left(2\frac{\mathbf{A}_{W}^{\prime}(0)}{\mathbf{A}_{W}(0)}-C_{0}(1)+\log(D)\right)\operatorname{vol}_{\mathbb{C}}(\widehat{\omega}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_{A/\mathcal{M}_{(1,0)}}).

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Coefficients of modular forms

In this appendix we quote a theorem of Bellaïche, and derive some consequences that play an essential role in the proof of Theorem
8.4.2
. We thank Joel Bellaïche for his help.
Let
N
be a positive integer and let
\chi
be a Dirichlet character modulo
N
.
Fix a modular form

f(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}c(m)\cdot q^{m}\in M_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N),\chi)

of weight
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with nebentypus character
\chi
(we do not assume that
f
is a Hecke eigenform).

Theorem A.1

(Bellaïche)
.

Suppose the coefficients of
f(\tau)
satisfy

(i)

c(m)=0
for all integers
m
with
\gcd(m,N)>1
, and

(ii)

c(p)=0
for a set of primes
p
of density
1
.

Then
f(\tau)
vanishes identically.

Proof.

If the nebentypus character
\chi
is trivial, this is a direct consequence of
[
Bel19
, Theorem I]
, which actually gives an analogous result in positive characteristic. An inspection of the proof shows that the result equally holds for general nebentypus.

For the convenience of the reader, we also sketch a more direct proof that was provided to us by Bellaïche.
Let
L
be be the field generated by the coefficients of
f
.
Without loss of generality, possibly replacing
L
by an extension, we may view
L
as an extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
for any prime
p
. We may also assume that
L
contains all
N
-th roots of unity.
We first consider the case when
N>1
. Choose a prime
p
that divides
N
.

Let
M_{f}
be the subspace of
M_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N),\chi,L)
generated by
f
under the action of the Hecke operators
T_{\ell}
for
\ell
prime not dividing
N
.
Let
A_{f}
be the
L
-subalgebra of

\operatorname{End}_{L}(M_{f})
generated by the
T_{\ell}
for
\ell
prime not dividing
N
.

Notice that
A_{f}
contains all the
T_{n}
for
n
prime to
N
. This is because any such
T_{n}
is a polynomial in the
T_{\ell}
(
\ell
prime not dividing
N
) and the diamond
operators, which are just scalar in
L
, and hence belong to
A_{f}
. (Here we use the hypothesis that
f
has a nebentypus).

Let
G=G_{\mathbb{Q},N}
be the Galois group of the maximal algebraic extension of
\mathbb{Q}
unramified
outside
N
. There exists a continuous
pseudo-representation
(\tau,\delta):G\to A_{f}
of dimension
2

which sends
\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}
(for
\ell
prime not dividing
N
)
to
T_{\ell}
, see e.g.
[
Bel19
, Section 10]
or
[
BS15
, Section 3.10]
. Here

\delta
is
w^{k-1}\chi
, where
w
denotes the
p
-adic cyclotomic character, and hence takes values in
L^{\times}
.

We claim that the linear subspace generated by
\tau(G)
in
A_{f}
has to be
A_{f}
. This follows from the formula
\tau(gh)+\delta(h)\tau(gh^{-1})=\tau(g)\tau(h)
, a
formula true for all pseudo-representations of dimension
2
, since it is true for
\tau=
the trace, and
\delta=
the determinant in the algebra
\operatorname{Mat}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. Thus the linear subspace
generated by
\tau(G)
is a subalgebra containing all the
T_{\ell}
(
\ell
not dividing
N
), hence is
A_{f}
.

Let
V
be the kernel of the linear map
A_{f}\to L
given by
T\mapsto c_{Tf}(1)
.
By the hypothesis (ii) we know that
c_{f}(\ell)=c_{T_{\ell}f}(1)=0
for a dense set of primes
\ell
. Hence
\tau(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell})
is in
V
for a dense set of
\ell
.
Since
V
is closed, this means by the Chebotarev density theorem that
\tau(G)
is contained in
V
. By the above claim, we find that
V=A_{f}
. This implies that
T_{n}\in V
for
all
n
prime to
N
, and hence that
c_{f}(n)=0
for all
n
prime to
N
.
On the other hand, by hypothesis (i) we know that
c_{f}(n)=0
for all
n
not prime to
N
. Hence
we have
f=0
, concluding the proof in the case
N>1
.

If
N=1
, we chose an auxiliary prime
q
and replace
f
by

f^{\prime}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\geq 0\\
(n,q)=1\end{subarray}}c(n)q^{n},

which belongs to
M_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(q^{2}),L)
by
[
Miy06
, Lemma 4.3.10]
.
Applying the above argument to
f^{\prime}
we find
c(n)=0
for all
n
coprime to
q
. But since
f
has level
1
, this means that
f=0
.
∎

Corollary A.2
.

Keep the form

f(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}c(m)\cdot q^{m}\in M_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N),\chi)

as above.
Let
\psi
be a quadratic Dirichlet character modulo
N
.
Fix
\delta\in\{\pm 1\}
, and suppose that
c(p)=0
for all but finitely many primes
p
with
\psi(p)=\delta
.
Then
c(m)=0
for all integers
m
with
\psi(m)=\delta
.

Proof.

Recall from
[
Miy06
, Lemma 4.3.10]
that if
\psi
is any quadratic Dirichlet character modulo
N
, then the twist

f_{\psi}(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}\psi(m)c(m)\cdot q^{m}

of
f(\tau)
defines an element of
M_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N^{2}),\chi)
.
As the Fourier coefficients of

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\geq 0\\
\psi(m)=\delta\end{subarray}}c(m)\cdot q^{m}=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\big(f_{\psi^{2}}(\tau)+\delta f_{\psi}(\tau)\big)\in M_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N^{2}),\chi)

satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem
A.1
, this modular form vanishes identically.
The claim follows.
∎

We now apply these results to the space of modular forms
M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})
relevant to the present paper. In other words,
\varepsilon
and
D
are as in the introduction.
Let us write

M\subset M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

for the
\mathbb{Q}
-subspace of forms with rational Fourier coefficients.
The
\mathbb{Q}
-linear dual
M^{\vee}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Q}}(M,\mathbb{Q})
is generated by the coefficient extraction functionals

a_{m}:M\to\mathbb{Q},

sending a modular form
f(\tau)
to its
m^{\mathrm{th}}
Fourier coefficient.

Corollary A.3
.

For
\delta\in\{\pm 1\}
and any positive
t\in\mathbb{Z}
, the subspaces

\displaystyle A_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{a_{m}:\text{$m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}$ with $\varepsilon(m)=\delta$}\}

\displaystyle P_{\delta,t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{a_{p}:\text{$p$ prime with $\varepsilon(p)=\delta$ and $p>t$}\}

of
M^{\vee}
are equal.

Proof.

For any subspace
B\subset M^{\vee}
we write
B^{\perp}\subset M
for its orthogonal complement with respect to the natural non-degenerate pairing
M^{\vee}\times M\to\mathbb{Q}
.
It suffices to show that the inclusion

A_{\delta}^{\perp}\subset P_{\delta,t}^{\perp}

is an equality, so fix an

f(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}c(m)\cdot q^{m}\in P_{\delta,t}^{\perp}\subset M.

By definition of
P_{\delta,t}^{\perp}
, we have
c(p)=0
for all primes
p>t
with
\varepsilon(p)=\delta
,
and so Corollary
A.2
implies that
c(m)=0
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
\varepsilon(m)=\delta
. In other words,
f(\tau)\in A_{\delta}^{\perp}
.
∎

Corollary A.4
.

Fix
\delta\in\{\pm 1\}
. If

f(\tau)=\sum_{m\geq 0}c(m)\cdot q^{m}\in M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})

is a modular form with the property that

c(p)\in\mathbb{Q}\log(p)

for all primes
p
with
\varepsilon(p)=\delta
, then
c(p)=0
for all such primes.

Proof.

Fix a prime
p
with
\varepsilon(p)=\delta
, and consider the coefficient extraction functional

a_{p}:M\to\mathbb{Q}.

Taking
t=p
in Corollary
A.3
, we find that there are rational numbers
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r}
and primes
p<p_{1}<\ldots<p_{r}
with all
\varepsilon(p_{i})=\delta
, such that

a_{p}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\cdot a_{p_{i}}

holds as an equality of
\mathbb{Q}
-linear functionals on
M
.
We now view this as an equality of
\mathbb{C}
-linear functionals on

M_{n}(\Gamma_{0}(D),\varepsilon^{n})=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C},

and apply this linear functional to
f(\tau)
. The result is

c(p)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\cdot c(p_{i})\in\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{Q}\log(p_{i}),

and the claim follows from the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear independence of the set

\{\log(p),\log(p_{1}),\ldots,\log(p_{r})\}.\qed

Remark A.5
.

Of course Corollary
A.2
allows us to strengthen the conclusion of Corollary
A.4

to
c(m)=0
whenever
\varepsilon(m)=\delta
.

Index
